# Existentialism — Complete Learning Session

> **Syllabus, verbatim:** *Existentialism (Kierkegaard, Sarte, Heidegger): Existence and Essence; Choice, Responsibility and Authentic Existence; Being-in-the-world and Temporality* — UPSC Philosophy Paper I, Section A, item 10. Three printed thinkers and three printed limbs. 📝 The paper prints **"Sarte"**; the philosopher is **Jean-Paul Sartre**, and the misprint is preserved only inside a verbatim quotation of the syllabus.
> **Evidence discipline:** ✅ marks doctrine on which the standard sources converge, ⚠️ marks analytical reconstruction for exam use, and ❓ marks a live scholarly dispute or a contested attribution. *Being and Time* is cited by **§ number**, which is stable across the Macquarrie and Robinson (1962) and Stambaugh (1996, revised Schmidt 2010) translations even though their wording differs. Kierkegaard's pseudonymous books are cited **by pseudonym**, following the request he printed in the "First and Last Declaration" appended to the *Concluding Unscientific Postscript* (1846). Model answers below are independent learner practice and are **never** official UPSC keys: UPSC publishes no descriptive model answer for Philosophy Paper I.
> **Terminology rule:** the English concept leads and the Danish, German or French term follows immediately in parentheses — *the single individual (den Enkelte)*, *anxiety or dread (Angest / Angst)*, *the leap (Springet)*, *the moment (Øjeblikket)*, *indirect communication (indirekte Meddelelse)*, *being-there (Dasein)*, *being-in-the-world (In-der-Welt-sein)*, *ready-to-hand (Zuhandenheit)*, *present-at-hand (Vorhandenheit)*, *thrownness (Geworfenheit)*, *care (Sorge)*, *the "they" (das Man)*, *idle talk (Gerede)*, *falling (Verfallen)*, *authenticity (Eigentlichkeit)*, *being-toward-death (Sein-zum-Tode)*, *anticipatory resoluteness (vorlaufende Entschlossenheit)*, *temporality (Zeitlichkeit)*, *historicality (Geschichtlichkeit)*, *being-in-itself (en-soi)*, *being-for-itself (pour-soi)*, *being-for-others (l'être-pour-autrui)*, *nihilation (néantisation)*, *nothingness (néant)*, *bad faith (mauvaise foi)*, *facticity (facticité)*, *the Look (le regard)*, *anguish (angoisse)*, *abandonment (délaissement)*, *despair (désespoir)*. A source word may sharpen an English explanation; it may never replace one.

## BASIC LEARNING SESSION

### CONCEPT SPINE — ENGLISH FIRST, SOURCE TERM IMMEDIATELY AFTER

*Concept spine: read it downward. One move, one warning, three printed limbs, three non-identical projects, one shared mood-structure, three answers to each of the two questions the clause actually asks — and a fence around what belongs to other clauses.*

> 🔑 **Mnemonic — the three thinkers, "F-F-F".** Kierkegaard = **F**aith; Sartre = **F**reedom; Heidegger = **F**initude. Three thinkers, three destinations, one shared refusal to define a person by a general essence.
> 🔑 **Mnemonic — the three printed limbs, "E-C-B".** **E**xistence and essence → **C**hoice, responsibility and authentic existence → **B**eing-in-the-world and temporality. Limb 1 removes the blueprint, limb 2 asks what a person must do once the blueprint is gone, and limb 3 is Heidegger's refusal to let limbs 1 and 2 be answered by a subject standing over against a world.
> 🔑 **Mnemonic — care, "A-A-A".** **A**head-of-itself, **A**lready-in, **A**longside. Three limbs of *Sorge* (*Being and Time* §41), which map exactly onto the three ecstases of temporality — future, having-been, present (§65). Learn the two lists together and the hardest twenty-marker in the clause becomes one diagram.
> 🔑 **Mnemonic — bad faith, "F-or-T".** Bad faith collapses a person into **F**acticity alone or into **T**ranscendence alone. Neither dimension is denied by Sartre; what is denied is the *tension*. A script that defines bad faith merely as "lying to yourself" has not stated the doctrine at all.
> 🔑 **Mnemonic — what anxiety is not, "no object".** Fear has an object; anxiety has none, which is exactly why it discloses a structure rather than a thing. This one line, written early, separates a prepared script from a paraphrase in every question on dread, anguish or *Angst*.
> 🔑 **Mnemonic — the slogan's owner, "S-only".** "Existence precedes essence" is **S**artre's, from the **1945** lecture published in **1946**. Heidegger repudiated it in the "Letter on Humanism" (written 1946, published 1947); Kierkegaard died in 1855 and never wrote it. Attributing the slogan to all three is the single commonest way to lose marks on this clause.

### ANSWER-WORTHINESS — WHAT EARNS MARKS AND WHAT DOES NOT

| Classification | Content |
|---|---|
| **CORE MAINS** | The **family-label warning** and the exact ownership of the slogan; **existence precedes essence** with the paper-knife (*coupe-papier*) analogy, atheism as a **premise**, and the 1945/1946 provenance; **Heidegger's repudiation** of the slogan and the "essence of Dasein lies in its existence" (*Being and Time* §9); **Kierkegaard's existing individual** against Hegel's system, **"truth is subjectivity"** attributed to **Johannes Climacus**, and its distinction from relativism; the **three spheres of existence** with their limits and the **leap** (*Springet*); the **teleological suspension of the ethical** as staged by **Johannes de Silentio**; **anxiety** (*Angest*) as "the dizziness of freedom" from **Vigilius Haufniensis**, and **despair** as a misrelation from **Anti-Climacus**; Sartre's **being-in-itself and being-for-itself** with the two formulas, **nihilation** (*néantisation*) and the impossible *pour-soi-en-soi*; **radical but situated freedom**, "condemned to be free", **non-choice as choice**, and **anguish, abandonment and despair**; **facticity and transcendence** and **bad faith** with the waiter and the woman on the date, plus the **self-deception paradox** and its pre-reflective solution; **freedom and determinism**, motive-constitution and the qualified "no excuses"; **being-for-others** and the **Look** (*le regard*) with the keyhole, shame, conflict, and the corrected reading of "hell is other people"; Heidegger's **Being-question** and **ontological difference**; **being-in-the-world** as one unitary structure and his rejection of the transcendental ego; **worldhood**, **ready-to-hand** and **present-at-hand**, the referential totality and breakdown; the three **existentialia** and **disclosedness**; **care** (*Sorge*) and its three limbs; **the "they"** (*das Man*) with idle talk, curiosity and ambiguity, and **falling**; the **call of conscience** and **being-toward-death** with its four characters, ending in **anticipatory resoluteness**; **authenticity as a non-moral existential modification**; **temporality** with the three ecstases, the priority of the future, and **primordial against ordinary time**; the **fear/anxiety distinction** across all three thinkers; and all **fourteen routed 2018–2025 question-parts** |
| **CORE SUPPORTING** | **Indirect communication** (*indirekte Meddelelse*) and **pseudonymity** — promoted out of optional status on 15 August 2026 and now marks-essential for any question on subjectivity or the critique of Hegel — including the pseudonym table, the **double authorship** and the "First and Last Declaration"; the **knight of faith** against the knight of infinite resignation; **historicality** (*Geschichtlichkeit*); the dialectic of **concrete relations** with the Other — love, language and masochism against indifference, desire, sadism and hate; the **four accounts of the Other** across Husserl, Heidegger, Sartre and Levinas; the **provenance rail**, because three languages, three translation traditions and one unfinished book make dating and attribution directly examinable |
| **SUPPORTING** | Sartre's *Critique of Dialectical Reason* (**1960**) for scarcity, seriality, the practico-inert and the group-in-fusion, used to show that the strongest objection to early Sartre landed; **de Beauvoir** and **Fanon** for one constraint objection; **Levinas** for one ethical counter to both Sartre and Heidegger; **Arendt** on natality against mortality; **Adorno's** "jargon of authenticity" charge; Heidegger's **turn** (*Kehre*) and the unwritten Division III; **Merleau-Ponty** on structural constraint; the **Nothing** (*das Nichts*) of "What is Metaphysics?" (**1929**) only as the point where this clause touches Topic 07 |
| **OPTIONAL ADVANCED** | How far a pseudonym's thesis may be treated as Kierkegaard's own, and whether the method leaves him any assertible doctrine at all; whether Sartrean freedom is fundamentally solitary or always exposed through the Other, and whether the conflictual social ontology admits a reciprocity reading; whether Heideggerian authenticity is heroic decisionism, a formally thin owning of finitude, or a doctrine permanently shadowed by 1933 |
| **NOT OWNED HERE** | Husserl's suspension (*epoché*), reduction, theory of essences and avoidance of psychologism **as doctrine** (Topic 09 — they enter here only as the method Heidegger redirects and the position Sartre inherits); Hegel's dialectic, Absolute Spirit and system **as doctrine** (Topic 05 — Hegel is the target Kierkegaard needs, not a topic to expound); Descartes' methodic doubt, the *cogito* and dualism **as doctrine** (Topic 02 — Descartes is what being-in-the-world is designed to dissolve); Kant's antinomies and his own account of freedom (Topic 04 — Sartre's universalisability is quasi-Kantian and is stated in one clause); **Nietzsche** entirely, since he is not a printed thinker here; Camus's absurd, Jaspers's boundary situations, Marcel's mystery and de Beauvoir's ethics of ambiguity as **systems** rather than as bounded comparisons; existentialist theology, faith and revelation, and death and immortality (Paper II) |

⚠️ **Ownership boundary, stated once.** This clause owns **14 of 112** Western Philosophy question-parts in the 2018–2025 corpus — **joint-highest** with Rationalism and with Moore, Russell and Early Wittgenstein — and it owns **at least one part in every one of the eight years**. Three consequences follow, and all three are visible in the first paragraph of a script. First, **no thinker is optional**: Sartre carries eight parts, Kierkegaard four and Heidegger three, and 2024 Q4(a) requires all three in one answer, so a candidate who prepares "the existentialists" as a single doctrine has prepared the one thing the paper repeatedly punishes. Second, **the paper tests attribution as often as it tests doctrine** — 2024 Q4(a) asks what "the existentialist thinkers" mean by a slogan that belongs to exactly one of them, and 2018 Q4(a) opens with a term (*epoché*) that belongs to Husserl before turning to Heidegger's rejection of it. Third, **statement-based framing dominates**: 2020 Q4(a), 2021 Q4(b) and 2023 Q3(c) each hand you a sentence and require you to locate it inside its own author's system before unpacking it, which means the technical pair each sentence turns on must be reproducible on demand.

> **Complete the Basic block first.** Interpretive depth — how far a pseudonym speaks for Kierkegaard, whether Sartrean freedom is solitary, and whether Heideggerian authenticity is decisionism — is deliberately withheld until the clearly labelled optional block after the practice sections. Nothing in that block is needed to write a complete answer to any of the fourteen routed questions.

### ORIGIN AND CHRONOLOGY — THE MINIMUM NEEDED TO AVOID ANACHRONISM

⚠️ This clause has an unusual hazard: **three languages, three translation traditions, one unfinished masterwork, one popular lecture its author later regretted, and an author who asked in print not to be quoted under his own name**. The rail below prevents the seven commonest anachronisms — treating "existentialism" as a movement Kierkegaard joined; sourcing "condemned to be free" to *Being and Nothingness*; calling *Being and Time* a completed system; quoting "leap of faith" as Kierkegaard's phrase; writing "Kierkegaard says in *Fear and Trembling*" when the book is signed by a pseudonym; dating the "Letter on Humanism" to the 1930s; and treating the *Critique of Dialectical Reason* as if it were the doctrine of 1943.

```text
   1813  Soren Kierkegaard born, Copenhagen
   1841  Kierkegaard's dissertation, On the Concept of Irony
   1843  EITHER/OR (ed. Victor Eremita) - aesthetic and ethical papers
         FEAR AND TREMBLING (Johannes de Silentio) - Abraham, the
           teleological suspension of the ethical, the knight of faith
         REPETITION (Constantin Constantius)
   1844  THE CONCEPT OF ANXIETY (Vigilius Haufniensis) - 'the dizziness
           of freedom'.  Lowrie translates the title The Concept of
           DREAD; Hong translates ANXIETY.  Danish: Angest
         PHILOSOPHICAL FRAGMENTS (Johannes Climacus)
   1846  CONCLUDING UNSCIENTIFIC POSTSCRIPT (Johannes Climacus) -
           'truth is subjectivity'; the FIRST AND LAST DECLARATION,
           signed by Kierkegaard, asks that the PSEUDONYM be cited
   1848  THE POINT OF VIEW FOR MY WORK AS AN AUTHOR - WRITTEN 1848,
           PUBLISHED POSTHUMOUSLY 1859.  Retrospective self-reading:
           do NOT cite it as contemporaneous evidence of intention
   1849  THE SICKNESS UNTO DEATH (Anti-Climacus) - despair as a
           MISRELATION in the self's relation to itself
   1850  PRACTICE IN CHRISTIANITY (Anti-Climacus)
   1855  Kierkegaard dies.  He never used the word 'existentialism'
   ....
   1889  Martin Heidegger born
   1905  Jean-Paul Sartre born
   1927  BEING AND TIME (Sein und Zeit) - PUBLISHED UNFINISHED.
           Only Division One and Division Two of PART ONE appeared.
           Division III (Time and Being) and the whole of Part Two
           were never published.  NEVER write that Heidegger
           'completes' his analysis of Being in this book
   1929  WHAT IS METAPHYSICS? - anxiety reveals the Nothing (das
           Nichts).  'Nothing noths' / 'the nothing nothings'.
           NEVER 'nothing noughts'.  This is the sentence Carnap
           attacks in 1932 - see Topic 07
   1943  BEING AND NOTHINGNESS (L'Etre et le Neant) - the ONTOLOGY:
           in-itself, for-itself, nihilation, bad faith, the Look.
           Eng. tr. Hazel Barnes 1956; Sarah Richmond 2018
   1944  HUIS CLOS / NO EXIT - the play. 'L'enfer, c'est les autres'
   1945  EXISTENTIALISM IS A HUMANISM - DELIVERED as a public lecture
           in October 1945; PUBLISHED 1946.  The SLOGANS come from
           HERE, not from Being and Nothingness.  Sartre later
           regretted publishing it
   1946  LETTER ON HUMANISM written; PUBLISHED 1947.  Heidegger
           repudiates Sartre's slogan and denies that his own thought
           is existentialism
   1960  CRITIQUE OF DIALECTICAL REASON - later Sartre: scarcity,
           seriality, the practico-inert, the group-in-fusion.  Cite
           it for the CONCESSION, never as the doctrine of 1943
   1962  Macquarrie and Robinson translate Being and Time
   1976  Heidegger dies         1980  Sartre dies
   1996  Stambaugh translation of Being and Time (rev. Schmidt 2010)
```

*Chronology rail: the dates are not decoration. Three of them — 1927 unfinished, 1945/1946 for the slogans, and 1946/1947 for the repudiation — are the difference between a script that has read the topic and a script that has read about it.*

⚠️ **Four attribution rules that are worth marks on their own.**

1. **Cite the pseudonym.** Write "Johannes Climacus argues in the *Postscript* that truth is subjectivity", not "Kierkegaard says". Kierkegaard printed the request himself, calling himself only the "author's author" and a prompter (*souffleur*). ✅ It is textually correct and instantly visible as expertise.
2. **Cite *Being and Time* by section.** §9 (the essence of Dasein), §26 (being-with), §§27 and 35–38 (the "they", idle talk, falling), §40 (anxiety), §41 (care), §§46–53 (being-toward-death), §§54–60 (conscience), §65 (temporality). Section numbers survive both translations; page numbers do not.
3. **Name the translation, or use the source word once.** *Angest* is "dread" in Lowrie and "anxiety" in Hong; *Zuhandenheit* is "readiness-to-hand" in Macquarrie and Robinson and "handiness" in Stambaugh; Barnes and Richmond differ throughout *Being and Nothingness*.
4. **Say where a slogan comes from.** "Condemned to be free", "existence precedes essence" and "man is nothing else but what he makes of himself" are all from the 1945 lecture published in 1946. Saying so in half a clause is a precision mark that costs nothing.

### SESSION 1 — WHAT THE PRINTED CLAUSE ASKS, AND WHY "EXISTENTIALISM" IS A FAMILY AND NOT A SCHOOL

**DEFINITION.** *Plain language:* existentialism is the name later given to a set of philosophies that refuse to explain a human life by first defining what a human being is in general, and start instead from the finite, situated individual who has to live one. *Technical:* **existentialism** is a retrospective family-label for projects that treat **concrete, finite, situated existence** as prior to any general essence, and that take **individuality, choice, anxiety, responsibility, finitude and authenticity** as the categories through which a person's being must be analysed — while disagreeing fundamentally about God, freedom, method and the ground of authentic existence.

**ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING.** ⚠️ *"'Existentialism' names a shared problem, not a shared doctrine: Kierkegaard, Heidegger and Sartre all deny that a person can be understood as a specimen of a general definition, but they disagree about almost everything that follows — and an answer that treats them as one school has already conceded the examiner's first trap."*

**MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS.** family resemblance · the single individual (*den Enkelte*) · being-for-itself (*pour-soi*) · being-there (*Dasein*) · concrete existence · finitude · situated choice · fundamental ontology · atheistic humanism · retrospective label.

**Plain-language visual — the idea before the vocabulary.**

```text
   THE OLD QUESTION                       THE EXISTENTIALIST QUESTION
   What IS a human being?                 What is it to BE this human
   Answer: a rational animal;             being, HERE, once, mortally,
   a creature made to a divine            with choices I cannot hand
   blueprint; a machine of                over to anyone else?
   causes                                          |
        |                                          v
        v                                 The general definition may be
   The individual is one INSTANCE         TRUE and still miss the thing
   of the definition, and the             that has to be lived.  No
   definition does the work               definition chooses for me.
```

*What this shows: the shift is not from one answer to another answer. It is from a question about a kind to a question about an existing individual — which is why "essence" is the word under attack.*

**🖼️ VISUAL — one problem, three non-identical projects, and the fences around them.**

```text
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   |   THE SHARED PROBLEM-FIELD                                        |
   |   An abstract system, a fixed nature or an impersonal method      |
   |   loses exactly what it was supposed to explain: the finite,      |
   |   temporal, choosing individual who has to exist.                 |
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
              |                    |                     |
              v                    v                     v

              |                    |                     |
              +--------------------+---------------------+
                                   |
                                   v
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   |   WHAT THEY DO NOT SHARE - SAY THIS EARLY                         |
   |   GOD          central | bracketed by the ontology | DENIED as a  |
   |                                                      PREMISE      |
   |   THE SLOGAN   never wrote it | REPUDIATED it 1946/47 | COINED it |
   |   LABEL        died 1855, never heard the word | DENIED being an  |
   |                existentialist | ACCEPTED and DEFINED the label    |
   |   AUTHENTICITY faith by a LEAP | owning thrown projection, NOT a  |
   |                virtue | no positive doctrine, only the critique   |
   |                of BAD FAITH                                       |
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
                                   |
                                   v
        THE THREE PRINTED LIMBS, AND WHO CARRIES EACH ONE
   1 EXISTENCE AND ESSENCE ........... Sartre owns the slogan;
       Heidegger supplies the repudiation; Kierkegaard supplies the
       older and deeper form of the same refusal
   2 CHOICE, RESPONSIBILITY AND AUTHENTIC EXISTENCE ... all three, in
       three incompatible registers - faith, freedom, finitude
   3 BEING-IN-THE-WORLD AND TEMPORALITY ... HEIDEGGER, almost alone;
       this limb is where the clause stops being about 'attitudes to
       life' and becomes fundamental ontology
```

*Caption: the printed clause is not a list of three topics with three owners. Limb 1 removes the blueprint, limb 2 asks what a person must then do, and limb 3 is Heidegger's insistence that limbs 1 and 2 cannot be answered by a subject facing a world — which is why the third limb has a different vocabulary from the first two.*

**NUMBERED TEACHING.**

1. **The label is retrospective, and the paper knows it.** ✅ Kierkegaard died in **1855**; the term "existentialism" belongs to the French debates of the 1940s. Heidegger, in the **"Letter on Humanism"** (written 1946, published **1947**), explicitly denies that his thought is existentialism. Only **Sartre** accepted the label and, in the lecture of October **1945** published in **1946**, gave it a definition. ⚠️ An answer that says "the existentialists held that…" has therefore made a claim that two of the three printed thinkers would reject.
2. **What genuinely unites them is a refusal, not a thesis.** ⚠️ Each denies that the being of a human individual is settled in advance by a general nature — Kierkegaard against Hegel's mediating system, Heidegger against the Cartesian subject who is first a thinking thing and only then in a world, Sartre against the divine artisan and the determinist. The unity is a **family resemblance**: overlapping problems, not a common creed.
3. **The three names for the human being are three different concepts, not three translations.** ✅ Kierkegaard's **single individual** (*den Enkelte*) is a religious and ethical category — the one who cannot hide in the crowd and must stand before God. Sartre's **being-for-itself** (*pour-soi*) is an ontological category — consciousness as a nihilating, self-surpassing structure. Heidegger's **Dasein** is neither: it is "the entity for which its own Being is at issue and which has an understanding of Being", chosen as the route into the Being-question and not as a name for the human subject. ⚠️ Translating all three as "the human person" flattens the entire clause.
4. **Why the disagreement about God is structural, not biographical.** ✅ For Sartre, atheism is a **premise**: if God existed, a divine artisan would have conceived human nature before creating individuals, and essence *would* precede existence. For Kierkegaard, the God-relation is what makes the single individual possible at all — despair, in Anti-Climacus's analysis, is precisely a misrelation of the self to "the power that established it". For Heidegger, the question is neither raised nor settled by the fundamental ontology of *Being and Time*. ⚠️ So "existentialism is atheistic" is false as stated; **Sartre's** existentialism is.
5. **What the clause is *not*.** ✅ It is not **pessimism** — anxiety, despair and death are analysed as *disclosive structures*, not as the claim that life is miserable. It is not **nihilism** — Kierkegaard grounds value in faith, Sartre in the responsibility that free commitment generates, Heidegger in a formal ownership of finitude. It is not **subjective whim** — Kierkegaardian appropriation, Sartrean choice and Heideggerian resoluteness are all situated and responsibility-laden. It is not **self-help** — the clause is ontology, ethics and religious thought.
6. **The exam-critical asymmetry in the printed limbs.** ⚠️ Limbs 1 and 2 are shared ground on which the three can be compared; limb 3 — **being-in-the-world and temporality** — is almost entirely **Heidegger's**, with Sartre entering only as a contrast. This asymmetry is why 2019 Q3(a) and 2022 Q2(a) are Heidegger questions and 2024 Q4(a) is a comparison question: the paper follows the structure of the clause.
7. **Boundary discipline — where Husserl and Hegel enter, and where they stop.** ⚠️ **Husserl** enters as the method Heidegger inherits and redirects, and as the phenomenology Sartre takes over and rewrites; his suspension, theory of essences and anti-psychologism are **Topic 09** doctrine and must not be expounded here. **Hegel** enters as the system Kierkegaard attacks and as the source of the negation and recognition themes Sartre reworks; the dialectic and Absolute Spirit are **Topic 05**. ✅ In 2018 Q4(a) the paper deliberately walks across that boundary — it asks "What is 'Epoché'?" before turning to Heidegger — and the correct move is to attribute the *epoché* to Husserl in one accurate sentence and then spend the answer on Heidegger.
8. **Nietzsche is not a printed thinker in this clause.** ✅ The syllabus prints Kierkegaard, "Sarte" and Heidegger. Nihilism, the will to power, eternal recurrence and the overhuman are **not** owner content here. ⚠️ Camus, Jaspers, Marcel and de Beauvoir may appear only as a bounded comparison or a single criticism, never as a section of the answer.

**HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification.**

> **Claim.** "Existentialism" is best treated as a family of projects united by a refusal rather than by a doctrine, and the refusal is that no general essence settles in advance what it is for this individual to exist. **Named evidence.** Kierkegaard's pseudonym Johannes Climacus argues in the *Concluding Unscientific Postscript* (1846) that a logical system is possible but an existential system is not; Heidegger writes in *Being and Time* (1927) §9 that the essence of Dasein lies in its existence, and in the "Letter on Humanism" (written 1946, published 1947) explicitly refuses both Sartre's slogan and the label; Sartre, in the lecture of October 1945 published in 1946, coins "existence precedes essence" and accepts the label as his own. **Analysis.** The three therefore converge on a negative claim and diverge on every positive one: the individual is grounded before God for Kierkegaard, disclosed as the site of Being's intelligibility for Heidegger, and self-made through situated freedom for Sartre — which is why their vocabularies (*den Enkelte*, *Dasein*, *pour-soi*) are not intertranslatable. **Qualification.** ⚠️ The family label remains useful only if it names a recurring problem-field; used as a creed, it silently converts Heidegger's fundamental ontology into humanism and Kierkegaard's Christianity into atheism, which is precisely the error the 2024 comparison question is built to expose.

**EXAM LINK.** This session is the *frame* for **2024 Q4(a)**, which asks what "the existentialist thinkers" mean by a slogan owned by one of them; it supplies the opening fence for **2018 Q4(a)**, where the *epoché* must be attributed to Husserl before Heidegger is discussed; and it supplies the closing qualification for every comparison answer in the clause.

**UPSC TRAPS.**

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| Opening "The existentialists believed that…" | ⚠️ Two of the three printed thinkers reject the label. Open with the shared **problem** and name the three projects separately. |
| Treating the syllabus spelling "Sarte" as a fourth thinker or as an error to be silently corrected | 📝 It is a printed misspelling of **Sartre**. Preserve it only inside a verbatim syllabus quotation; spell the philosopher correctly everywhere else. |
| Making atheism definitional of existentialism | ✅ Atheism is a **premise of Sartre's** version. Kierkegaard is a Christian thinker; Heidegger's ontology does not settle the question. |
| Calling existentialism pessimism or nihilism | ✅ Anxiety, despair and death are **disclosive structures**. The analysis is diagnostic, not an emotional verdict on life. |
| Importing Nietzsche as a printed thinker | ✅ He is not named in the clause. Nihilism, will to power and recurrence are outside this owner. |
| Expounding Husserl's method or Hegel's dialectic at length | ⚠️ They are Topics 09 and 05. Here they are the position being redirected and the system being attacked — one accurate sentence each. |

**REVISION NOTES.**

- **Retrospective family label**, not a school; Kierkegaard d. **1855**, Heidegger **denied** the label (1946/47), Sartre **accepted and defined** it (1945/46).
- Shared **refusal**: no general essence settles what it is for this individual to exist.
- Three non-identical concepts: **den Enkelte** (religious-ethical) · **pour-soi** (ontological structure of consciousness) · **Dasein** (route into the Being-question).
- God: **central** (K) · **denied as premise** (S) · **not settled by the ontology** (H).
- Not pessimism, not nihilism, not whim, not self-help.
- Limb 3 (**being-in-the-world and temporality**) is almost entirely **Heidegger's**.
- Fences: Husserl = Topic 09; Hegel = Topic 05; Descartes = Topic 02; Nietzsche = not printed.

**CLOSING RECALL FLOW.**

```text
   ONE PROBLEM (abstraction loses the existing individual) -> THREE
   PROJECTS (faith / Being / freedom) -> THREE NAMES (den Enkelte /
   Dasein / pour-soi) -> THREE POSITIONS ON GOD (central / bracketed /
   denied as premise) -> ONE SLOGAN OWNED BY ONE MAN (Sartre 1945-46,
   repudiated by Heidegger 1946-47) -> FAMILY RESEMBLANCE, NOT A CREED
```

### SESSION 2 — KIERKEGAARD: THE EXISTING INDIVIDUAL AGAINST THE SYSTEM, AND WHY "TRUTH IS SUBJECTIVITY" IS NOT RELATIVISM

**DEFINITION.** *Plain language:* Kierkegaard's complaint against Hegel is not that the system is wrong in detail but that it explains everything except the one person who has to stand somewhere and decide — and "truth is subjectivity" says that for the truths that matter existentially, *how* you hold a belief is what makes it true *for you as an existing person*, not what the belief reports. *Technical:* the **single individual** (*den Enkelte*) is the finite, temporal, passionate, deciding existent who cannot be exhaustively represented as a moment within a completed rational totality; **"truth is subjectivity"** — argued by the pseudonym **Johannes Climacus** in the *Concluding Unscientific Postscript* (1846) — asserts that in ethical and religious matters the **mode of appropriation** (infinite passionate inwardness, personal commitment, risk) is the locus of truth for the existing subject, without denying objective truth in logic or the sciences.

**ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING.** ⚠️ *"Climacus's thesis that truth is subjectivity is not a licence to believe what one likes; it is the claim that a truth about how to exist cannot be possessed in the way a result can be possessed — and Hegel's system fails precisely because it hands over results to a reader it cannot account for."*

**MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS.** the single individual (*den Enkelte*) · Johannes Climacus · appropriation · infinite passionate inwardness · the *how* against the *what* · "subjectivity is untruth" · "the crowd is untruth" · existential system · mediation · either/or against both/and.

**Plain-language visual — the idea before the vocabulary.**

```text
   TWO WAYS OF 'KNOWING' THE SAME SENTENCE: 'GOD EXISTS'

   OBJECTIVE ROUTE                        SUBJECTIVE ROUTE
   Treat it as a proposition.             Ask what it is to EXIST
   Weigh the arguments.  Reach            before God: in fear, in
   a probability.  File the               trembling, staking a life
   result.                                on it without proof.
        |                                          |
        v                                          v
   I now HAVE an opinion, and             I am now a DIFFERENT
   nothing about my existence             EXISTING PERSON, and the
   has changed.                           'result' cannot be handed
                                          to anyone else.
```

*What this shows: the two routes do not disagree about the sentence. They disagree about what it would be to be in the truth about it.*

**🖼️ VISUAL — the anti-Hegelian argument, and the two things "truth is subjectivity" does not say.**

```text
   |   HEGEL'S SYSTEM, AS KIERKEGAARD RECEIVES IT                      |

   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+

   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+

              v                    v                     v
   THE INDIVIDUAL IS      MEDIATION vs THE LEAP    BOTH/AND vs EITHER/OR
   PARTICULAR, TEMPORAL,  Hegel: opposites are     Hegel: the higher
   PASSIONATE, DECIDING   reconciled by thought    unity dissolves the
   - none of which is a   Kierkegaard: the pass-   exclusive choice
   category the system    age between spheres is   Kierkegaard: to exist

                          (Springet), not a        thing rather than
                          logical transition       another

   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+

   |                                                                    |
   |   IT DOES SAY                     IT DOES NOT SAY                  |
   |   For ETHICAL and RELIGIOUS       That 'anything I believe is      |
   |   truth, the HOW of appropria-    true'                            |
   |   tion is decisive                                                 |
   |   Infinite passionate inward-     That 2+2=4 or a historical date  |
   |   ness, commitment, RISK          varies with my passion           |
   |   The truth must be LIVED, not    That objective inquiry is        |

   |                                   INSUFFICIENT for existence       |
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+

   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   |   THE DIALECTICAL COMPLEMENT THAT STOPS THE RELATIVIST READING     |
   |   'SUBJECTIVITY IS UNTRUTH.'  The existing subject is ALSO in      |
   |   untruth - in sin, in finitude, not identical with the eternal -  |
   |   and therefore NEEDS the leap.  A relativist has no use for this  |
   |   sentence; Kierkegaard cannot do without it.                      |

```

*Caption: the two boxes at the bottom are the answer to the commonest objection. If "truth is subjectivity" meant that whatever I sincerely believe is true, the companion sentence "subjectivity is untruth" would be unintelligible — and Climacus writes both.*

**NUMBERED TEACHING.**

1. **Name the target precisely.** ✅ Kierkegaard's opponent is **Hegel's System**: the claim that reality is the self-development of Absolute Spirit (*Geist*), that oppositions are **mediated** into higher unities, and that the individual is a moment within a total rational process. ⚠️ Do not expound the dialectic here — that is Topic 05. State the target in two clauses and spend the answer on the counter.
2. **The counter is structural, not a complaint about difficulty.** ✅ The system explains everything **except the existing individual who is supposed to understand it**. To exist is to be *particular*, *temporal*, *passionate* and *deciding*; none of these is a category the system can accommodate without cancelling it. The single sentence to deploy is: **"A logical system is possible; an existential system is impossible."**
3. **"Truth is subjectivity" belongs to Johannes Climacus.** ✅ It is argued in the *Concluding Unscientific Postscript* (**1846**), written under the pseudonym **Johannes Climacus** — a self-described humorist who explicitly says he is **not** a Christian, which is exactly why he can pose the problem of *becoming* one. ⚠️ Attribute it to Climacus. Kierkegaard printed the request that his pseudonyms be cited by name.
4. **What the thesis asserts.** ✅ For **ethical and religious** truth, the *mode of appropriation* matters more than the objective content: **how** one holds a belief — with infinite passionate inwardness, personal commitment and risk — is the locus of truth for the existing subject. The worked contrast: to "know" that God exists as an item in a philosophical system is not the same as *existing before God* in faith and trembling.
5. **What the thesis does not assert, in three parts.** ⚠️ (i) It is **not** the relativist claim that anything believed is true; that is the standard misreading and a direct UPSC trap. (ii) It does **not** deny objective truth for logic, mathematics or history; it denies its **sufficiency** for existential and religious truth. (iii) It is **not** a general theory that factual truth varies with the passion of the believer.
6. **The complement that settles the reading: "subjectivity is untruth".** ✅ Climacus's dialectical counterpart states that the existing subject is *also* in untruth — in sin, in finitude, in non-identity with the eternal — and therefore stands in need of the **leap**. ⚠️ This is the decisive evidence against the relativist reading and should be quoted in any answer on subjectivity: a relativist has no use for the sentence, and Climacus cannot do without it.
7. **"The crowd is untruth."** ✅ The social form of the same point. The anonymous public permits ethical evasion, because a responsibility distributed among everyone is borne by no one. Authentic existence requires the individual to stand alone — before God, or before the ethical demand. ⚠️ Note the structural parallel with Heidegger's **das Man** (Session 15), but do not merge them: Kierkegaard's target is evasion of *responsibility before God*, Heidegger's is a *levelling of possibilities* with no theological content.
8. **The Hegelian reply, and Kierkegaard's counter.** ❓ A Hegelian may answer that the "existing individual" whose truth resists systematisation is simply an unreflective stage that philosophy sublates. Kierkegaard's counter is that **no system can contain the one who builds it**: the systematiser must still exist, choose and die. ⚠️ The honest verdict is bounded — Kierkegaard exposes a first-person appropriation that a completed system-view risks losing, but he does not thereby refute Hegel's ontology or offer a rival comprehensive system, and Hegel need not simply deny individuality.
9. **Why the method follows from the doctrine.** ⚠️ If existential truth is a *mode of existing* rather than a result, it cannot be handed over directly without being falsified — which is why Kierkegaard writes pseudonymously and indirectly. Session 5 develops this; note here only that the method is a **consequence** of the thesis, not a literary preference.

**HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification.**

> **Claim.** Kierkegaard's thesis that truth is subjectivity is a claim about the mode in which existential truth can be possessed, and its force comes from a structural objection to systematic philosophy rather than from any doubt about objective knowledge. **Named evidence.** The pseudonym Johannes Climacus argues in the *Concluding Unscientific Postscript* (1846) that for ethical and religious truth the *how* of appropriation — infinite passionate inwardness, commitment, risk — is decisive, and in the same work states the dialectical complement that "subjectivity is untruth", since the existing subject stands in sin and finitude and therefore needs the leap; the accompanying formula against Hegel is that a logical system is possible but an existential system is not. **Analysis.** The two sentences must be read together: if the thesis were relativism, its complement would be unintelligible, and the point of the pair is that the existing individual is neither a self-certifying authority nor a moment inside a completed totality, but a finite subject whose relation to the truth is itself part of what makes the truth his. **Qualification.** ⚠️ The critique is bounded — it exposes what a system-view risks losing about first-person appropriation without refuting Hegel's ontology, and Kierkegaard offers no rival comprehensive system in its place.

**EXAM LINK.** This session is the core of **2020 Q3(a)** ("Subjectivity is the truth" and the single individual), **2022 Q4(a)** (subjectivity, then the three stages), **2023 Q1(d)** (against Hegel's universal spirit) and **2024 Q1(e)** (truth in terms of subjectivity). Four of the fourteen owned parts — nearly a third of the clause — begin here.

**UPSC TRAPS.**

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| "Truth is subjectivity" = relativism | ✅ It concerns the **mode of appropriation**, not the propositional content, and Climacus also writes "**subjectivity is untruth**". |
| Writing "Kierkegaard says in the *Postscript*…" | ⚠️ The book is signed **Johannes Climacus**, and Kierkegaard printed a request that the pseudonym be cited. Attribution is a free precision mark. |
| Expounding Hegel's dialectic at length | ⚠️ Topic 05. Two clauses of target-setting, then the counter. |
| Reducing the critique to "Kierkegaard disliked abstraction" | ✅ The objection is structural: no system can contain the one who builds it; **either/or** against **both/and**; the **leap** against **mediation**. |
| Treating "the crowd is untruth" as Heidegger's *das Man* | ⚠️ Structurally parallel, doctrinally different — evasion before God against the levelling of possibilities. |
| Claiming Kierkegaard refutes Hegel outright | ❓ The honest verdict is bounded: he exposes a loss, not a contradiction, and offers no rival system. |

**REVISION NOTES.**

- Target: **Hegel's System** — Absolute Spirit, mediation, the individual as a moment, "the truth is the whole".
- Counter-sentence: **"A logical system is possible; an existential system is impossible."**
- **Johannes Climacus**, *Concluding Unscientific Postscript*, **1846** — a humorist who says he is not a Christian.
- Thesis = the **how** of appropriation for **ethical/religious** truth: infinite passionate inwardness, commitment, risk.
- Three denials: not relativism · not a denial of objective truth · not a theory that facts vary with passion.
- Complement: **"subjectivity is untruth"** — sin, finitude, need of the leap.
- Social form: **"the crowd is untruth"** — distributed responsibility is no responsibility.
- Verdict: exposes what system-view loses; does not refute Hegel or replace him.

**CLOSING RECALL FLOW.**

```text
   HEGEL'S SYSTEM (mediation, the whole) -> IT CANNOT CONTAIN THE ONE
   WHO BUILDS IT -> 'a logical system is possible, an existential system
   is impossible' -> CLIMACUS, Postscript 1846: TRUTH IS SUBJECTIVITY =
   the HOW of appropriation for ETHICAL/RELIGIOUS truth -> NOT relativism
   (objective truth retained, only INSUFFICIENT) -> 'SUBJECTIVITY IS
   UNTRUTH' proves it -> 'THE CROWD IS UNTRUTH' -> the single individual
   must stand alone -> and therefore the METHOD must be INDIRECT
```

### SESSION 3 — KIERKEGAARD: THE THREE SPHERES OF EXISTENCE, THE LEAP, AND THE TELEOLOGICAL SUSPENSION OF THE ETHICAL

**DEFINITION.** *Plain language:* Kierkegaard maps three ways a life can be organised — around enjoyment, around duty, and around a relation to God — and insists you do not grow from one into the next; you have to jump, because there is no argument that carries you across. *Technical:* the **spheres** or **stages of existence** (aesthetic, ethical, religious) are **qualitative** modes of existing, each with its own governing category and its own characteristic collapse; transition between them is not psychological maturation and not Hegelian mediation but a **qualitative leap** (*Springet*) made in freedom.

**ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING.** ⚠️ *"The three spheres are not a staircase up which a person matures; they are three incompatible ways of organising a life, each of which breaks down in its own way — and what carries a person across the break is not an argument but a leap, which is exactly why Hegelian mediation cannot describe the movement."*

**MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS.** aesthetic · ethical · religious · qualitative sphere, not chronological stage · the leap (*Springet*) · boredom, guilt, paradox · Judge William · Johannes de Silentio · teleological suspension of the ethical · knight of faith · knight of infinite resignation · the absurd.

**Plain-language visual — the idea before the vocabulary.**

```text
   THREE WAYS TO ANSWER 'WHAT IS MY LIFE ORGANISED AROUND?'

   AESTHETIC          ->  the INTERESTING.  Keep options open; avoid
                          anything that binds.  Ends in BOREDOM and
                          a scattered self.
   ETHICAL            ->  DUTY.  Marry, work, be transparent, accept
                          the universal.  Ends in GUILT, because the
                          demand cannot be perfectly met.
   RELIGIOUS          ->  GOD, related to as a single individual.
                          Ends in PARADOX: faith is not rational, not
                          universal, and cannot be mediated.

   YOU DO NOT GRADUATE.  YOU LEAP.  AND YOU CAN FALL BACK.
```

*What this shows: each sphere is defined by what it treats as final, and each collapses from the inside — which is what makes the next sphere thinkable without making it deducible.*

**🖼️ VISUAL — the three spheres, their limits, and the two knights.**

```text
   |   RESIGNATION                                                      |
   |   Gives the finite up, and     Gives it up AND RECEIVES IT BACK.   |
   |   the renunciation SHOWS.      Outwardly INDISTINGUISHABLE from a  |
   |   Visibly heroic, visibly      bourgeois citizen: he lives fully   |
   |   apart from ordinary life.    in the finite world WHILE relating  |
   |                                absolutely to the Absolute.         |
   |                                                                    |
   |   THE DOUBLE MOVEMENT: infinite resignation FIRST, then faith -    |
   |   which is why faith is HIGHER than resignation, not easier        |
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
```

*Caption: the two knights are the fastest way to show that faith, for Johannes de Silentio, is not withdrawal from the world. The knight of infinite resignation is legible; the knight of faith is invisible — and that invisibility is the doctrine.*

**NUMBERED TEACHING.**

1. **Spheres, not stages in time.** ⚠️ The commonest error is to present the three as a developmental chronology. They are **qualitative spheres**: one does not "grow out of" the aesthetic by maturation, and **relapse is always possible**. The word "stages" is standard in English translations, which is exactly why the qualification must be written explicitly.
2. **Aesthetic.** ✅ The life organised around the **interesting** — enjoyment, immediacy, irony, and the systematic avoidance of anything that binds. Portrayed pseudonymously in *Either/Or* (**1843**), edited by **Victor Eremita**, who prints the aesthetic papers ("A") and the ethical papers side by side **without deciding between them**, so that the reader must choose. Its limit is **boredom, fragmentation and despair**: a self with no binding commitment has nothing to hold it together.
3. **Ethical.** ✅ The life organised around **universal duty** — commitment, marriage, vocation, transparency — voiced by **Judge William** in *Either/Or*. The self here is a responsible agent choosing under universal categories. Its limit is **guilt**: the ethical demand cannot be perfectly fulfilled, and the universal cannot accommodate the individual's anguished singularity.
4. **Religious.** ✅ The life organised around standing **before God as a singular individual**, with faith as "the absurd" and infinite passion. Its limit — better, its defining structure — is **paradox**: faith is not rational, not universal, and not mediated by the crowd or the church.
5. **The leap (*Springet*).** ✅ Transition is a **qualitative choice**, not a Hegelian deduction and not a psychological development. ⚠️ **Provenance caution:** Kierkegaard writes of *the leap* and of *faith*; the fused English phrase "**leap of faith**" belongs to the English tradition and is not a literal translation of a Kierkegaardian term. Write "the leap — what the English tradition calls the leap of faith" and the caution is discharged in six words.
6. **The teleological suspension of the ethical.** ✅ Analysed by the pseudonym **Johannes de Silentio** in *Fear and Trembling* (**1843**) through Abraham, commanded to sacrifice Isaac. The ethical (the universal) is *suspended* in relation to a higher *telos*, the absolute relation to the Absolute. ⚠️ The text **stages the paradox** of faith; it must not be cited as Kierkegaard's straightforward licence for private revelation to override morality. De Silentio himself writes as one who **admires faith but does not have it** — hence "silentio", the silence of one who can describe Abraham but not understand him.
7. **The two knights.** ✅ The **knight of infinite resignation** renounces the finite visibly; the **knight of faith** is outwardly indistinguishable from a bourgeois citizen — he lives fully in the finite world *while* relating absolutely to the Absolute, having made the double movement of resignation *and* faith. ⚠️ This is the fastest available refutation of the "existentialism is world-denying" reading.
8. **Why the spheres matter for the subjectivity thesis.** ⚠️ The spheres are the **content** of which "truth is subjectivity" is the **form**: each sphere is a mode of appropriating existence, and the intensification from dispersion (aesthetic) through duty (ethical) to the absolute relation (religious) *is* the intensification of inwardness. In 2022 Q4(a) the paper asks for both in one answer, in exactly this order.
9. **The standing objection.** ❓ **Fideism/irrationalism**: the leap abandons rational accountability. Kierkegaard's reply is that reason is not *wrong* but *limited* — it cannot deliver what the religious individual needs. ⚠️ Whether that is a defensible epistemology or a *reductio* is a live question, and the honest closing move is to say so rather than to pretend the objection has been answered.

**HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification.**

> **Claim.** Kierkegaard's three spheres are qualitative modes of existing rather than developmental stages, and the movement between them must be a leap because each sphere collapses in a way that no argument internal to it can repair. **Named evidence.** *Either/Or* (1843), edited by the pseudonym Victor Eremita, prints the aesthetic papers of "A" beside Judge William's ethical papers without adjudicating between them, so that the reader is forced into the choice; *Fear and Trembling* (1843), written by Johannes de Silentio, stages the religious sphere through Abraham and the teleological suspension of the ethical, and distinguishes the visibly renouncing knight of infinite resignation from the knight of faith, who is outwardly indistinguishable from an ordinary citizen. **Analysis.** Each sphere has a characteristic collapse — boredom in the aesthetic, guilt in the ethical, paradox in the religious — and because the collapse is *of* the sphere's own governing category, no mediation can carry the individual across; only a qualitative leap in freedom can, which is precisely the movement Hegelian dialectic has no room for. **Qualification.** ⚠️ *Fear and Trembling* stages the paradox rather than licensing private revelation against morality, and the fused phrase "leap of faith" is the English tradition's rather than Kierkegaard's, who writes of the leap (*Springet*).

**EXAM LINK.** This session carries the second half of **2022 Q4(a)** ("explain it with reference to three stages of existence") and supplies the decisive movement-concept — the leap against mediation — for **2023 Q1(d)** and **2020 Q3(a)**.

**UPSC TRAPS.**

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| Presenting the spheres as a chronological or developmental ladder | ✅ They are **qualitative spheres**; one leaps, and relapse is always possible. |
| Quoting "leap of faith" as Kierkegaard's phrase | ⚠️ He writes of **the leap** (*Springet*); the fused phrase is the English tradition's. Say so in one clause. |
| Using the teleological suspension as a licence for private revelation over morality | ✅ *Fear and Trembling* **stages the paradox**, and de Silentio explicitly does not possess the faith he describes. |
| Writing "Kierkegaard argues in *Fear and Trembling*" | ⚠️ The signatory is **Johannes de Silentio**. |
| Conflating the two knights | ✅ Resignation is **visible**; faith is **invisible** and lives fully in the finite after the double movement. |
| Treating the aesthetic sphere as mere hedonism | ⚠️ Its category is the **interesting**, and its structural feature is the refusal of binding commitment — irony and detachment, not simply pleasure. |

**REVISION NOTES.**

- Three **spheres**: aesthetic (the interesting → **boredom**) · ethical (universal duty → **guilt**) · religious (before God → **paradox**).
- *Either/Or* **1843**, ed. **Victor Eremita**; Judge William voices the ethical; "A" the aesthetic.
- *Fear and Trembling* **1843**, **Johannes de Silentio** — Abraham, **teleological suspension of the ethical**.
- Transition = **qualitative leap** (*Springet*), not mediation, not maturation; **relapse possible**.
- **Knight of infinite resignation** (visible renunciation) vs **knight of faith** (invisible; double movement; lives in the finite).
- Provenance: "**leap of faith**" is the **English tradition's** phrase.
- Objection: **fideism**; reply: reason is limited, not wrong; verdict left open.

**CLOSING RECALL FLOW.**

```text
   AESTHETIC (the interesting) -> collapses in BOREDOM -> ETHICAL
   (universal duty, Judge William) -> collapses in GUILT -> RELIGIOUS
   (before God, de Silentio's Abraham) -> lives as PARADOX ->
   MOVEMENT = QUALITATIVE LEAP (Springet), never mediation ->
   KNIGHT OF RESIGNATION is visible, KNIGHT OF FAITH is not ->
   the spheres are the CONTENT of which 'truth is subjectivity' is the FORM
```

### SESSION 4 — KIERKEGAARD: ANXIETY AS THE DIZZINESS OF FREEDOM, AND DESPAIR AS A MISRELATION IN THE SELF

**DEFINITION.** *Plain language:* anxiety is what you feel when you realise you *could* — not that something will hurt you, but that nothing stops you; and despair, for Kierkegaard, is not sadness but a structural fault in the way a self is related to itself and to what made it. *Technical:* **anxiety or dread** (*Angest*), analysed by the pseudonym **Vigilius Haufniensis** in *The Concept of Anxiety* (**1844**), is the "dizziness of freedom" — an objectless disclosure of **possibility**, distinct in kind from fear; **despair**, analysed by **Anti-Climacus** in *The Sickness unto Death* (**1849**), is a **misrelation** in the relation the self is — the self failing to be itself before the power that established it.

**ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING.** ⚠️ *"Anxiety, for Vigilius Haufniensis, is not fear intensified but fear's opposite in structure: fear has an object and drives me back into the world, while anxiety has none and discloses that I am able — which is why Kierkegaard can call it the dizziness of freedom and make it the presupposition of the leap."*

**MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS.** anxiety or dread (*Angest*) · Vigilius Haufniensis · "the dizziness of freedom" · "a sympathetic antipathy and an antipathetic sympathy" · the object of anxiety is a nothing · innocence · despair · Anti-Climacus · misrelation · the self is a relation relating itself to itself · not willing to be oneself · defiantly willing to be oneself.

**Plain-language visual — the idea before the vocabulary.**

```text
   AT THE EDGE OF A CLIFF

   FEAR                                 ANXIETY
   'The rock might give way.'           'Nothing is stopping me from
   An OBJECT threatens me.              throwing myself over.'
   I step back, INTO the world.         No object at all - what
                                        appears is that I AM ABLE.
        |                                          |
        v                                          v
   Discloses a THREAT.                  Discloses POSSIBILITY, i.e.
                                        FREEDOM - and it dizzies me
                                        because the ground of the
                                        choice is nowhere but in me.

   'The dizziness is as much in the EYE as in the ABYSS.'
```

*What this shows: the difference is not intensity. It is whether there is an object at all — and it is the absence of the object that makes anxiety philosophically informative.*

**🖼️ VISUAL — anxiety and the two shapes of despair.**

```text
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   |   ANXIETY (Angest) - Vigilius Haufniensis, THE CONCEPT OF ANXIETY  |
   |   1844.  Lowrie translates the title 'The Concept of DREAD'.       |
   |                                                                    |
   |   OBJECT ......... none.  'The object of anxiety is a NOTHING.'   |
   |   WHAT APPEARS ... POSSIBILITY - 'the possibility of being able'  |
   |   FORMULA ........ 'the DIZZINESS OF FREEDOM'                     |
   |   AMBIVALENCE .... 'a sympathetic antipathy and an antipathetic   |
   |                     sympathy' - we are DRAWN to what we SHRINK    |
   |                     from                                          |
   |   IN INNOCENCE ... there is no knowledge of good and evil, yet    |
   |                     anxiety is ALREADY there, because freedom's   |
   |                     possibility is already sensed                 |
   |   ROLE ........... anxiety is the PRESUPPOSITION of the LEAP by   |
   |                     which sin - and selfhood - enters             |
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
                                   |
                                   v
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   |   DESPAIR - Anti-Climacus, THE SICKNESS UNTO DEATH 1849            |
   |   Anti-Climacus writes as a Christian at an EXTRAORDINARILY HIGH   |
   |   level - deliberately the MIRROR IMAGE of Climacus, who says he   |
   |   is not a Christian at all                                        |
   |                                                                    |
   |   THE SELF IS NOT A THING.  It is a RELATION that relates itself   |
   |   to itself - and, in relating to itself, relates to THE POWER     |
   |   THAT ESTABLISHED IT.                                             |
   |                                                                    |
   |   DESPAIR = A MISRELATION IN THAT RELATION.  Not an emotion.       |
   |   Not sadness.  A STRUCTURAL FAULT that may be entirely unfelt.    |
   |                                                                    |
   |   FORM 1  NOT WILLING TO BE ONESELF                                |
   |           - fleeing the self one is; wanting to be someone else,   |
   |             or no one                                              |
   |   FORM 2  DEFIANTLY WILLING TO BE ONESELF                          |
   |           - insisting on being oneself WITHOUT the grounding       |
   |             God-relation; self-assertion as the refusal of         |
   |             dependence                                             |
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
                                   |
                                   v
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   |   THE FENCE THAT MUST BE STATED                                    |
   |   KIERKEGAARDIAN DESPAIR (theological: misrelation before God)     |
   |        IS NOT                                                      |
   |   SARTREAN DESPAIR (desespoir, 1946 lecture: acting without        |
   |        relying on outcomes beyond one's control)                   |
   |   Same English word, two unrelated doctrines.                      |
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
```

*Caption: the bottom panel is worth a mark on its own. "Despair" appears in both Kierkegaard and Sartre, and the two uses have nothing in common beyond the translator's word.*

**NUMBERED TEACHING.**

1. **Anxiety is objectless, and that is the whole argument.** ✅ **Vigilius Haufniensis** ("the watchman of Copenhagen") writes in *The Concept of Anxiety* (**1844**) as a **psychological observer**, deliberately not as a dogmatician. Anxiety's object "is a **nothing**"; what discloses itself is **possibility** — that one can choose, and can fail.
2. **The formula and the ambivalence.** ✅ Anxiety is "the **dizziness of freedom**", and it is "a **sympathetic antipathy and an antipathetic sympathy**" — we are drawn towards what we shrink from. ⚠️ Both phrases are safe as **renderings with attribution**; Lowrie and Hong differ, and Lowrie's title is *The Concept of Dread*. Name the translation or use the Danish *Angest* once.
3. **The canonical example.** ✅ A man at the edge of an abyss looks down and grows dizzy — "the dizziness is as much in the **eye** as in the **abyss**." The vertigo is not produced by the drop; it is produced by the disclosure that nothing prevents him.
4. **Anxiety in innocence.** ✅ In innocence there is no knowledge of good and evil, and yet anxiety is already present, because freedom's possibility is already sensed. ⚠️ This is why anxiety is the **presupposition** of the leap by which sin — and, with it, selfhood — enters: anxiety does not *cause* the leap, which would make the leap a determined event, but it is the condition in which a leap becomes intelligible.
5. **Despair is structural, not emotional.** ✅ **Anti-Climacus** opens *The Sickness unto Death* (**1849**) with the definition that the self is a **relation that relates itself to itself**, and, in so relating, relates to **the power that established it**. **Despair** is a **misrelation** in that relation. ⚠️ Consequently a person may be in despair without feeling anything — the commonest and most examinable feature of the doctrine.
6. **The two shapes of despair.** ✅ **Not willing to be oneself** — fleeing the self one actually is. **Defiantly willing to be oneself** — insisting on being oneself *without* the grounding God-relation. The second is the deeper form precisely because it looks like strength.
7. **Anti-Climacus is a constructed standpoint.** ✅ He writes as a Christian at an **extraordinarily high** level — above Kierkegaard himself, and the deliberate mirror image of **Climacus**, who says he is not a Christian. ⚠️ The pair is evidence that the pseudonyms are **existence-possibilities**, not disguises (Session 5).
8. **The fence against Sartre.** ⚠️ Sartre's **despair** (*désespoir*), in the 1946 lecture, means acting without relying on outcomes beyond one's control and reasonable probabilities. It is not a theological misrelation, and the two must never be merged. ✅ Similarly, Kierkegaard's *Angest* and Heidegger's *Angst* are related but non-identical: Session 17 sets out the three-way comparison.
9. **Why the examiner cares.** ⚠️ Anxiety is the point at which Kierkegaard's psychology becomes an argument about **freedom**, and despair is the point at which his account of the self becomes an argument about **dependence**. Both feed the subjectivity questions: the single individual is anxious because free, and in despair because it will not be grounded.

**HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification.**

> **Claim.** In Kierkegaard's pseudonymous psychology, anxiety and despair are not moods reported but structures disclosed: anxiety discloses freedom, and despair discloses the self's failure to be grounded. **Named evidence.** Vigilius Haufniensis, in *The Concept of Anxiety* (1844), states that the object of anxiety is a nothing and calls anxiety the dizziness of freedom, illustrating it with the man at the abyss in whom the dizziness lies as much in the eye as in the depth; Anti-Climacus, in *The Sickness unto Death* (1849), defines the self as a relation that relates itself to itself and, in so doing, relates to the power that established it, and defines despair as a misrelation within that relation, appearing either as not willing to be oneself or as defiantly willing to be oneself without that grounding. **Analysis.** Because anxiety has no object it cannot be disclosing an entity, so what it discloses must be a structure — possibility, "the possibility of being able" — and because despair is a misrelation rather than a feeling, it can be present unfelt, which is why Anti-Climacus can call it universal without making an empirical claim about how people feel. **Qualification.** ⚠️ Both analyses are **pseudonymous** and voice-specific, and the English word "despair" must be kept apart from Sartre's *désespoir*, which concerns acting without reliance on outcomes rather than any relation to God.

**EXAM LINK.** This session supplies the mood-analysis that any Kierkegaard answer needs as its psychological engine (**2020 Q3(a)**, **2022 Q4(a)**, **2024 Q1(e)**), and it is one of the three legs of the fear/anxiety comparison in **Session 17** that a question on dread or anguish requires.

**UPSC TRAPS.**

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| Defining anxiety as intense fear | ✅ Fear has a determinate object; anxiety's object "is a **nothing**". The difference is in **kind**. |
| Treating despair as sadness or depression | ✅ It is a **misrelation** in the self's relation to itself and to the power that established it — and may be entirely unfelt. |
| Merging Kierkegaardian and Sartrean despair | ⚠️ Two doctrines, one translator's word. Sartre's *désespoir* is about not relying on outcomes beyond one's control. |
| Attributing the analyses to Kierkegaard directly | ⚠️ **Vigilius Haufniensis** (1844) and **Anti-Climacus** (1849) — cite the pseudonyms. |
| Saying anxiety *causes* the leap | ⚠️ It is the **presupposition** of the leap; a caused leap would not be a leap. |
| Ignoring the translation split | ⚠️ *Angest* is "dread" in Lowrie and "anxiety" in Hong; the book title differs accordingly. |

**REVISION NOTES.**

- **Anxiety** (*Angest*), **Vigilius Haufniensis**, *The Concept of Anxiety*, **1844**; Lowrie: *The Concept of Dread*.
- Object = **a nothing**; what appears is **possibility**; formula = "the **dizziness of freedom**".
- "A **sympathetic antipathy and an antipathetic sympathy**"; the abyss example — dizziness in the **eye**.
- Anxiety already present **in innocence**; it is the **presupposition** of the leap.
- **Despair**, **Anti-Climacus**, *The Sickness unto Death*, **1849**: the self = a relation relating itself to itself and thereby to **the power that established it**.
- Despair = **misrelation**, possibly unfelt; two forms — **not willing** to be oneself; **defiantly willing** to be oneself without the God-relation.
- Fence: Kierkegaardian despair ≠ **Sartrean *désespoir***.

**CLOSING RECALL FLOW.**

```text
   FEAR has an object -> ANXIETY has NONE -> so it discloses not a
   THING but a STRUCTURE -> POSSIBILITY = 'the possibility of being
   able' -> 'THE DIZZINESS OF FREEDOM' (Haufniensis 1844) -> present
   already IN INNOCENCE -> PRESUPPOSITION of the LEAP -> and the self
   that leaps is a RELATION relating itself to itself (Anti-Climacus
   1849) -> failure of that relation = DESPAIR, in two forms, possibly
   UNFELT -> not Sartre's desespoir
```
