### SESSION 5 — KIERKEGAARD: INDIRECT COMMUNICATION AND PSEUDONYMITY AS THE FORM OF THE DOCTRINE

**DEFINITION.** *Plain language:* if the truth you want to convey is a way of living rather than a piece of information, then telling someone directly will destroy it — they will file it as a fact instead of becoming it — so Kierkegaard writes in voices that refuse to give the reader a result. *Technical:* **indirect communication** (*indirekte Meddelelse*) is a form of address that withholds the result, declines authority and forces the recipient to relate to the possibility for himself; **pseudonymity** is its execution, in which each pseudonym is a **personified existence-possibility** writing from within a sphere of existence it cannot see beyond.

**ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING.** ⚠️ *"Pseudonymity is not literary decoration but the formal counterpart of the doctrine that truth is subjectivity: because ethical and religious truth is a mode of existing rather than a result, it can be communicated only in a form that refuses to deliver a result."*

**MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS.** indirect communication (*indirekte Meddelelse*) · direct communication · appropriation · Socratic maieutics · "deceives into the truth" · vanishing occasion · personified existence-possibility · double authorship · upbuilding discourses · First and Last Declaration · "author's author" · prompter (*souffleur*) · revocation.

**Plain-language visual — the idea before the vocabulary.**

```text
   TWO WAYS OF TELLING SOMEONE SOMETHING

   DIRECT                                INDIRECT
   'Here is the result.'                 'Here is a person living this
   The reader RECEIVES it and            possibility to its end.  I
   files it.  Nothing about              give you no result and no
   his existence need change.            authority.'
        |                                          |
        v                                          v
   PERFECT for history, science,         NECESSARY when the truth IS
   mathematics.                          a way of existing - because
                                         a received result would
                                         FALSIFY exactly what was
                                         being communicated.
```

*What this shows: the choice of form is forced by the kind of truth in question. It is an argument, not a style.*

**🖼️ VISUAL — the six-step argument, the pseudonyms, and the double authorship.**

```text
   1  OBJECTIVE TRUTH - historical, scientific, metaphysical - can be
      transmitted DIRECTLY.  I state a result; you receive it; nothing
      about your existence need change.
                                |
   2  ETHICAL AND RELIGIOUS TRUTH is not a result but a MODE OF
      EXISTING, an APPROPRIATION in inwardness (Climacus, Postscript
      1846: truth is subjectivity - the HOW of the relation).
                                |
   3  IF such truth were communicated DIRECTLY, the recipient would
      take it over AS A RESULT - as information, as doctrine, as
      something one can KNOW WITHOUT BECOMING.  The communication
      would thereby FALSIFY what it communicates.
                                |
   4  THEREFORE ethical-religious truth requires INDIRECT
      COMMUNICATION: a form that WITHHOLDS the result, REFUSES to be
      an authority, and forces the reader to relate to the
      possibility FOR HIMSELF.
                                |
   5  THE MODEL IS SOCRATIC MAIEUTICS - the midwife who brings to
      birth what the other must produce, and who 'DECEIVES INTO THE
      TRUTH'.  The author must become a VANISHING OCCASION, not a
      teacher with a doctrine.
                                |
   6  THEREFORE PSEUDONYMITY.  The pseudonyms are not disguises for a
      shy author but PERSONIFIED EXISTENCE-POSSIBILITIES, each writing
      from INSIDE a sphere it cannot see beyond.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------
   PSEUDONYM              WORK (YEAR)        STANDPOINT WRITTEN FROM
   Victor Eremita (ed.)   Either/Or 1843     prints aesthetic 'A' and
                                             ethical Judge William side
                                             by side WITHOUT deciding
   Johannes de Silentio   Fear and           ADMIRES faith but DOES NOT
                          Trembling 1843     HAVE it - hence 'silence'
   Constantin Constantius Repetition 1843    aesthetic-experimental
                                             observer
   Vigilius Haufniensis   The Concept of     psychological observer of
   'watchman of           Anxiety 1844       anxiety, deliberately NOT
   Copenhagen'                               a dogmatician
   Johannes Climacus      Philosophical      a HUMORIST who is
                          Fragments 1844;    explicitly NOT A CHRISTIAN
                          Postscript 1846    - which is why he can pose
                                             the problem of BECOMING one
   Anti-Climacus          The Sickness unto  a Christian at an
                          Death 1849;        EXTRAORDINARILY HIGH level
                          Practice in        - above Kierkegaard
                          Christianity 1850  himself; Climacus's mirror
   ------------------------------------------------------------------
   THE DOUBLE AUTHORSHIP: simultaneously, UNDER HIS OWN NAME,
   Kierkegaard published the UPBUILDING / EDIFYING DISCOURSES.  The
   signed religious writings stand BESIDE the pseudonymous works as the
   DIRECT pole of a single strategy.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------
   THE INSTRUCTION HE PRINTED: the 'FIRST AND LAST DECLARATION'
   appended to the Postscript (1846).  He acknowledges authorship while
   insisting he is only the 'AUTHOR'S AUTHOR', a PROMPTER (souffleur)
   who produced characters, and ASKS THAT ANYONE CITING THEM QUOTE THE
   PSEUDONYM'S NAME, NOT HIS.
```

*Caption: steps 1–6 are an argument, not a biography. Read them in order and pseudonymity stops being a curiosity about Kierkegaard and becomes a consequence of the thesis in Session 2.*

**NUMBERED TEACHING.**

1. **This is now Core, not optional background.** ✅ Indirect communication and the pseudonymous authorship were promoted out of optional-enrichment status on **15 August 2026** and are marks-essential for any question on subjectivity or the critique of Hegel. ⚠️ A "truth is subjectivity" answer that omits the form the doctrine forces is incomplete, and the point is almost never made in ordinary scripts.
2. **Follow the six steps in order.** ✅ They are set out in the visual above; the load-bearing step is **3** — direct communication would convert a mode of existing into a result and thereby falsify it.
3. **Socratic maieutics and the vanishing occasion.** ✅ The model is the **midwife** who brings to birth what the other must produce, and who, in Kierkegaard's phrase, "**deceives into the truth**". The author must become a **vanishing occasion**, not a teacher with a doctrine. ⚠️ Note the deliberate provocation in "deceives": the deception is formal, not moral — the reader is denied a result *for his own sake*.
4. **The pseudonyms are standpoints, not masks.** ✅ Each writes from inside a sphere and cannot see beyond it, which is why **de Silentio** can describe Abraham but not understand him, why **Climacus** can pose the problem of becoming a Christian precisely because he is not one, and why **Anti-Climacus** is placed *above* Kierkegaard rather than beside him.
5. **The double authorship.** ✅ Simultaneously and **under his own name**, Kierkegaard published the *Upbuilding* (Edifying) *Discourses*. The signed religious writings are the **direct pole** of a single strategy. ⚠️ Saying this shows that pseudonymity is a **method**, not an evasion.
6. **The printed instruction.** ✅ In the "**First and Last Declaration**" appended to the *Postscript* (**1846**), Kierkegaard acknowledges authorship of the pseudonymous books while insisting that he is only their "**author's author**", a **prompter** (*souffleur*), and asks that anyone citing them **quote the pseudonym's name, not his**. ❌ Writing "Kierkegaard says in *Fear and Trembling*…" ignores an instruction the author printed.
7. **Objection 1 — unattributability.** ❓ If the author speaks through masks, his own position becomes unpinnable and the method insulates him from criticism. **Reply:** the signed discourses supply the direct pole, and *The Point of View for My Work as an Author* — **written 1848, published posthumously 1859** — gives an explicit account of the strategy. ⚠️ **Residual:** *The Point of View* is itself a retrospective self-interpretation and is disputed as evidence.
8. **Objection 2 — self-undermining.** ❓ If subjective truth cannot be said directly, the thesis "truth is subjectivity" cannot be said directly either. **Reply:** Climacus **concedes exactly this** — the *Postscript* ends by **revoking** itself. ⚠️ This is a first-class exam observation: Kierkegaard's revocation, the early Wittgenstein's ladder at *Tractatus* 6.54, and Hegel's Unhappy Consciousness all end in a text that consumes itself. Cross-link, in one clause, to Topics 06 and 05 — and do not expound either.
9. **Objection 3 — the Hegelian reply.** ❓ The "existing individual" whose truth resists systematisation is simply an unreflective stage philosophy sublates. **Counter:** no system can contain the one who *builds* it; the systematiser must still exist, choose and die.
10. **The executable verdict.** ⚠️ Pseudonymity is the formal counterpart of the doctrine that truth is subjectivity. The method's **cost** is that it is self-consuming — Climacus revokes the *Postscript* — and its **power** is that the cost is intended: Kierkegaard is not evading the demand for a position but denying that a position, in the systematic sense Hegel required, is the sort of thing an existing individual can hand over.

**HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification.**

> **Claim.** Kierkegaard's pseudonymity is the formal consequence of his account of existential truth, not a literary habit that can be omitted from an exposition of it. **Named evidence.** Climacus argues in the *Concluding Unscientific Postscript* (1846) that ethical and religious truth is a mode of existing rather than a result; the same volume carries the "First and Last Declaration", in which Kierkegaard acknowledges the pseudonymous books while calling himself only their "author's author" and a prompter, and asks that citations name the pseudonym; and the *Upbuilding Discourses*, published simultaneously under his own name, form the direct pole of the same strategy. **Analysis.** If such truth were transmitted directly, the recipient would take it over as information and could hold it without becoming it, so the communication would falsify its own content; the remedy is a Socratic form that withholds the result and makes the author a vanishing occasion, which is executed by giving each existence-possibility its own voice — de Silentio who admires faith without having it, Climacus who is not a Christian, Anti-Climacus who stands above the author. **Qualification.** ⚠️ The method is deliberately self-limiting: Climacus revokes the *Postscript* at its close, so the thesis cannot be asserted directly even by its own author, and *The Point of View*, written 1848 and published posthumously in 1859, is a retrospective self-reading rather than contemporaneous evidence of intention.

**EXAM LINK.** This session upgrades **2020 Q3(a)**, **2022 Q4(a)**, **2023 Q1(d)** and **2024 Q1(e)** from competent to distinctive: each is a subjectivity or anti-Hegel question, and each rewards the observation that the doctrine dictates the form. The attribution rule alone — writing "Climacus argues" rather than "Kierkegaard says" — is visible expertise in the first line.

**UPSC TRAPS.**

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| Treating pseudonymity as biography or shyness | ✅ It is the **formal counterpart** of the doctrine; the pseudonyms are **existence-possibilities**. |
| Citing pseudonymous works under Kierkegaard's own name | ⚠️ The "**First and Last Declaration**" (1846) asks for the pseudonym; ignoring it discards a free precision mark. |
| Omitting the **double authorship** | ✅ The signed *Upbuilding Discourses* are the direct pole; without them the method looks like evasion. |
| Using *The Point of View* as contemporaneous evidence | ⚠️ **Written 1848, published posthumously 1859**; a retrospective self-interpretation, and disputed. |
| Missing the **revocation** of the *Postscript* | ❓ It is the strongest evidence that the self-undermining objection was anticipated, not overlooked. |
| Expounding *Tractatus* 6.54 or the Unhappy Consciousness | ⚠️ One clause of cross-link each; they belong to Topics 06 and 05. |

**REVISION NOTES.**

- **Promoted to Core, 15 Aug 2026** — marks-essential for subjectivity and anti-Hegel questions.
- Six steps: objective truth is transmissible → existential truth is a **mode of existing** → direct transmission **falsifies** it → therefore **indirect communication** → **Socratic maieutics**, "deceives into the truth", **vanishing occasion** → therefore **pseudonymity**.
- Pseudonyms: **Victor Eremita** · **Johannes de Silentio** · **Constantin Constantius** · **Vigilius Haufniensis** · **Johannes Climacus** · **Anti-Climacus**.
- **Double authorship**: signed *Upbuilding Discourses* run beside the pseudonymous books.
- **"First and Last Declaration"**, *Postscript* **1846**: "author's author", **prompter** (*souffleur*), cite the pseudonym.
- Objections: unattributability (reply: signed pole + *Point of View*, itself disputed) · self-undermining (reply: Climacus **revokes** the *Postscript*) · Hegelian sublation (counter: no system contains its builder).

**CLOSING RECALL FLOW.**

```text
   EXISTENTIAL TRUTH IS A MODE OF EXISTING -> DIRECT TELLING TURNS IT
   INTO A RESULT AND FALSIFIES IT -> SO THE FORM MUST WITHHOLD THE
   RESULT -> SOCRATIC MIDWIFERY, 'deceives into the truth', the author
   VANISHES -> PSEUDONYMS AS EXISTENCE-POSSIBILITIES (Eremita, de
   Silentio, Constantius, Haufniensis, Climacus, Anti-Climacus) ->
   DOUBLE AUTHORSHIP with the SIGNED discourses -> 'FIRST AND LAST
   DECLARATION' 1846: cite the pseudonym -> and the POSTSCRIPT REVOKES
   ITSELF, which is the cost the method intends
```

### SESSION 6 — SARTRE: "EXISTENCE PRECEDES ESSENCE", THE PAPER-KNIFE, AND WHY HEIDEGGER REPUDIATED THE SLOGAN

**DEFINITION.** *Plain language:* a paper-knife exists because someone first had a design for it; Sartre says a human being is the one thing that turns up first and gets defined afterwards, because there is no designer. *Technical:* **existence precedes essence** is the thesis that, for the human being alone, there is no antecedent nature or blueprint that fixes what it is to be; a person **first exists, encounters himself, surges up in the world — and defines himself afterwards**, so that what he is is constituted through the project he takes up in situation.

**ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING.** ⚠️ *"Sartre's slogan is an argument, not a motto: if there is no divine artisan then there is no antecedent design for the human being, and everything the lecture goes on to claim about freedom, anguish and responsibility is an inference from that single missing premise."*

**MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS.** existence precedes essence · paper-knife (*coupe-papier*) · artisan and design · atheism as a **premise**, not a conclusion · "man is nothing else but what he makes of himself" · facticity as constraint · *Existentialism Is a Humanism*, lecture October **1945**, published **1946** · "Letter on Humanism", written 1946, published **1947** · "the essence of Dasein lies in its existence" (*Being and Time* §9) · metaphysics of subjectivity · shepherd of Being.

**Plain-language visual — the idea before the vocabulary.**

```text
   THE PAPER-KNIFE                       THE HUMAN BEING (Sartre)

   1 The artisan has a CONCEPT:          1 There is NO artisan, because
     what it is for, how it cuts.          there is no God.
   2 He then MAKES it.                   2 So there is no prior concept
   3 So ESSENCE (design/function)          of 'human nature' waiting to
     comes BEFORE EXISTENCE.               be filled in.
                                         3 The person turns up FIRST -
   Traditional theism said MAN was         and is defined AFTERWARDS,
   like this: God conceives human          by what he makes of himself.
   nature, then creates individuals
   to that blueprint.                    So EXISTENCE PRECEDES ESSENCE.
```

*What this shows: the slogan is the conclusion of a two-line argument whose premise is the denial of the artisan. State the premise and the slogan stops being a slogan.*

**🖼️ VISUAL — the argument, its provenance, and the two other thinkers' positions on it.**

```text
   slogan.  The correct answer NAMES ITS OWNER and then shows that the
   other two printed thinkers do NOT hold it - one repudiating it, one
   never having encountered it.
```

*Caption: the right-hand panel is the whole reason 2024 Q4(a) is a twenty-mark question rather than a definition. The paper attributes the slogan to "the existentialist thinkers"; a prepared answer corrects the attribution while still answering the question that was asked.*

**NUMBERED TEACHING.**

1. **The paper-knife (*coupe-papier*).** ✅ For a manufactured object the artisan first conceives its design and function, and *then* produces it: essence precedes existence. Traditional philosophy and theism treated *man* analogously — God conceives "human nature" and then creates individuals to that blueprint.
2. **Sartre's denial, and the exact place of atheism.** ✅ There is no God; therefore there is no prior design. ⚠️ **Atheism is a premise, not a conclusion.** Sartre does not argue from existentialism to atheism; he argues from atheism to the slogan. This is why the slogan in its Sartrean form is **not definitional of existentialism as such** — Kierkegaard is an existentialist *and* a theist.
3. **The formula.** ✅ "Man first of all exists, encounters himself, surges up in the world — and defines himself afterwards." The consequence for the **for-itself** (*pour-soi*) is that no predetermined blueprint exhausts human reality.
4. **The qualification that separates a good script from a slogan-repeating one.** ⚠️ Biology, history and society form **facticity** and constraint; they do not by themselves settle the meaning or project one takes up. "Man is nothing else but what he makes of himself" belongs to the **1946 lecture** and must be **balanced by situation**. The thesis denies an antecedent essence; it does not deny that circumstances limit what is practically possible.
5. **Provenance — a free precision mark.** ✅ *Existentialism Is a Humanism* (*L'existentialisme est un humanisme*) was **delivered as a public lecture in October 1945 and published in 1946**. The slogans — "existence precedes essence", "condemned to be free", "man is nothing else but what he makes of himself" — come from **there**, not from *Being and Nothingness* (**1943**). ⚠️ Sartre later regretted publishing the lecture; say so in a clause and the examiner sees a reader rather than a repeater.
6. **Heidegger's repudiation.** ✅ In the "**Letter on Humanism**" (written **1946**, published **1947**) Heidegger explicitly disowns Sartre's reading. His own sentence — "**the essence of Dasein lies in its existence**", *Being and Time* **§9** — does **not** mean "existence precedes essence" in Sartre's voluntarist sense. It is an **ontological** claim: Dasein's "what" is not a fixed *quidditas* but its very **manner of being** — its being-open-to-Being, its *Existenz*, its standing-out into the clearing of Being.
7. **The charge behind the repudiation.** ⚠️ Sartre, Heidegger holds, remains trapped in the **metaphysics of subjectivity**: he merely **reverses** the essence/existence priority while keeping the humanistic framework with man at the centre. For Heidegger the question is not man's self-creation but **Being** — man is the "**shepherd of Being**", not the lord of beings.
8. **Kierkegaard's very different refusal.** ✅ Kierkegaard never wrote the slogan and could not have accepted its premise. His refusal of an antecedent essence takes the form of the **existing individual** who cannot be completed inside a system (Session 2) — a refusal made *within* a God-relation, not against it.
9. **What the three share, stated exactly.** ⚠️ All three deny that a general essence settles in advance what it is for this individual to exist. **How** they deny it differs: Sartre by removing the designer, Heidegger by reconceiving what "essence" means for the entity whose being is at issue, Kierkegaard by insisting that existing is a task rather than a classification. That sentence is the correct core of any 2024 Q4(a) answer.
10. **The standing objection.** ❓ If there are no antecedent values, what makes one project better than another? Sartre's answer — that the *engagement* of choice generates its own criteria — is charged with circularity and with **decisionism** (Iris Murdoch, *The Sovereignty of Good*; Marxist critics). ⚠️ The honest verdict: the doctrine foregrounds project and responsibility but leaves value-criteria and situated constraint underdeveloped.

**HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification.**

> **Claim.** "Existence precedes essence" is Sartre's thesis and Sartre's alone among the three printed thinkers, and it is the conclusion of an argument whose decisive premise is the denial of a divine artisan. **Named evidence.** In the lecture *Existentialism Is a Humanism*, delivered in October 1945 and published in 1946, Sartre argues from the paper-knife (*coupe-papier*), whose design precedes its manufacture, to the claim that since there is no God there is no antecedent concept of human nature, so that man "first of all exists, encounters himself, surges up in the world — and defines himself afterwards"; Heidegger, in the "Letter on Humanism", written 1946 and published 1947, rejects the formula and insists that his own sentence at *Being and Time* §9, that the essence of Dasein lies in its existence, is an ontological claim about Dasein's manner of being rather than a humanistic inversion. **Analysis.** The two positions are not variants of one doctrine: Sartre reverses the traditional priority while keeping man at the centre, which is exactly what Heidegger calls the metaphysics of subjectivity, and Kierkegaard, who died in 1855, refuses antecedent essence in a third way — by treating existing as a task before God that no system can complete. **Qualification.** ⚠️ Even in Sartre the slogan must be balanced by facticity: biology, history and social position constrain what can be done, and the lecture's formula that man is nothing but what he makes of himself is a rhetorical compression of a doctrine that elsewhere insists freedom is exercised only in situation.

**EXAM LINK.** This session is the whole of **2024 Q4(a)** and the opening of **2020 Q4(a)**; it also supplies the fence sentence that any comparison question needs, and the provenance line that upgrades every Sartre answer in the clause.

**UPSC TRAPS.**

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| Attributing the slogan to all three thinkers | ✅ It is **Sartre's**. Heidegger **repudiated** it in 1946/47; Kierkegaard died in **1855**. |
| Sourcing the slogan to *Being and Nothingness* | ✅ It is from the lecture of **October 1945**, published **1946**. *Being and Nothingness* is **1943**. |
| Treating atheism as a conclusion of the argument | ✅ It is a **premise**. The argument runs *from* the absence of a designer *to* the absence of a blueprint. |
| Reading the slogan as "circumstances do not matter" | ⚠️ **Facticity** constrains. The thesis denies an antecedent essence, not situation. |
| Quoting §9 as Heideggerian agreement with Sartre | ✅ "The essence of Dasein lies in its existence" is **ontological**; Heidegger denies the humanist reading in the same breath. |
| Ignoring the value-criteria objection | ❓ Decisionism is the strongest standing objection; name it and rule on it. |

**REVISION NOTES.**

- Argument: artisan → design → object; **no God** → **no blueprint** → existence first, essence afterwards.
- Example: **paper-knife** (*coupe-papier*). Formula: "**man first of all exists … and defines himself afterwards**".
- **Atheism is a premise**, not a conclusion.
- Provenance: lecture **Oct 1945**, published **1946**; *Being and Nothingness* is **1943**; Sartre **regretted** publishing the lecture.
- Heidegger: "**Letter on Humanism**" written **1946**, published **1947**; §9 is **ontological**; Sartre stays in the **metaphysics of subjectivity**; man is the **shepherd of Being**.
- Kierkegaard: never wrote it; refuses antecedent essence as the **existing individual** before God.
- Objection: **decisionism** / no value-criteria (Murdoch, Marxists); verdict — powerful on responsibility, thin on criteria.

**CLOSING RECALL FLOW.**

```text
   PAPER-KNIFE: design BEFORE object -> theism said MAN is like that ->
   SARTRE'S PREMISE: THERE IS NO GOD -> so NO blueprint -> EXISTENCE
   PRECEDES ESSENCE (lecture Oct 1945, published 1946) -> but FACTICITY
   still constrains -> HEIDEGGER REPUDIATES (Letter on Humanism, 1946/
   47): Section 9 is ONTOLOGICAL, Sartre only REVERSES metaphysics ->
   KIERKEGAARD never wrote it and could not accept the premise ->
   SHARED REFUSAL, THREE DIFFERENT REASONS
```

### SESSION 7 — SARTRE: BEING-IN-ITSELF AND BEING-FOR-ITSELF, NIHILATION, AND "CONSCIOUSNESS IS WHAT IT IS NOT AND IS NOT WHAT IT IS"

**DEFINITION.** *Plain language:* a stone simply is what it is; consciousness never simply is anything, because it is always standing back from what it is and reaching towards what it is not yet — and that gap is what Sartre calls nothingness. *Technical:* **being-in-itself** (*en-soi*) is being that is full, solid, self-identical and opaque — "it is what it is"; **being-for-itself** (*pour-soi*) is consciousness as **nihilating** and self-surpassing — "it is what it is not and is not what it is"; the for-itself is "the being by which **nothingness** (*néant*) comes into the world", and its structural desire to become self-identical while remaining conscious — to be a *pour-soi-en-soi*, which is God — is contradictory.

**ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING.** ⚠️ *"Sartre's formula that consciousness is what it is not and is not what it is looks like a paradox and is in fact a definition: it says that the for-itself is constituted by a gap it can never close, and every later doctrine — freedom, bad faith, temporality, the useless passion — is an application of that single structural claim."*

**MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS.** being-in-itself (*en-soi*) · being-for-itself (*pour-soi*) · self-identity against non-self-identity · nihilation (*néantisation*) · nothingness (*néant*) · "de trop" (superfluous, in excess) · project · the impossible *pour-soi-en-soi* · "man is a useless passion" · ontological, not mind–matter.

**Plain-language visual — the idea before the vocabulary.**

```text
   A STONE                                 A WAITER

   It is a stone.  Fully.  With no         He IS a waiter (job, uniform,
   distance from itself.  Nothing          history) - and simultaneously
   in it stands back and asks              IS NOT a waiter, because he
   whether it should be a stone.           can question it, resent it,
                                           quit tomorrow, imagine
   IT IS WHAT IT IS.                       otherwise.

                                           HE IS WHAT HE IS NOT
                                           AND IS NOT WHAT HE IS.
```

*What this shows: the formula is not word-play. It marks the difference between a being with no internal distance and a being that is nothing but internal distance.*

**🖼️ VISUAL — the two regions of being, the two halves of the formula, and the impossible synthesis.**

```text
   +----------------------+--------------------------------------------+
   |                      | EN-SOI  (being-in-itself)                  |
   +----------------------+--------------------------------------------+
   | NATURE               | full, solid, SELF-IDENTICAL, opaque        |
   | FORMULA              | 'IT IS WHAT IT IS'                         |
   | EXAMPLES             | a stone, a table - they simply ARE         |
   | FREEDOM              | none: complete, determined                 |
   | TEMPORALITY          | not defined by projective temporal         |
   |                      | transcendence                              |
   | CONTINGENCY          | groundless, brute - 'DE TROP', in excess   |
   +----------------------+--------------------------------------------+
   |                      | POUR-SOI  (being-for-itself)               |
   +----------------------+--------------------------------------------+
   | NATURE               | NIHILATING, self-surpassing, NEVER self-   |
   |                      | identical                                  |
   | FORMULA              | 'IT IS WHAT IT IS NOT AND IS NOT WHAT IT   |
   |                      | IS'                                        |
   | EXAMPLE              | consciousness - always BEYOND itself,      |
   |                      | intending what it is not, negating what    |
   |                      | it is                                      |
   | FREEDOM              | structurally inescapable, yet SITUATED and |
   |                      | FACTICAL                                   |
   | TEMPORALITY          | ECSTATIC - related to past facticity and   |
   |                      | future project                             |
   | DESIRE               | to BECOME en-soi while staying conscious   |
   +----------------------+--------------------------------------------+
                                   |
                                   v
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   |   THE TWO HALVES OF THE FORMULA - UNPACK BOTH, ALWAYS              |
   |                                                                    |
   |   'IS WHAT IT IS NOT'        consciousness is always AHEAD of      |
   |                              itself, projecting toward possibil-   |
   |                              ities it does not yet possess.  It    |
   |                              IS (is defined by) its FUTURE - which |
   |                              does not yet exist                    |
   |                                                                    |
   |   'IS NOT WHAT IT IS'        consciousness can never COINCIDE with |
   |                              its past or its facticity.  The       |
   |                              waiter IS a waiter (facticity) and    |
   |                              simultaneously IS NOT one (he         |
   |                              transcends the role, is free to quit) |
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
                                   |
                                   v
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   |   HOW IS THIS POSSIBLE?  NIHILATION (neantisation)                 |
   |   Consciousness introduces NOTHINGNESS (neant) into being: it      |
   |   stands back from what is and NEGATES it - questions it,          |
   |   imagines alternatives, projects beyond it.  The pour-soi is      |
   |   'THE BEING BY WHICH NOTHINGNESS COMES INTO THE WORLD'.           |
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
                                   |
                                   v
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   |   THE IMPOSSIBLE SYNTHESIS                                         |
   |   The pour-soi PERPETUALLY DESIRES to become en-soi - to achieve   |
   |   full self-identity WHILE REMAINING CONSCIOUS.  That would be a   |
   |   POUR-SOI-EN-SOI = GOD, and it is a CONTRADICTION.  Human reality |
   |   is defined by this impossible desire: hence 'MAN IS A USELESS    |
   |   PASSION'.                                                        |
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
```

*Caption: read the two-column table first and the formula becomes readable; read the bottom two panels and it becomes an argument. Nihilation explains **how** the gap is possible; the impossible synthesis explains **why** it can never be closed.*

**NUMBERED TEACHING.**

1. **The distinction is ontological, not psychological or physical.** ⚠️ *En-soi* is **not** "matter" and *pour-soi* is **not** "mind". The contrast is between **being that is what it is** (self-identical) and **being that is not what it is** (nihilating). ✅ This correction is a standing UPSC trap and belongs in the first three lines of any answer on the pair.
2. **The in-itself.** ✅ Full, solid, self-identical, opaque; it *simply is*. It is **contingent** and groundless — "**de trop**", in excess — which is the ontological root of the experience Sartre calls nausea.
3. **The for-itself.** ✅ Consciousness, which is always *beyond* itself: intending what it is not, negating what it is, never coinciding with itself.
4. **"Is what it is not."** ✅ Consciousness is always **ahead of itself**, projecting toward possibilities it does not yet possess; it *is* — in the sense of being defined by — its **future**, which does not yet exist.
5. **"Is not what it is."** ✅ Consciousness can never coincide with its **past** or its **facticity**. The waiter *is* a waiter (facticity) and simultaneously *is not* a waiter (he transcends the role and is free to quit).
6. **Nihilation (*néantisation*) is the mechanism.** ✅ Non-self-identity is possible because consciousness introduces **nothingness** (*néant*) into being: it stands back from what is and negates it — questions it, imagines alternatives, projects beyond it. The for-itself is "the being by which nothingness comes into the world." ⚠️ Say *how*, not just *that*: the question form, the imagined alternative and the projected possibility are the three concrete faces of nihilation.
7. **The impossible synthesis and the "useless passion".** ✅ The for-itself perpetually desires to become **en-soi** — to achieve full self-identity *while remaining conscious*. That would be a **pour-soi-en-soi**, which is what "God" would have to mean, and it is a contradiction. Human reality is defined by this impossible desire; hence Sartre's formula that man is a **useless passion**.
8. **What the formula grounds.** ⚠️ This single structure generates the rest of the system: **freedom** (nothing in me is fixed enough to determine me), **bad faith** (the attempt to be self-identical anyway), **temporality** (the for-itself exists as past-facticity and future-project), and the **desire to be God**. Saying this explicitly is what turns 2023 Q3(c) from an exposition into an argument.
9. **The standing objection.** ❓ Is "non-self-identity" coherent, or a contradiction dressed in phenomenological language? ⚠️ The reply is that the claim is not that consciousness violates the law of identity but that consciousness is not the kind of thing that has a completed nature to be identical with — it exists as a relation to its own possibilities. Whether this reply succeeds is contested, and the honest closer says so.

**HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification.**

> **Claim.** Sartre's formula that consciousness is what it is not and is not what it is states the defining structure of the for-itself: a being constituted by an internal distance from itself that it cannot close. **Named evidence.** *Being and Nothingness* (1943) divides being into the in-itself, which is full, self-identical and contingent — "de trop" — and the for-itself, which is nihilating and self-surpassing; the waiter who is a waiter in his facticity and is not one in his transcendence is Sartre's own illustration of the second half of the formula, and the first half is exhibited in the fact that consciousness is defined by a future that does not yet exist. **Analysis.** The two halves are made possible by nihilation (*néantisation*): consciousness introduces nothingness into being by standing back from what is, questioning it and projecting beyond it, which is why Sartre calls the for-itself the being by which nothingness comes into the world, and why the for-itself's desire to become self-identical while remaining conscious — to be a *pour-soi-en-soi*, that is, God — is a contradictory ideal that makes man a useless passion. **Qualification.** ❓ Whether non-self-identity is a coherent ontological category or a contradiction in phenomenological dress remains disputed; the reply that the for-itself has no completed nature with which to be identical is defensible but not decisive.

**EXAM LINK.** This session is the whole of **2025 Q1(c)** and the whole of **2023 Q3(c)**, and it supplies the ontology that **2019 Q4(c)** (freedom and determinism), **2021 Q4(b)** (choice) and **2018 Q1(d)** (bad faith and alienation) all presuppose. Five of the fourteen owned parts stand on this one distinction.

**UPSC TRAPS.**

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| *En-soi* = matter, *pour-soi* = mind | ✅ The distinction is **ontological**: self-identical being against nihilating being, not a mind–matter dualism. |
| Unpacking only one half of the formula | ✅ "Is what it is not" = the **future/project**; "is not what it is" = the **past/facticity**. Both halves, always. |
| Treating nihilation as destruction | ⚠️ It is the **introduction of nothingness**: questioning, imagining alternatives, projecting beyond the given. |
| Omitting the impossible synthesis | ✅ The *pour-soi-en-soi* is God and is contradictory; hence the "useless passion". Without it the account has no conclusion. |
| Forgetting contingency | ✅ The in-itself is "**de trop**" — groundless and in excess; this is the root of nausea. |
| Presenting non-self-identity as beyond criticism | ❓ Name the coherence objection and rule on it. |

**REVISION NOTES.**

- **En-soi**: full, self-identical, opaque, contingent (**de trop**) — "**it is what it is**".
- **Pour-soi**: nihilating, self-surpassing, never self-identical — "**it is what it is not and is not what it is**".
- Half 1 = ahead of itself, defined by a **future** that does not exist; Half 2 = cannot coincide with **past/facticity** (the waiter).
- **Nihilation** (*néantisation*): consciousness introduces **nothingness** (*néant*) — questions, imagines, projects.
- Impossible synthesis: **pour-soi-en-soi = God**; hence "**man is a useless passion**".
- Grounds freedom, bad faith, temporality, the desire to be God.
- Objection: is non-self-identity coherent? Reply: no completed nature to be identical with. **Contested.**

**CLOSING RECALL FLOW.**

```text
   TWO REGIONS OF BEING -> EN-SOI 'is what it is' (full, self-identical,
   DE TROP) | POUR-SOI 'is what it is not and is not what it is' ->
   HALF 1: ahead of itself, defined by a FUTURE that does not exist ->
   HALF 2: cannot coincide with its PAST/FACTICITY (the waiter) ->
   HOW? NIHILATION - consciousness puts NOTHINGNESS into being ->
   THEREFORE freedom, bad faith, ecstatic temporality -> AND the
   impossible desire to be POUR-SOI-EN-SOI (God) -> 'MAN IS A USELESS
   PASSION'
```

---

### SESSION 8 — SARTRE: RADICAL BUT SITUATED FREEDOM, CHOICE, RESPONSIBILITY, AND THE THREE MOODS

**DEFINITION.** *Plain language:* for Sartre freedom is not something you have, like a talent; it is what you are, so you cannot put it down — and even refusing to decide is a decision. *Technical:* the **for-itself** is *constituted* by freedom: it is the nihilating, self-transcending movement that continually surpasses what it is toward what it is not yet. Freedom is therefore **structurally inescapable** but exercised only **in situation**; **anguish** (*angoisse*), **abandonment** (*délaissement*) and **despair** (*désespoir*) are the three moods in which this structure is lived.

**ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING.** ⚠️ *"Sartre's claim that we are condemned to be free is not rhetoric about the burden of choice but a structural thesis: freedom is not a capacity the for-itself possesses and might set aside, it is what the for-itself is — which is why the attempt to escape choosing is itself a choice, and typically a choice of bad faith."*

**MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS.** freedom is what we **are**, not what we have · "man is condemned to be free" · non-choice is choice · situation and facticity · anguish (*angoisse*) · abandonment (*délaissement*) · despair (*désespoir*) · "in choosing myself, I choose man" · quasi-Kantian universalisability · radical responsibility.

**Plain-language visual — the idea before the vocabulary.**

```text
   'I'LL DECIDE LATER.'                  SARTRE'S REPLY

   Deferral feels like NOT               Deferring IS deciding - to let
   choosing.  So does going              circumstances, or other people,
   along with what everyone              or the passage of time settle
   does.  So does 'having no             the matter.  The outcome you
   choice'.                              did not choose is the outcome
                                         your non-choice produced.
                                                     |
                                                     v
                                         And pretending otherwise -
                                         'I had no option' - is the
                                         standard form of BAD FAITH.
```

*What this shows: the doctrine is not that every option is open. It is that no stance towards a situation is stance-free.*

**🖼️ VISUAL — freedom, its three moods, and the two boundaries that must be stated.**

```text

   |   pour-soi IS the nihilating, self-transcending movement that      |
   |   continually surpasses what it is toward what it is NOT YET.      |
   |   'MAN IS CONDEMNED TO BE FREE' (lecture 1945, published 1946):    |
   |   we did not choose to exist, yet cannot escape taking up a        |
   |   stance within situations.                                        |
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
                                   |
              +--------------------+--------------------+
              v                                         v
   +----------------------------+       +--------------------------------+
   | NON-CHOICE IS CHOICE       |       | CHOICE LEGISLATES              |
   | 'I can always choose, but  |       | 'In choosing myself, I choose  |
   | I ought to know that if I  |       | man.'  Every choice implies an |
   | do not choose, I am still  |       | image of what human beings     |
   | choosing.'                 |       | OUGHT to be.  Quasi-KANTIAN    |

   | conformity are CHOICES -   |       | This is where the WEIGHT of    |
   | and the attempt to escape  |       | responsibility, and the        |

   | (typically BAD FAITH)      |       |                                |
   +----------------------------+       +--------------------------------+
                                   |
                                   v
   +----------------+--------------------------+-----------------------+
   | MOOD           | SOURCE                   | STRUCTURE             |
   +----------------+--------------------------+-----------------------+
   | ANGUISH        | I recognise that I am    | NOT fear OF an object |
   | (angoisse)     | FREE - my future is      | but VERTIGO before my |
   |                | radically undetermined;  | own freedom: 'I am    |
   |                | nothing compels me; I    | not sustained by      |
   |                | stand before the abyss   | anything, nothing     |
   |                | of possibility           | justifies me'         |
   +----------------+--------------------------+-----------------------+
   | ABANDONMENT    | There is no God, no      | I must INVENT values  |
   | (delaissement) | cosmic moral order, no   | through my choosing;  |
   |                | a priori values written  | I cannot lean on a    |
   |                | in an 'intelligible      | pre-given ethical     |
   |                | heaven'                  | framework             |
   +----------------+--------------------------+-----------------------+
   | DESPAIR        | I should not rely on     | Sartre's LECTURE-TERM |
   | (desespoir)    | outcomes beyond my       | - it differs entirely |
   |                | action and reasonable    | from KIERKEGAARD's    |
   |                | probabilities            | theological despair   |
   +----------------+--------------------------+-----------------------+

                                   v
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   |   TWO BOUNDARIES THAT MUST BE STATED OR THE ANSWER IS WRONG        |
   |   1  FREEDOM IS SITUATED.  Facticity - body, past, class, place -  |
   |      limits practical possibilities.  Early Sartre calls freedom   |
   |      'absolute' in the STRUCTURAL sense that the for-itself cannot |
   |      become a completed thing.  THAT IS NOT OMNIPOTENCE.           |
   |   2  'THERE ARE NO EXCUSES' means responsibility for TAKING UP     |
   |      motives and situations.  It must NOT become the claim that    |
   |      trauma, illness, coercion or affect can be dismissed at will. |

```

*Caption: the bottom panel is the difference between a script that has understood Sartre and one that has caricatured him. Sartre's freedom is unlimited in *kind* — there is no situation in which the for-itself becomes a thing — and severely limited in *scope*.*

**NUMBERED TEACHING.**

1. **Freedom is constitutive, not possessed.** ✅ The *pour-soi* is *constituted* by freedom: it **is** the nihilating, self-transcending movement of consciousness that continually surpasses what it is toward what it is not yet. ⚠️ This is why Sartre cannot treat freedom as one human capacity among others that might be absent in some people or some conditions.
2. **"Man is condemned to be free."** ✅ From *Existentialism Is a Humanism* (lecture **1945**, published **1946**). We did not choose to exist, yet cannot escape taking up a stance within our situations. ⚠️ "Thrown" is **Heidegger's** term (*Geworfenheit*) and should not be silently made Sartre's own — a small attribution discipline that examiners notice.
3. **Non-choice is choice.** ✅ "I can always choose, but I ought to know that if I do not choose, I am still choosing." Inaction, deferral and conformity are themselves choices; the attempt to escape choice is already a choice, and typically a choice of **bad faith** (Session 9).
4. **Anguish (*angoisse*).** ✅ Not fear *of* an object but **vertigo before one's own freedom**: I recognise that my future is radically undetermined, that nothing compels me, and that "I am not sustained by anything, nothing justifies me." ⚠️ Session 17 gives the cliff-path and the gambler, which are the two examples that make this concrete.
5. **Abandonment (*délaissement*).** ✅ There is no God, no cosmic moral order and no *a priori* values written in an "intelligible heaven"; I must therefore **invent** values through my choosing and cannot lean on a pre-given ethical framework.
6. **Despair (*désespoir*).** ✅ In the lecture this means confining oneself to what lies within one's action and to reasonable probabilities, rather than relying on outcomes beyond one's control. ⚠️ It is **not** Kierkegaard's theological despair, and the two must be kept apart explicitly (Session 4).
7. **Universalisability: "in choosing myself, I choose man."** ✅ A quasi-Kantian principle: every choice implies an image of what human beings *ought* to be. If I choose to marry, I affirm that monogamy is valuable *for everyone*; I cannot choose "just for me" in moral isolation. This generates the **weight** of responsibility and the anguish that accompanies it — I am not merely deciding my own fate but **legislating** an image of humanity.
8. **The internal tension in universalisability.** ❓ It appears to conflict with the denial of *a priori* values: whence the universalisability demand if no prior moral framework is given? **Sartre's reply:** the *form* of universalisability is inherent in the **structure of free choice**, not imported from a pre-existing ethics. ⚠️ Kantians and communitarians press the point; Sartre concedes tension, and the posthumous *Notebooks for an Ethics* attempts a more substantive ethics but was never completed.
9. **Boundary 1 — situation.** ⚠️ Sartre does **not** deny that facticity limits practical possibilities. Early Sartre calls freedom "absolute" in the **structural** sense that the for-itself cannot become a completed thing: it must take up a stance. This is not omnipotence, and coercion, trauma and social structures can constrain both action and the intelligibility of alternatives.
10. **Boundary 2 — "there are no excuses".** ⚠️ The phrase expresses responsibility for **taking up** motives and situations. It must not be converted into the psychologically implausible claim that trauma, illness, coercion or affect can be dismissed at will. ✅ Stating this boundary is what allows an answer to be critical without being dismissive.

**HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification.**

> **Claim.** Sartre's freedom is inescapable in structure and constrained in scope, and the two claims are consistent only because freedom is what the for-itself is rather than something it has. **Named evidence.** In *Existentialism Is a Humanism* (lecture 1945, published 1946) Sartre writes that man is condemned to be free, that not choosing is still choosing, and that in choosing myself I choose man; the same lecture names the three moods in which this structure is lived — anguish before one's own undetermined future, abandonment in the absence of any *a priori* value written in an intelligible heaven, and despair in the sense of not relying on what lies beyond one's action. **Analysis.** Because the for-itself is the nihilating movement by which any given is surpassed, no situation converts it into a completed thing, which is why deferral and conformity are stances rather than absences of stance, and why responsibility extends to the meaning one confers on one's circumstances; the quasi-Kantian universalisability clause then supplies the weight that turns this structure into anguish, since each choice projects an image of humanity. **Qualification.** ⚠️ "Absolute" here is structural rather than practical: facticity limits what can be done, "there are no excuses" concerns the taking up of motives and not the dismissal of trauma or coercion, and Sartre himself broadened the account in the *Critique of Dialectical Reason* (1960) with scarcity, seriality and the practico-inert.

**EXAM LINK.** This session is the whole of **2021 Q4(b)** and the second half of **2024 Q4(a)**; it is the doctrinal base for **2019 Q4(c)** and **2020 Q4(a)** (Session 10), and it supplies the responsibility clause that a bad-faith answer (Session 9) needs in order to reach a verdict.

**UPSC TRAPS.**

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| "Sartrean freedom means constraints are unreal" | ⚠️ **Facticity constrains.** The early claim concerns how situations acquire meaning and are taken up. |
| Using "thrown" as Sartre's own term | ✅ *Geworfenheit* is **Heidegger's**. Sartre's parallel is that we did not choose to exist yet must take a stance. |
| Merging Sartre's *désespoir* with Kierkegaard's despair | ✅ Lecture-term about outcomes against a theological **misrelation**. Two doctrines. |
| Reading "no excuses" as a denial of trauma or coercion | ⚠️ It concerns responsibility for **taking up** motives; the stronger reading is the caricature critics attack. |
| Dropping the universalisability clause | ✅ "In choosing myself, I choose man" is where the **weight** of responsibility and the anguish come from. |
| Presenting universalisability as untroubled | ❓ It sits uneasily with the denial of objective values; name the tension and Sartre's structural reply. |

**REVISION NOTES.**

- Freedom is **what the for-itself is**, not what it has; the nihilating surpassing of any given.
- "**Man is condemned to be free**" — lecture **1945**, published **1946**. "Thrown" is **Heidegger's** word.
- **Non-choice is choice**; the attempt to escape choice is a choice, usually **bad faith**.
- Three moods: **anguish** (vertigo before my own freedom) · **abandonment** (no *a priori* values) · **despair** (do not rely on what is beyond my action).
- "**In choosing myself, I choose man**" — quasi-Kantian universalisability; source of the **weight** and the anguish.
- Tension: universalisability against the denial of objective values; reply = form inherent in free choice; *Notebooks for an Ethics* unfinished.
- Two boundaries: freedom is **situated**; "**no excuses**" = responsibility for taking up motives, not denial of coercion.

**CLOSING RECALL FLOW.**

```text
   FREEDOM IS WHAT THE FOR-ITSELF IS -> 'CONDEMNED TO BE FREE' (1945/46)
   -> NON-CHOICE IS CHOICE -> lived as ANGUISH (vertigo before my
   freedom) + ABANDONMENT (no a priori values) + DESPAIR (no reliance on
   outcomes) -> weighted by 'IN CHOOSING MYSELF I CHOOSE MAN' ->
   BUT FREEDOM IS SITUATED (facticity limits) AND 'NO EXCUSES' MEANS
   RESPONSIBILITY FOR TAKING UP MOTIVES, NOT DENIAL OF COERCION
```

### SESSION 9 — SARTRE: FACTICITY AND TRANSCENDENCE, BAD FAITH, AND THE ROUTE FROM INAUTHENTICITY TO ALIENATION

**DEFINITION.** *Plain language:* bad faith is not ordinary lying; it is the trick of treating yourself as a fixed thing so that you do not have to answer for what you do — or, in the opposite direction, of pretending your situation places no limits on you at all. *Technical:* **bad faith** (*mauvaise foi*) is a form of self-deception in which the for-itself flees its freedom by identifying exclusively with **facticity** — the given: body, past, social position, situation — or flees its situation by identifying exclusively with **transcendence** — the freedom to negate facticity and project beyond what one presently is. Bad faith is the denial of the **tension** between the two, not the denial of either.

**ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING.** ⚠️ *"Bad faith is not the lie one tells another but the project of ceasing to be a question to oneself: by collapsing into facticity alone or into transcendence alone, the for-itself buys relief from the tension that constitutes it — and pays for that relief with the alienation of living as a thing among things."*

**MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS.** bad faith (*mauvaise foi*) · facticity (*facticité*) · transcendence · collapsing into one dimension · the waiter · the woman on the date · the double lie · the paradox of self-deception · pre-reflective, non-thetic awareness · the Freudian unconscious rejected · inauthenticity · alienation · the serious spirit.

**Plain-language visual — the idea before the vocabulary.**

```text
   EVERY PERSON HAS TWO SIDES AT ONCE

   FACTICITY                             TRANSCENDENCE
   what is GIVEN: my body, my            what SURPASSES it: my freedom
   past, my class, my job, my            to negate, question, project
   situation                             beyond what I presently am
        \                                          /
         \                                        /
          +------------------+------------------+
                             |
                   BAD FAITH = LIVE AS IF
                   ONLY ONE OF THEM WERE TRUE
                             |
          +------------------+------------------+
          v                                     v
   'I am just a waiter / just             'My past, my body, my
   this way / it is my nature.'           circumstances are nothing to
   -> FACTICITY ONLY.  No                 me.'
   responsibility, because no             -> TRANSCENDENCE ONLY.  No
   freedom.                               situation, so no real cost.
```

*What this shows: bad faith always has the same shape — one of the two dimensions is silently deleted. Naming which one is deleted is how an answer analyses an example instead of retelling it.*

**🖼️ VISUAL — the structure, the two examples, the paradox, and the route to alienation.**

```text
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   |   THE STRUCTURE                                                    |
   |   Human reality has TWO dimensions AT ONCE:                        |
   |     FACTICITY (facticite) - the given: body, past, social          |
   |       position, situation                                          |
   |     TRANSCENDENCE - the self-surpassing: freedom to NEGATE my      |
   |       facticity, to project myself beyond what I presently am      |
   |   BAD FAITH occurs when I identify EXCLUSIVELY WITH ONE dimension  |
   |   AT THE EXPENSE OF THE OTHER.                                     |
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
                                   |
       +---------------------------+---------------------------+
       v                                                       v
   +-------------------------------+     +----------------------------+
   | THE WAITER                    |     | THE WOMAN ON THE DATE      |
   | He performs the role with     |     | A man takes her hand; she  |
   | exaggerated, THING-LIKE       |     | leaves it there, 'not      |
   | precision - too exactly a     |     | noticing' - treating the   |
   | waiter to be one.             |     | hand as a mere physical    |
   |                               |     | thing.                     |
   | READ IT CAREFULLY: Sartre     |     | DOUBLE LIE:                |
   | reads this as DENIAL that a   |     | (i) denies her TRANSCEND-  |
   | social role EXHAUSTS the for- |     |     ENCE (her freedom to   |
   | itself.  Role-performance     |     |     accept or refuse) by   |
   | ALONE is NOT AUTOMATICALLY    |     |     reducing herself to    |
   | bad faith - the analysis is   |     |     pure FACTICITY (a      |
   | about the CLAIM to BE the     |     |     body-object)           |
   | role, not about doing a job   |     | (ii) denies the FACTICITY  |
   | well.                         |     |     of the gesture (its    |
   |                               |     |     meaning) by treating   |
   |                               |     |     it as neutral          |
   +-------------------------------+     +----------------------------+
                                   |
                                   v
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   |   THE PARADOX OF SELF-DECEPTION                                    |
   |   To deceive MYSELF I must SIMULTANEOUSLY                          |
   |     KNOW the truth (in order to conceal it)                        |
   |     NOT KNOW it (in order to be deceived)                          |
   |   Sartre REJECTS the Freudian unconscious, so he cannot split the  |
   |   person into a deceiving censor and a deceived subject.           |
   |                                                                    |
   |   SARTRE'S SOLUTION: consciousness is NON-SELF-IDENTICAL - the     |
   |   pour-soi 'is what it is not and is not what it is' - so it can   |
   |   hold truth and denial in a SINGLE PRE-REFLECTIVE stance, aware   |
   |   NON-THETICALLY without reflectively AVOWING.  No two persons     |
   |   are required.                                                    |
   |   CONTESTED: Fingarette (Self-Deception) and analytic philosophy   |
   |   of mind press whether this is coherent.                          |
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
                                   |
                                   v
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   |   THE ROUTE THE 2018 PAPER ASKS FOR                                 |
   |   BAD FAITH (project of fleeing the tension)                        |
   |        -> INAUTHENTICITY (consciousness denies its own unstable     |
   |           freedom and lives as if settled)                          |
   |        -> ALIENATION (the self lives THROUGH fixed social roles,    |
   |           or through the OBJECTIFYING LOOK of others, and so is     |
   |           related to itself as to a thing)                          |
   |   The three are NOT synonyms: bad faith is the PROJECT,             |
   |   inauthenticity the RESULTING MODE, alienation the COST.           |
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
```

*Caption: the bottom panel is the exact chain 2018 Q1(d) asks for. Answering it well means showing that the three terms are ordered — a project, a mode and a cost — rather than three names for one idea.*

**NUMBERED TEACHING.**

1. **Define bad faith by its structure, not by "lying to oneself".** ✅ It is a flight from freedom achieved by pretending to be a fixed *thing* (*en-soi*) — or, conversely, a flight from facticity achieved by pretending to be pure transcendence. ⚠️ The doctrine is the **collapse of the tension**, and an answer that stops at "self-deception" has not stated it.
2. **Facticity and transcendence.** ✅ **Facticity** is the given — body, past, social position, situation. **Transcendence** is the self-surpassing — the freedom to negate facticity and project beyond what one presently is. Both are always present; bad faith deletes one.
3. **The waiter, read accurately.** ✅ The waiter performs his role with exaggerated, thing-like precision. ⚠️ **Sartre's point is the denial that a social role exhausts the for-itself; role-performance alone is not automatically bad faith.** The bad faith lies in the claim to *be* the role in the mode of the in-itself, not in doing the job well — a distinction most scripts miss.
4. **The woman on the date, and the double lie.** ✅ A man takes her hand; she leaves it there, "not noticing", treating the hand as a mere physical thing. She denies her **transcendence** — her freedom to accept or reject the advance — by reducing herself to pure **facticity**, a body-object; and simultaneously she denies the **facticity** of the gesture, its sexual meaning, by treating it as neutral. Two denials, in opposite directions, in one act.
5. **The paradox of self-deception.** ⚠️ To deceive myself I must *know* the truth in order to conceal it and *not know* it in order to be deceived. Sartre **rejects the Freudian unconscious**, so he cannot solve the paradox by splitting the person into a censor and a subject.
6. **Sartre's solution.** ✅ Consciousness is **non-self-identical** — the for-itself "is what it is not and is not what it is" (Session 7) — so it can hold truth and denial in a single **pre-reflective** stance: I am aware **non-thetically** without reflectively avowing. No two persons are required. ❓ Whether this is coherent remains debated; **Fingarette's** *Self-Deception* and analytic philosophy of mind press the point.
7. **The chain the 2018 paper asks for.** ✅ **Bad faith** is the *project* of treating oneself as only facticity or only transcendence in order to flee responsibility; **inauthenticity** follows because consciousness denies its own unstable freedom; **alienation** follows when the self lives through fixed social roles or through the objectifying look of others. ⚠️ Present them as ordered — project, mode, cost — not as synonyms.
8. **Why bad faith is not a rare pathology.** ⚠️ Because the tension it flees is constitutive, bad faith is a permanent temptation rather than an occasional vice; the "**serious spirit**" — treating values as though they were properties of the world rather than products of commitment — is its everyday social form.
9. **What Sartre does not supply.** ❓ *Being and Nothingness* gives a powerful critique of bad faith but **no developed positive doctrine of authenticity**; the promised ethics was never completed. ⚠️ Saying this is the correct closing qualification for any bad-faith answer, and it is also the honest reason Sartre's "authenticity" cannot be compared straightforwardly with Heidegger's *Eigentlichkeit*.
10. **The strongest external objection.** ❓ Radical freedom under-describes **gendered, racialised and material constraint** — a criticism pressed by **de Beauvoir**, **Fanon** and later feminist and postcolonial writers. ⚠️ Reply: situated freedom can appropriate facticity without being reducible to it, and later Sartre broadens social mediation — but the early vocabulary remains abstract, and that residue should be conceded.

**HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification.**

> **Claim.** Bad faith is best defined as the collapse of the tension between facticity and transcendence rather than as self-deception in general, and this definition is what makes Sartre's route from bad faith through inauthenticity to alienation intelligible. **Named evidence.** In *Being and Nothingness* (1943) Sartre analyses the waiter, whose exaggerated precision expresses the denial that a social role exhausts the for-itself, and the woman on the date, who leaves her hand in her companion's while "not noticing", thereby denying her own transcendence by reducing herself to a body-object and denying the facticity of the gesture by treating it as neutral. **Analysis.** Because the for-itself is constituted by the tension between what it is given as and what it projects, any project of living as though only one dimension were real must be a flight from that constitution, which is why bad faith is a permanent temptation rather than an occasional vice, and why its outcome is alienation: a self that lives through a fixed role or through another's objectifying gaze relates to itself as to a thing. **Qualification.** ❓ The account faces a live objection — since Sartre rejects the Freudian unconscious, self-deception must be lodged in a single pre-reflective, non-thetic awareness, and whether that is coherent is disputed by Fingarette and others; and *Being and Nothingness* offers no completed positive doctrine of authenticity to set against the critique.

**EXAM LINK.** This session is the whole of **2018 Q1(d)** (inauthenticity, bad faith, alienation), the second half of **2023 Q3(c)** (bad faith as the application of the non-self-identity formula), and the analytic core of **2020 Q4(a)** (Session 10) and **2021 Q4(b)** (Session 8), where evasion of choice is diagnosed as bad faith.

**UPSC TRAPS.**

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| Defining bad faith as "lying to oneself" | ✅ It is the **collapse of the facticity/transcendence tension** into one dimension. |
| Saying the waiter is in bad faith merely by performing his role | ⚠️ Sartre's point is the denial that the role **exhausts** the for-itself; role-performance alone is not automatically bad faith. |
| Giving only one half of the woman's double lie | ✅ Both: transcendence denied (reduced to a body-object) **and** facticity denied (the gesture treated as neutral). |
| Solving the paradox by invoking the unconscious | ✅ Sartre **rejects** it; the solution is **pre-reflective, non-thetic** awareness within a non-self-identical consciousness. |
| Treating bad faith, inauthenticity and alienation as synonyms | ✅ **Project → mode → cost.** The 2018 question is precisely about the chain. |
| Claiming Sartre supplies a full theory of authenticity | ❓ *Being and Nothingness* gives the critique of its opposite; the positive ethics was never completed. |

**REVISION NOTES.**

- **Bad faith** = flight from the **tension** by collapsing into **facticity** alone or **transcendence** alone.
- **Facticity** = body, past, social position, situation. **Transcendence** = freedom to negate and project.
- **Waiter** = denial that a role exhausts the for-itself (not "acting a role is bad faith").
- **Woman on the date** = **double lie** — denies her transcendence *and* the gesture's facticity.
- **Paradox**: must know and not know. **Unconscious rejected.** Solution = **pre-reflective, non-thetic** awareness; contested (Fingarette).
- 2018 chain: **bad faith (project) → inauthenticity (mode) → alienation (cost)**.
- Everyday form: the **serious spirit**. Residue: no completed positive authenticity; de Beauvoir/Fanon on constraint.

**CLOSING RECALL FLOW.**

```text
   TWO DIMENSIONS AT ONCE: FACTICITY + TRANSCENDENCE -> BAD FAITH =
   DELETE ONE -> WAITER (denies the role does not exhaust him) | WOMAN
   ON THE DATE (DOUBLE lie, both directions) -> PARADOX: know AND not
   know -> no Freudian unconscious, so PRE-REFLECTIVE NON-THETIC
   awareness in a NON-SELF-IDENTICAL consciousness -> BAD FAITH (project)
   -> INAUTHENTICITY (mode) -> ALIENATION (cost) -> and NO completed
   positive doctrine of authenticity in Being and Nothingness
```
