### SESSION 10 — SARTRE: FREEDOM OF CHOICE AND DETERMINISM, AND "YOU CAN ALWAYS MAKE SOMETHING OUT OF WHAT YOU HAVE BEEN MADE INTO"

**DEFINITION.** *Plain language:* Sartre's reply to determinism is not that causes do not exist but that a cause only becomes a *reason for acting* once I have given it that role — and giving it that role is something I do. *Technical:* causal, psychological and social explanations cannot convert the for-itself into a thing whose project is wholly fixed in advance, because a **motive** acquires action-guiding force only through the **meaning** conferred on it, and meaning-conferral is itself an act of the for-itself (nihilation and transcendence). Freedom is therefore exercised **in** situation, never **despite** it.

**ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING.** ⚠️ *"For Sartre the quarrel between freedom and determinism is a false problem, because determinism misdescribes the for-itself as an in-itself: it treats a being that must confer meaning on its own past as though it were a thing standing among causes."*

**MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS.** motive and meaning-conferral · nihilation as the condition of motivation · the for-itself is not a thing among causes · "you can always make something out of what you have been made into" · situation · "there are no excuses", qualified · practico-inert · scarcity · seriality · group-in-fusion · *Critique of Dialectical Reason* (1960).

**Plain-language visual — the idea before the vocabulary.**

```text
   THE DETERMINIST PICTURE               SARTRE'S CORRECTION

   Desire ---> pushes ---> Action        Desire is THERE.  But it moves
   like one billiard ball                me only as the desire I have
   striking another.                     TAKEN UP as a reason, inside
        |                                a project I am pursuing.
        v                                          |
   The person is a THING among                     v
   causes.                               Remove the project and the
                                         same desire motivates nothing.
                                         So the 'cause' had to pass
                                         through an act of MEANING.
```

*What this shows: the argument does not deny the desire. It denies that the desire can do the work without me.*

**🖼️ VISUAL — the argument, the 2020 sentence, and what later Sartre concedes.**

```text
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   |   THE KEY ARGUMENT (Being and Nothingness 1943)                    |
   |   1  A MOTIVE - a desire, an emotion, a situation - can become a   |
   |      REASON FOR ACTION only through the MEANING I confer on it.    |
   |   2  Meaning-conferral is itself an ACT of the pour-soi -          |
   |      nihilation, transcendence, projection.                        |
   |   3  THEREFORE no motive determines me UNLESS I FREELY CONSTITUTE  |
   |      IT AS A MOTIVE.                                               |
   |   4  THEREFORE determinism misdescribes the for-itself: it treats  |
   |      a nihilating being as an EN-SOI, a thing among causes.        |
   |      The 'problem' of freedom and determinism is a FALSE PROBLEM.  |
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
                                   |
                                   v
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   |   THE 2020 SENTENCE, UNPACKED                                      |
   |   'YOU CAN ALWAYS MAKE SOMETHING OUT OF WHAT YOU HAVE BEEN MADE    |
   |   INTO.'                                                           |
   |                                                                    |
   |   'WHAT YOU HAVE BEEN MADE INTO' = FACTICITY.  Sartre CONCEDES     |
   |     the making: class, body, upbringing, history, the gaze of      |
   |     others.  The sentence GRANTS the determinist his data.         |
   |   'YOU CAN ALWAYS MAKE SOMETHING OUT OF' = TRANSCENDENCE.  The     |
   |     for-itself must still take up that facticity in a project;     |
   |     it cannot receive it as a completed nature.                    |
   |   'ALWAYS' = the STRUCTURAL claim, not a promise of success.       |
   |     It says there is no situation in which the for-itself becomes  |
   |     a thing - NOT that every outcome is available.                 |
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
                                   |
                                   v
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   |   THE CRITICISM THAT LANDED, AND THE EVIDENCE THAT IT LANDED       |
   |   OBJECTION  Radical freedom is psychologically and socially       |
   |     implausible: slavery, torture, mental illness and structural   |
   |     oppression limit not only ACTION but the COGNITIVE range of    |
   |     alternatives.  (Merleau-Ponty, Adventures of the Dialectic;    |
   |     Marxist critics; de Beauvoir; Fanon)                           |
   |   FIRST REPLY  Distinguish freedom-of-CONSCIOUSNESS (I can always  |
   |     interpret) from freedom-of-ACTION (limited by situation).      |
   |     BUT this WEAKENS the radical thesis.                           |
   |   THE DECISIVE EVIDENCE  In the CRITIQUE OF DIALECTICAL REASON     |
   |     (1960) SARTRE HIMSELF integrates SCARCITY, SERIALITY and the   |
   |     PRACTICO-INERT, and introduces the GROUP-IN-FUSION.  That      |
   |     self-revision is the best evidence the objection landed.       |
   |   USE IT AS A CONCESSION, NEVER AS THE DOCTRINE OF 1943.           |
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
```

*Caption: the third panel is how to be critical without being crude. The strongest evidence against early Sartre is late Sartre — and citing the 1960 self-revision by name converts a generic "critics say" into an argument.*

**NUMBERED TEACHING.**

1. **State the position precisely.** ✅ In the early Sartrean account, causal, psychological and social explanations cannot convert the for-itself into a thing whose project is wholly fixed in advance. ⚠️ Note the wording: the claim is about *conversion into a thing*, not about the non-existence of causes.
2. **The key argument, in three steps.** ✅ A **motive** — desire, emotion, situation — becomes a reason for action only through the **meaning** I confer on it; meaning-conferral is itself an act of the for-itself (nihilation and transcendence); therefore **no motive determines me unless I freely constitute it as a motive**.
3. **Why the dispute is a false problem.** ✅ Determinism misunderstands the *pour-soi* by treating it as an *en-soi* — a thing among causes. The correct response is not to defeat determinism on its own ground but to show that its subject-matter has been misdescribed.
4. **The 2020 sentence, word by word.** ✅ "You can always make something out of what you have been made into." **"What you have been made into"** concedes facticity: class, body, upbringing, history, the gaze of others. **"You can always make something out of"** asserts transcendence: the for-itself must still take up that facticity in a project. **"Always"** is the **structural** claim — there is no situation in which the for-itself becomes a thing — and **not** a promise that every outcome is available. ⚠️ Unpacking the sentence in these three moves *is* the answer to 2020 Q4(a).
5. **"There are no excuses", qualified.** ⚠️ The phrase expresses responsibility for **taking up** motives and situations. It should not become the psychologically implausible claim that trauma, illness, coercion or affect can simply be dismissed at will.
6. **Situation and freedom.** ✅ Sartre does not deny that facticity limits practical possibilities. Early Sartre nevertheless calls freedom "absolute" in the **structural** sense that the for-itself cannot become a completed thing: it must take up a stance. ⚠️ This is not omnipotence — "I cannot fly, but I am free to interpret my inability and to project alternatives."
7. **The objection.** ❓ Radical freedom is psychologically and socially implausible: people under extreme oppression — slavery, torture, severe mental illness — are not "absolutely free", and structural oppression limits not merely action but the **cognitive** range of alternatives. Pressed by common sense, by the Marxist tradition (**Merleau-Ponty**, *Adventures of the Dialectic*), and by **de Beauvoir** and **Fanon** on gendered, racialised and material constraint.
8. **The first reply, and why it costs something.** ⚠️ Sartre distinguishes freedom of **consciousness** — always present, since I can always interpret — from freedom of **action**, limited by situation. ❓ This is a real reply, but it **weakens** the radical thesis, and an honest answer says so rather than presenting it as a clean defence.
9. **The decisive evidence.** ✅ In the *Critique of Dialectical Reason* (**1960**) Sartre himself integrates **scarcity**, **seriality** and the **practico-inert**, and introduces the **group-in-fusion** as a form of non-objectifying solidarity. ⚠️ Cite it as a **concession**, never as the doctrine of 1943 — the point is that the strongest objection is confirmed by the author's own revision.
10. **The executable verdict.** ⚠️ Freedom is the for-itself's non-coincidence with facticity, exercised only in situation; motives acquire action-guiding significance within a project rather than operating as external causes; and coercion, trauma and material structures genuinely pressure the early radicalism, which later scarcity and seriality broaden without simply retracting.

**HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification.**

> **Claim.** Sartre treats the opposition between freedom and determinism as a false problem, because determinism describes the for-itself in categories that belong to the in-itself. **Named evidence.** *Being and Nothingness* (1943) argues that a motive acquires action-guiding force only through the meaning conferred on it and that such conferral is itself an act of the for-itself, so that no motive determines unless it is freely constituted as a motive; the 1945 lecture's "there are no excuses" states the responsibility that follows; and the sentence "you can always make something out of what you have been made into" concedes the making — class, body, upbringing, the gaze of others — while insisting that what has been made must still be taken up. **Analysis.** The word "always" is doing structural rather than practical work: it denies that any situation converts the for-itself into a completed thing, and it does not assert that every outcome is available, which is why the doctrine is compatible with severe practical constraint and incompatible with the claim that a person is exhaustively explained by causes. **Qualification.** ❓ The objection from slavery, torture, mental illness and structural oppression is strong, the distinction between freedom of consciousness and freedom of action weakens the radical thesis in answering it, and Sartre's own *Critique of Dialectical Reason* (1960), with scarcity, seriality and the practico-inert, is the clearest evidence that the criticism landed.

**EXAM LINK.** This session is the whole of **2019 Q4(c)** and the whole of **2020 Q4(a)**; it also supplies the criticism paragraph for **2021 Q4(b)** and the constraint qualification that a well-judged **2024 Q4(a)** needs in its final third.

**UPSC TRAPS.**

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| Presenting Sartre as denying that causes exist | ✅ The claim is that a cause becomes a **motive** only through conferred **meaning**. |
| Reading "always" as "every outcome is available" | ⚠️ It is **structural**: no situation converts the for-itself into a thing. |
| Ignoring the first half of the 2020 sentence | ✅ "What you have been made into" **concedes facticity**; the answer must show the concession before the reply. |
| Presenting freedom-of-consciousness as a clean defence | ❓ It answers the objection but **weakens** the radical thesis. Say so. |
| Using the 1960 *Critique* as Sartre's standing doctrine | ⚠️ It is a **later concession**; the 1943 ontology is what the question asks about. |
| Dismissing the oppression objection as a misreading | ❓ Merleau-Ponty, de Beauvoir and Fanon press a real limitation; the residue must be conceded. |

**REVISION NOTES.**

- Argument: motive → **meaning-conferral** → an act of the for-itself → no motive determines unless **constituted** as one.
- Determinism misdescribes the *pour-soi* as an *en-soi*: the problem is **false**, not merely mistaken.
- 2020 sentence: **facticity conceded** + **transcendence asserted** + "**always**" is **structural**.
- "**No excuses**" = responsibility for taking up motives; not the denial of trauma or coercion.
- Objection: oppression limits action **and** the cognitive range (Merleau-Ponty, Marxists, de Beauvoir, Fanon).
- Reply: freedom of **consciousness** vs freedom of **action** — real, but weakening.
- **Critique of Dialectical Reason 1960**: **scarcity**, **seriality**, **practico-inert**, **group-in-fusion** — the concession that proves the objection landed.

**CLOSING RECALL FLOW.**

```text
   MOTIVE MOVES ME ONLY THROUGH MEANING -> MEANING-CONFERRAL IS AN ACT
   OF THE FOR-ITSELF -> SO NO MOTIVE DETERMINES UNLESS I CONSTITUTE IT
   -> DETERMINISM TREATS THE POUR-SOI AS AN EN-SOI: FALSE PROBLEM ->
   'YOU CAN ALWAYS MAKE SOMETHING OUT OF WHAT YOU HAVE BEEN MADE INTO':
   facticity CONCEDED, transcendence ASSERTED, 'always' is STRUCTURAL ->
   OBJECTION: oppression limits action AND awareness -> REPLY splits
   consciousness/action and WEAKENS the thesis -> CRITIQUE 1960 concedes
   scarcity, seriality, practico-inert
```

### SESSION 11 — SARTRE: THE LOOK, BEING-FOR-OTHERS, SHAME, AND WHY CONFLICT IS THE ORIGINAL MEANING OF THE RELATION

**DEFINITION.** *Plain language:* when someone catches you, you do not infer that another mind exists — you *feel* it, because you suddenly have an outside you cannot see and did not choose. *Technical:* **being-for-others** (*l'être-pour-autrui*) is the dimension of my being that is constituted from a perspective I cannot occupy; the **Look** (*le regard*) is the encounter in which the Other is given not as an object I observe but as the subject for whom I am an object, and **shame** (*la honte*) is the mode in which that dimension is lived.

**ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING.** ⚠️ *"Sartre's argument from the Look is not a proof that other minds exist but a demonstration that I already live one: shame is by its structure shame of myself before someone, so the Other is given in the very form of my self-experience rather than reached at the end of an inference."*

**MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS.** the Look (*le regard*) · being-for-others (*l'être-pour-autrui*) · shame (*la honte*) · pre-reflective consciousness · "shame is by nature recognition" · "the Other is the indispensable mediator between myself and me" · the body-for-others (*le corps-pour-autrui*) · conflict (*conflit*) · love, language, masochism · indifference, desire, sadism, hate · "l'enfer, c'est les autres".

**Plain-language visual — the idea before the vocabulary.**

```text
   AT THE KEYHOLE

   STAGE 1  ABSORBED                     STAGE 2  CAUGHT
   Bent at a keyhole out of              Footsteps in the hall.  I AM
   jealousy or curiosity.  I am          SEEN.  Instantly I am no
   wholly absorbed: there is NO          longer pure surveying
   EGO in my consciousness, only         transcendence - I am A PEEPING
   the scene and my project.             TOM: an object with a nature,
   Consciousness is PRE-REFLECTIVE.      seen from outside, judged.
   THERE IS NO SELF TO BE ASHAMED.       SHAME.
```

*What this shows: nothing changed in the corridor or in the room. What changed is that I acquired an outside — and the fact that I could not have given it to myself is the whole argument.*

**🖼️ VISUAL — the seven-step argument, the two failing strategies, and the four accounts of the Other.**

```text
   1  In being looked at I experience SHAME - and shame is not a
      private feeling I might have alone; by its structure it is
      SHAME OF MYSELF BEFORE SOMEONE.
   2  'SHAME IS BY NATURE RECOGNITION': in being ashamed I RECOGNISE
      that I am as the Other sees me.  I do not INFER the Other; I
      LIVE his presence as the condition of my new mode of being.
   3  The Other is NOT reached by explicit inference.  Sartre contrasts
      the Look with Husserlian APPRESENTATION and Heideggerian BEING-
      WITH - though neither rival is fairly reduced to an argument
      from analogy or to neutral co-existence.  THE OTHER IS GIVEN AS
      THE SUBJECT FOR WHOM I AM AN OBJECT.
   4  THEREFORE 'THE OTHER IS THE INDISPENSABLE MEDIATOR BETWEEN
      MYSELF AND ME': I acquire an OUTSIDE, a 'nature', a being I AM
      without having chosen it and without being able to know it from
      within - my BEING-FOR-OTHERS.
   5  The Look reveals a dimension of FACTICITY: the meanings of my
      acts and my body are constituted from a perspective I cannot
      control.  This is ONE limit on mastery, not the only constraint
      Sartre recognises.
   6  THE DIALECTIC OF CONCRETE RELATIONS (Being and Nothingness,
      Part III, ch. 3).  Objectified by the Other's freedom, I can
      respond in only TWO directions - AND BOTH FAIL:
      ---------------------------------------------------------------
      (a) ASSIMILATE the Other's freedom - possess the freedom that
          judges me WHILE IT REMAINS FREE
          FORMS: LOVE, LANGUAGE, MASOCHISM
          WHY IT FAILS: love wants to be loved FREELY - but to be
          loved is to be made an object, so the very freedom sought is
          destroyed in the possessing.  Masochism collapses because I
          cannot succeed in being pure object while remaining
          conscious.
      ---------------------------------------------------------------
      (b) TRANSCEND the Other's freedom - reduce him to an object so
          his look cannot fix me
          FORMS: INDIFFERENCE, DESIRE, SADISM, HATE
          WHY IT FAILS: an object cannot confer the recognition I
          wanted; the moment he looks back the structure reverses.
          Hate wants the Other's DISAPPEARANCE - which would leave my
          being-for-others intact.
      ---------------------------------------------------------------
   7  THEREFORE 'CONFLICT IS THE ORIGINAL MEANING OF BEING-FOR-
      OTHERS.'  The line of HUIS CLOS (No Exit, 1944) - 'L'ENFER,
      C'EST LES AUTRES' - follows from this.
      QUOTE IT, THEN CORRECT THE POPULAR MISREADING: Sartre himself
      explained, in a 1965 recorded preface to the play, that the line
      does NOT mean relations with others are always poisonous.  It
      means that IF one's relations are twisted, the Other's judgement
      becomes the medium of one's own self-torment - and that the
      three characters are DEAD, i.e. FIXED, unable to change their
      acts.  MAKING THIS CORRECTION IS A HIGH-VALUE MOVE.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------
   THE FOUR ACCOUNTS OF THE OTHER - A TABLE WORTH MEMORISING
   HUSSERL   passive PAIRING and APPRESENTATION, not a discursive
             analogy-inference.  The Other is non-originally given;
             ownness-first circularity remains
   HEIDEGGER Dasein is ALWAYS ALREADY BEING-WITH (Mitsein).  Social
             existence is constitutive - though Sartre presses for the
             conflictual concrete encounter
   SARTRE    the LOOK - the Other is revealed in the SHAME of being
             objectified.  CONFLICT; recognition structurally
             unattainable
   LEVINAS   the FACE (visage) of the Other, which COMMANDS before it
             is known.  ETHICAL ASYMMETRY - responsibility precedes
             freedom; explicitly a reply to both Husserl and Sartre
```

*Caption: steps 1–4 give the argument, steps 6–7 give its consequence, and the four-account table is what turns a description of the keyhole into a comparative answer. The Levinas row is the strongest single line of criticism in the clause.*

**NUMBERED TEACHING.**

1. **Bounded relevance — state the fence.** ⚠️ Being-for-others is **necessary** for questions on alienation, bad faith or intersubjectivity, but it must **not displace the printed freedom/choice spine** in every Sartre answer. One enriching development is right; a whole section is not.
2. **The exact printed subterms.** ✅ *le regard* (the Look) · *l'être-pour-autrui* (being-for-others) · *la honte* (shame) · *le corps-pour-autrui* (the body-for-others) · *"objet-autrui"* / *"sujet-autrui"* · *"la chute originelle"* (the original fall) · *conflit* as "the original meaning of being-for-others".
3. **The keyhole, in two stages.** ✅ **Absorbed:** bent at the keyhole out of jealousy or curiosity, I am wholly absorbed; there is **no ego** in my consciousness, only the scene and my project; consciousness is **pre-reflective**, non-positional about itself; **there is no self to be ashamed**. **Caught:** footsteps in the hall — **I am seen** — and instantly my whole being is transformed: I am no longer pure surveying transcendence but *a peeping Tom*, an object with a nature, seen from outside and judged.
4. **The argument.** ✅ Shame is by its structure shame **of myself before someone**; "**shame is by nature recognition**"; the Other is therefore not inferred but **lived** as the condition of my new mode of being; hence "**the Other is the indispensable mediator between myself and me**" — I acquire an **outside** I did not choose and cannot know from within.
5. **What the Look adds to facticity.** ✅ It reveals that the meanings of my acts and my body are constituted from a perspective I cannot control. ⚠️ This is **one** limit on mastery, not the only constraint Sartre recognises.
6. **The dialectic of concrete relations.** ✅ Two possible responses, both failing. **(a) Assimilate** the Other's freedom — love, language, masochism — which fails because love wants to be loved *freely*, yet to be loved is to be made an object, so the freedom sought is destroyed in the possessing, and masochism collapses because I cannot be pure object while remaining conscious. **(b) Transcend** the Other's freedom — indifference, desire, sadism, hate — which fails because an object cannot confer the recognition wanted, and hate wants a disappearance that would leave my being-for-others intact.
7. **The conclusion and the famous line.** ✅ "**Conflict is the original meaning of being-for-others.**" The line of *Huis clos* (*No Exit*, **1944**) — "**L'enfer, c'est les autres**" — follows from it. ⚠️ **Quote it, then correct the popular reading**: Sartre explained in a **1965** recorded preface to the play that the line does not mean relations with others are always poisonous, but that if one's relations are twisted the Other's judgement becomes the medium of one's own self-torment — and that the three characters are **dead**, that is, **fixed**, unable to change their acts.
8. **The presuppositions.** ⚠️ (P1) shame is irreducibly relational and cannot be analysed away as self-assessment; (P2) pre-reflective consciousness is egoless, so the ego that appears under the Look is genuinely **conferred**; (P3) subject and object are exclusive — I cannot be looked-at and looking in the same act, which is what makes reciprocity impossible; (P4) there is no third term, no "we-subject" of the kind Marxists posit, that could stabilise the relation — Sartre discusses and rejects it in Part III.
9. **The three strongest objections.** ❓ (i) Sartre **generalises from pathological encounters** — the peeping Tom, sadism, seduction — to the structure of all relations. *Reply:* the claim is ontological rather than statistical; it analyses possibility-conditions, and conflict is the structure even where unnoticed. *Residual:* a strong objection, and Sartre's own move toward the **group-in-fusion** in the *Critique* (1960) is the best evidence it landed. (ii) **Love, trust and being-cared-for** are equally basic and are not objectifications. *Reply:* Sartre analyses love and finds an internal contradiction — but the analysis is compelling only if P3 is accepted. (iii) **Levinas:** to encounter the Other only as a rival freedom is still to think him from the self. ⚠️ Sartre has **no reply within *Being and Nothingness***; this is the single strongest line of criticism.
10. **The executable verdict.** ⚠️ The Look gives a powerful non-inferential account of being exposed to another perspective, but Sartre may generalise conflict from selective examples; later solidarity, feminist and postcolonial accounts **test** rather than simply repeat the subject/object model.

**HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification.**

> **Claim.** Sartre's Look establishes not that other minds exist but that I already live a dimension of my being that only another's perspective can confer. **Named evidence.** In *Being and Nothingness* (1943) the man bent at the keyhole is pre-reflectively absorbed and has no ego to be ashamed of until footsteps are heard, whereupon he is constituted as a peeping Tom; Sartre's formula that shame is by nature recognition and that the Other is the indispensable mediator between myself and me states the result; and Part III, chapter 3 works out the two available responses — love, language and masochism, which try to possess the Other's freedom, and indifference, desire, sadism and hate, which try to abolish it — before concluding that conflict is the original meaning of being-for-others, a conclusion dramatised in *Huis clos* (1944). **Analysis.** The argument turns on the exclusivity of looking and being looked at: because I cannot occupy the perspective from which I am an object, the outside I acquire is genuinely conferred rather than constructed, and both strategies for recovering mastery fail for the same structural reason — the first destroys the freedom it wants to possess, the second destroys the recognition it wants to receive. **Qualification.** ❓ The subject/object exclusivity is a presupposition rather than a result, the examples are drawn from a narrow and largely pathological range, and Levinas's charge that a rival freedom is still an Other thought from the self receives no answer within *Being and Nothingness*.

**EXAM LINK.** This session supplies the **alienation** limb of **2018 Q1(d)** — the self living through the objectifying gaze — and gives one enriching development to **2023 Q3(c)** and **2021 Q4(b)**. ⚠️ It is bounded content: it should occupy one paragraph, never the spine of a freedom answer.

**UPSC TRAPS.**

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| Treating the Look as an argument for other minds | ✅ It is a **description of a lived structure**, not an inference; shame already contains the Other. |
| Retelling the keyhole without the two stages | ✅ Stage 1 has **no ego**; stage 2 is where the ego is **conferred**. Without stage 1 the argument disappears. |
| Quoting "hell is other people" without the correction | ⚠️ Sartre's **1965** recorded preface corrects the popular reading; the characters are **dead**, i.e. fixed. |
| Listing the failing strategies without saying **why** each fails | ✅ Love destroys the freedom it seeks; hate leaves being-for-others intact. The reasons are the doctrine. |
| Letting the Look displace freedom and choice in a Sartre answer | ⚠️ Bounded content: one development, not a section. |
| Presenting conflict as demonstrated rather than presupposed | ❓ **P3**, subject/object exclusivity, is a presupposition; Levinas's objection is unanswered in 1943. |

**REVISION NOTES.**

- **Keyhole**: absorbed (**no ego**, pre-reflective) → **caught** (an outside is conferred) → **shame**.
- "**Shame is by nature recognition**"; "**the Other is the indispensable mediator between myself and me**".
- Two failing strategies: **assimilate** (love, language, masochism) · **transcend** (indifference, desire, sadism, hate).
- "**Conflict is the original meaning of being-for-others**"; *Huis clos* **1944**; correction in the **1965** preface.
- Presuppositions P1–P4, esp. **P3 subject/object exclusivity** and the rejection of a "we-subject".
- Four accounts: **Husserl** (pairing/appresentation) · **Heidegger** (*Mitsein*) · **Sartre** (the Look, conflict) · **Levinas** (the **face**, ethical asymmetry).
- Objections: pathological sample · love and trust as basic · **Levinas unanswered** in 1943; *Critique* 1960 **group-in-fusion**.

**CLOSING RECALL FLOW.**

```text
   ABSORBED AT THE KEYHOLE (no ego) -> FOOTSTEPS: I AM SEEN -> SHAME,
   which is BY NATURE RECOGNITION -> so the Other is LIVED, not inferred
   -> 'THE OTHER IS THE INDISPENSABLE MEDIATOR BETWEEN MYSELF AND ME'
   -> I have an OUTSIDE I did not choose -> TWO responses: ASSIMILATE
   (love/masochism) or TRANSCEND (sadism/hate) -> BOTH FAIL -> CONFLICT
   IS THE ORIGINAL MEANING -> 'hell is other people' + Sartre's 1965
   correction -> LEVINAS: still thought from the self (unanswered 1943)
```

### SESSION 12 — HEIDEGGER: THE BEING-QUESTION, THE ONTOLOGICAL DIFFERENCE, AND WHY THE ROUTE RUNS THROUGH DASEIN RATHER THAN A TRANSCENDENTAL EGO

**DEFINITION.** *Plain language:* Heidegger is not asking what exists; he is asking what it *means* for anything to be at all — and he says we can only get at that by examining the one entity that already understands, however dimly, what "being" means: ourselves. *Technical:* **fundamental ontology** is the inquiry into the **meaning of Being** (*Sein*), conducted through an **existential analytic of Dasein**; the **ontological difference** distinguishes **Being** — the intelligibility in virtue of which entities show up as entities — from **particular beings**, and denies that Being is one supreme entity among others.

**ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING.** ⚠️ *"Heidegger's starting point is not the subject but the question: what does it mean for anything to be? — and the reason the inquiry runs through Dasein is not that the human being is philosophically privileged but that it is the only entity that already operates with an understanding of Being it has never made explicit."*

**MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS.** the Being-question (*Seinsfrage*) · fundamental ontology · ontological difference · Being (*Sein*) against beings (*Seiende*) · pre-ontological understanding · Dasein · "the essence of Dasein lies in its existence" (§9) · existentiale against category · *Being and Time* (1927), **unfinished** · suspension (*epoché*) belongs to Husserl · transcendental ego rejected · being-in-the-world as the more primordial condition.

**Plain-language visual — the idea before the vocabulary.**

```text
   TWO QUESTIONS THAT LOOK ALIKE

   'WHAT THINGS EXIST?'                  'WHAT DOES IT MEAN TO BE?'
   Answer: stars, numbers, laws,         Answer: not another THING on
   minds, God.  You produce a            the list.  It asks what has to
   LIST.                                 be in place for anything at all
        |                                to show up AS something.
        v                                          |
   Every answer names a BEING.                     v
                                         THIS is the ONTOLOGICAL
                                         DIFFERENCE: BEING is not one
                                         more BEING, however supreme.
```

*What this shows: the difference between an inventory and a condition. Miss it and Heidegger's whole project reads as a strange theology.*

**🖼️ VISUAL — the question, the difference, the choice of Dasein, and the break with Husserl.**

```text
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   |   THE PROJECT: FUNDAMENTAL ONTOLOGY                                |
   |   BEING AND TIME (Sein und Zeit, 1927)                             |
   |   NOT an existentialist psychology.  NOT a general ethics of        |
   |   authenticity.  The question is THE MEANING OF BEING.             |
   |   PUBLISHED UNFINISHED: only Division One and Division Two of      |
   |   PART ONE appeared.  Division III and Part Two never published.   |
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
                                   |
                                   v
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   |   THE ONTOLOGICAL DIFFERENCE                                       |
   |   BEING (Sein) ......... the intelligibility in virtue of which    |
   |     entities show up AS entities                                   |
   |   BEINGS (Seiende) ..... the entities themselves                   |
   |   BEING IS NOT ONE SUPREME ENTITY.  Treating it as such is the     |
   |   'onto-theological' mistake Heidegger charges the tradition with. |
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
                                   |
                                   v
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   |   WHY DASEIN?                                                      |
   |   Dasein ('being-there') is selected because                       |
   |     (i) it ALREADY HAS A PRE-ONTOLOGICAL UNDERSTANDING of Being -  |
   |         it copes competently with a world before it theorises      |
   |     (ii) ITS OWN BEING IS AT ISSUE FOR IT - it is the entity for   |
   |         whom what it is to be is a question it must live out       |
   |   THEREFORE the existential analysis is a ROUTE toward the Being-  |
   |   question.  Moods, death and authenticity are NOT isolated        |
   |   psychological topics.                                            |
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
                                   |
                                   v
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   |   SECTION 9: 'THE ESSENCE OF DASEIN LIES IN ITS EXISTENCE'         |
   |   Dasein's 'WHAT' is not a fixed quidditas but its MANNER OF       |
   |   BEING.  Hence EXISTENTIALIA (structures of Dasein) replace       |
   |   CATEGORIES (which apply to things).                              |
   |   THIS IS NOT SARTRE'S SLOGAN - see Session 6 and the 1946/47      |
   |   Letter on Humanism.                                              |
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
                                   |
                                   v
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   |   THE BREAK WITH HUSSERL - EXACTLY WHAT 2018 Q4(a) NEEDS           |
   |                                                                    |
   |   WHAT THE EPOCHE IS (ATTRIBUTE IT TO HUSSERL - Topic 09):         |
   |     the SUSPENSION of the natural attitude's EXISTENCE-POSIT.      |
   |     Nothing is denied or doubted; the positing is put OUT OF       |
   |     PLAY so that what is given can be described purely.  The       |
   |     residue is TRANSCENDENTAL SUBJECTIVITY.                        |
   |     ONE ACCURATE SENTENCE.  DO NOT EXPOUND TOPIC 09 HERE.          |
   |                                                                    |
   |   HEIDEGGER'S OBJECTION:                                           |
   |     The epoche BEGINS FROM A REFLECTIVE SUSPENSION - and so        |
   |     already presupposes a spectator standing over against a        |
   |     world.  But the MORE PRIMORDIAL condition is DASEIN'S          |
   |     ALREADY-INVOLVED BEING-IN-THE-WORLD.  SUBJECT and OBJECT are   |
   |     DERIVATIVE ABSTRACTIONS from practical involvement.            |
   |                                                                    |
   |   WHAT HE DOES NOT DO:                                             |
   |     He does NOT reject careful phenomenological description.  He   |
   |     REDIRECTS it - from transcendental consciousness to the        |
   |     DISCLOSEDNESS of Being through care, equipment and time.       |
   |                                                                    |
   |   FORBIDDEN MOVES:                                                 |
   |     Do NOT equate being-in-the-world with SPATIAL CONTAINMENT.     |
   |     Do NOT equate it with SARTREAN CONSCIOUSNESS.                  |
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
```

*Caption: the last panel is the map of 2018 Q4(a). The paper asks three things in sequence — what the *epoché* is, how Heidegger rejects it, and what being-in-the-world is *as opposed to* a transcendental ego — and the answer must move in that order, spending one accurate sentence on Husserl and the rest on Heidegger.*

**NUMBERED TEACHING.**

1. **State the project correctly in the first line.** ✅ Heidegger's enterprise is **fundamental ontology** — the question of the **meaning of Being** — not an existentialist psychology and not a general ethics of authenticity. ⚠️ An answer that opens by describing Heidegger as a philosopher of anxiety and death has already misplaced the topic.
2. **The ontological difference.** ✅ **Being** is the intelligibility in virtue of which entities show up *as* entities; **beings** are the entities. Being is **not one supreme entity**. ⚠️ This single distinction blocks the commonest misreading, in which "Being" is treated as a metaphysical super-object or as a synonym for God.
3. **Why Dasein is the route.** ✅ Two reasons, both textual: Dasein already has a **pre-ontological understanding of Being** — it copes competently with a world long before it theorises about it — and its **own Being is at issue for it**. ⚠️ Dasein is therefore chosen for a methodological reason, not because human beings are metaphysically special.
4. **What "Dasein" does and does not mean.** ✅ "Being-there": the entity each of us is insofar as its own Being is at issue and it understands Being. ⚠️ It is **not** merely a biological synonym for "human being", and it is not a name for consciousness, a subject or a soul.
5. **§9 and the replacement of categories by existentialia.** ✅ "The essence of Dasein lies in its existence" means that Dasein's "what" is not a fixed *quidditas* but its **manner of being**. Consequently Heidegger replaces the traditional **categories**, which apply to things, with **existentialia**, the structures of Dasein's being. ⚠️ This is the sentence Sartre appropriates and Heidegger repudiates (Session 6).
6. **The unfinished book.** ✅ *Being and Time* (**1927**) appeared as Divisions One and Two of **Part One only**; **Division III ("Time and Being") and the whole of Part Two were never published**. ❌ Never write that Heidegger "completes his analysis of Being" in the book. ⚠️ He later underwent the **turn** (*Kehre*), moving from the Dasein-analytic toward the history of Being, language and art; whether that is a failure or a deepening is contested.
7. **The *epoché*, attributed correctly.** ✅ Husserl's **suspension** puts the natural attitude's **existence-posit** out of play — nothing is denied or doubted — so that what is given may be described purely, leaving transcendental subjectivity as the residue. ⚠️ **One accurate sentence.** The doctrine belongs to **Topic 09**, and 2018 Q4(a) asks for it only as the position Heidegger rejects.
8. **Heidegger's objection, stated as an argument.** ✅ The reduction **begins from a reflective suspension** and therefore already presupposes a spectator set over against a world. But the **more primordial** condition is Dasein's **already-involved being-in-the-world**: subject and object are **derivative abstractions** from practical involvement. ⚠️ The point is one of *order*, not of accuracy — Husserl's descriptions are not being called false; his starting point is being called late.
9. **What Heidegger does not do.** ⚠️ He does **not** reject careful phenomenological description; he **redirects** it from transcendental consciousness to the **disclosedness of Being** through care, equipment and temporality. ✅ This qualification is what keeps a 2018 Q4(a) answer from becoming a caricature.
10. **Two forbidden equations.** ❌ Do **not** equate being-in-the-world with **spatial containment** — Dasein is not "in" the world as water is in a glass. ❌ Do **not** equate it with **Sartrean consciousness** — the for-itself is still a consciousness facing being, which is precisely the framework Heidegger's structure dissolves.

**HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification.**

> **Claim.** Heidegger's rejection of the transcendental ego is not a rejection of phenomenological description but a claim about where description must begin. **Named evidence.** Husserl's suspension (*epoché*) puts the natural attitude's existence-positing out of play so that givenness can be described without prejudice, and its residue is transcendental subjectivity; Heidegger, in *Being and Time* (1927), argues that this procedure already presupposes a reflective spectator, and sets against it the structure of being-in-the-world (*In-der-Welt-sein*), together with the claim at §9 that the essence of Dasein lies in its existence, which is why Dasein's structures must be called existentialia rather than categories. **Analysis.** The objection is one of order rather than of accuracy: if Dasein is always already absorbed in a referential context of equipment and concern, then the subject-object pair is an abstraction *from* that involvement, so a method that starts by suspending the world's positing has begun one step too late and has silently retained the very framework it meant to bracket. **Qualification.** ⚠️ Heidegger does not discard phenomenology but redirects it toward the disclosedness of Being through care, equipment and temporality; and the project itself remained incomplete, since Division III of *Being and Time* was never published and Heidegger subsequently turned toward the history of Being.

**EXAM LINK.** This session is the first two-thirds of **2018 Q4(a)** ("What is 'Epoché'? How does Heidegger reject this method…?") and the necessary frame for **2019 Q3(a)** and **2022 Q2(a)**, both of which fail if Dasein is introduced as a name for the human subject.

**UPSC TRAPS.**

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| Treating "Being" as a supreme entity or as God | ✅ The **ontological difference**: Being is not one more being, however exalted. |
| Introducing Dasein as "the human being" | ✅ It is the entity **for whom its own Being is at issue** and which has a pre-ontological understanding of Being. |
| Expounding Husserl's method at length in 2018 Q4(a) | ⚠️ **One accurate sentence** attributing the *epoché* to Husserl; the burden of the question is Heidegger. |
| Saying Heidegger rejects phenomenology | ✅ He **redirects** it; description is retained, its starting point is changed. |
| Writing that *Being and Time* completes the analysis of Being | ✅ **Unfinished** — Division III and Part Two never appeared. |
| Reading being-in-the-world as spatial containment | ❌ Explicitly forbidden; it is a **unitary structure**, not a location. |

**REVISION NOTES.**

- Project = **fundamental ontology**: the **meaning of Being**, not a psychology of moods.
- **Ontological difference**: **Being** (intelligibility) ≠ **beings** (entities); Being is not a supreme entity.
- Dasein chosen for two reasons: **pre-ontological understanding**; its **own Being is at issue**.
- **§9**: "the essence of Dasein lies in its existence" → **existentialia**, not categories.
- *Being and Time* **1927**, **unfinished**; Division III never published; later **the turn** (*Kehre*).
- *Epoché* = **Husserl's** suspension of the existence-posit — one sentence, Topic 09.
- Objection: reduction **starts from reflection**; the primordial condition is **already-involved being-in-the-world**; subject/object are **derivative**.
- Forbidden: being-in-the-world as **spatial containment** or as **Sartrean consciousness**.

**CLOSING RECALL FLOW.**

```text
   THE QUESTION IS 'WHAT DOES IT MEAN TO BE?' -> ONTOLOGICAL
   DIFFERENCE: Being is NOT a being -> ROUTE: the entity that ALREADY
   UNDERSTANDS Being and whose OWN BEING IS AT ISSUE = DASEIN ->
   Section 9: essence lies in EXISTENCE -> so EXISTENTIALIA, not
   categories -> HUSSERL'S EPOCHE suspends the existence-posit and
   leaves TRANSCENDENTAL SUBJECTIVITY -> HEIDEGGER: that STARTS FROM
   REFLECTION and presupposes a spectator -> the PRIMORDIAL condition
   is BEING-IN-THE-WORLD -> subject and object are DERIVATIVE ->
   description REDIRECTED, not abandoned -> and the book is UNFINISHED
```

### SESSION 13 — HEIDEGGER: BEING-IN-THE-WORLD, WORLDHOOD, AND WHY THE HAMMER IS NOT FIRST AN OBJECT

**DEFINITION.** *Plain language:* you are not a mind that happens to be located near some objects; you are already caught up in a world of tasks and tools that make sense together, and things show up first as *for* something, not as things with properties. *Technical:* **being-in-the-world** (*In-der-Welt-sein*) is a **unitary** structure — one hyphenated phenomenon, not subject **plus** relation **plus** world; the **world** is not the totality of physical objects but the **referential context of meaning** within which entities show up as significant; entities are encountered primarily as **ready-to-hand** (*Zuhandenheit*) equipment and only derivatively as **present-at-hand** (*Vorhandenheit*) objects with properties.

**ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING.** ⚠️ *"The hyphens in 'being-in-the-world' are the doctrine: Heidegger's compound is designed to resist decomposition, because the moment it is broken into a subject, a relation and a world, the Cartesian picture it was built to dissolve has been reinstated."*

**MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS.** being-in-the-world (*In-der-Welt-sein*) · unitary, not composite · worldhood (*Weltlichkeit*) · referential context · ready-to-hand (*Zuhandenheit*) · present-at-hand (*Vorhandenheit*) · equipment (*Zeug*) · the in-order-to (*Um-zu*) · referential totality · breakdown — unusability, absence, obstruction · dwelling as a way of being.

**Plain-language visual — the idea before the vocabulary.**

```text
   HOW A HAMMER IS ACTUALLY MET

   THE THEORY-FIRST PICTURE              HEIDEGGER'S PICTURE
   1 An object: 700 g, wooden            1 Something TO HAMMER WITH -
     handle, steel head.                   met INSIDE a task.
   2 I notice its properties.            2 In skilled use it 'withdraws':
   3 I decide it could be used             the better it works, the LESS
     for hammering.                        it is noticed.
        |                                3 It refers BEYOND itself: nail
        v                                  -> board -> house -> shelter
   The USE is added on at the             -> a WAY OF LIVING.
   end.
                                         The OBJECT-WITH-PROPERTIES
                                         appears LATER - typically when
                                         something GOES WRONG.
```

*What this shows: the order of encounter is reversed. Use is not applied to a previously known object; the object emerges out of a disturbance in use.*

**🖼️ VISUAL — the unitary structure, the two modes, the referential chain, and breakdown.**

```text
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   |   WHY THE COMPOUND CANNOT BE DECOMPOSED                            |
   |                                                                    |
   |   THE CARTESIAN PICTURE          HEIDEGGER'S REPLACEMENT           |
   |   [ SUBJECT ] --- relation ---   [ BEING-IN-THE-WORLD ]            |
   |        --- [ WORLD ]             ONE hyphenated phenomenon.        |
   |   An inner mind; an outer        Dasein does not first EXIST and   |
   |   world; a bridge problem        then ENCOUNTER a world.  It IS    |
   |   ('how do I know it is          constitutively IN a world -       |
   |   there?')                       practically engaged, absorbed,    |
   |                                  concerned.  THE BRIDGE PROBLEM    |
   |                                  DOES NOT ARISE, because the gap   |
   |                                  it bridges was an artefact.       |
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
                                   |
                                   v
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   |   WORLDHOOD (Weltlichkeit)                                         |
   |   'World' is NOT the totality of physical objects (the universe    |
   |   of natural science).  It is the REFERENTIAL CONTEXT OF MEANING   |
   |   within which things show up AS SIGNIFICANT.                      |
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
                                   |
       +---------------------------+---------------------------+
       v                                                       v
   +-------------------------------+     +----------------------------+
   | READY-TO-HAND (Zuhandenheit)  |     | PRESENT-AT-HAND            |
   | THE PRIMARY MODE.  Entities   |     | (Vorhandenheit)            |
   | are met as EQUIPMENT (Zeug) - |     | Entities THEMATISED as     |
   | tools, instruments - that we  |     | OBJECTS WITH PROPERTIES.   |
   | USE in a practical context.   |     | Prominent in THEORETICAL   |
   | The hammer is NOT first 'an   |     | INQUIRY.                   |
   | object with properties' and   |     | DERIVATIVE, not primary -  |
   | THEN 'used for hammering'; it |     | which reverses the whole   |
   | is ORIGINALLY equipment-for-  |     | modern order of            |
   | hammering.                    |     | explanation.               |
   +-------------------------------+     +----------------------------+
                                   |
                                   v
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   |   THE REFERENTIAL TOTALITY - THE 'IN-ORDER-TO' (Um-zu)             |
   |   HAMMER --in-order-to--> drive the NAIL --in-order-to--> fix the  |
   |   BOARD --in-order-to--> build the HOUSE --in-order-to--> SHELTER  |
   |   --for-the-sake-of--> DWELLING, i.e. A WAY OF BEING FOR DASEIN    |
   |                                                                    |
   |   EVERY piece of equipment REFERS BEYOND ITSELF.  There is no such |
   |   thing as ONE piece of equipment.  The chain terminates not in    |
   |   another tool but in a POSSIBILITY OF DASEIN'S OWN BEING.         |
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
                                   |
                                   v
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   |   BREAKDOWN - HOW THE OBJECT APPEARS                               |
   |   UNUSABILITY   the hammer is too heavy, the head is loose         |
   |   ABSENCE       the hammer is missing from where it should be      |
   |   OBSTRUCTION   something is in the way of the work                |
   |        |                                                           |
   |        v                                                           |
   |   The equipment-network becomes CONSPICUOUS, and a shift toward    |
   |   explicit OBJECT-THEMATISATION can open.                          |
   |   QUALIFY: breakdown DOES NOT INSTANTLY ERASE ALL PRACTICAL        |
   |   SIGNIFICANCE.  The workshop does not become a heap of physical   |
   |   objects the moment a tool fails.                                 |
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
```

*Caption: read the chain in the third panel from left to right and the phrase "referential totality" becomes concrete. Note where it ends — not in a further tool but in a way of being — which is why worldhood is an existential structure rather than an inventory.*

**NUMBERED TEACHING.**

1. **The compound is unitary.** ✅ Being-in-the-world is a **single hyphenated structure**, not three separate elements (subject + relation + world). ⚠️ Its purpose is to dissolve the Cartesian split between an inner subject and an external world — which is why the traditional "bridge" problem (how do I know the external world exists?) does not arise: the gap it bridges was an artefact of the decomposition.
2. **Dasein does not first exist and then encounter a world.** ✅ It **is** constitutively *in* a world — practically engaged, absorbed, concerned. ❌ "In" is not spatial containment.
3. **Worldhood.** ✅ The "world" is not the totality of physical objects — that is the universe of natural science — but the **referential context** of meaning within which things show up **as** significant.
4. **Ready-to-hand is primary.** ✅ Entities are met first as **equipment** (*Zeug*) that we *use* within a practical context. The hammer is not first "an object with properties" and then "used for hammering"; it is **originally** encountered as equipment-for-hammering, within a totality of references.
5. **Present-at-hand is derivative.** ✅ Entities thematised as objects with properties — a mode prominent in theoretical inquiry. ⚠️ Heidegger's claim is about **order of foundation**: the theoretical stance is a *modification* of practical engagement, not the neutral baseline from which practice is a special case. This reverses the modern order of explanation and is the real content of the doctrine.
6. **The referential totality and the in-order-to.** ✅ Every piece of equipment refers beyond itself — hammer to nail, nail to board, board to house, house to **dwelling-as-a-way-of-being**. This **in-order-to** (*Um-zu*) structure constitutes the worldhood of the world as a meaningful whole. ⚠️ There is no such thing as a *single* piece of equipment: the chain ends not in another tool but in a possibility of Dasein's own being.
7. **Breakdown analysis, with its qualification.** ✅ **Unusability**, **absence** or **obstruction** first makes the equipment-network **conspicuous** and can open a shift toward explicit object-thematisation. ⚠️ **Breakdown does not instantly erase all practical significance** — the workshop does not become a heap of physical objects the moment a tool fails. State this qualification; it is where careless scripts overclaim.
8. **Why this answers the Cartesian question rather than solving it.** ⚠️ Heidegger's move is not to prove the external world but to show that the demand for such a proof arises only after the unitary phenomenon has been broken up. ✅ In *Being and Time* he treats the persistence of that demand as the scandal, not its lack of an answer.
9. **The fence.** ⚠️ Descartes' own system — methodic doubt, the *cogito*, dualism — belongs to **Topic 02**. Here he appears as the position being dissolved, in one or two clauses.

**HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification.**

> **Claim.** Being-in-the-world is a single structure whose hyphens carry the argument, because any decomposition of it restores the subject-object picture it was constructed to replace. **Named evidence.** In *Being and Time* (1927) the world is defined not as the totality of physical objects but as the referential context within which entities show up as significant; the hammer is encountered primarily as ready-to-hand equipment within an in-order-to chain running from nail to board to house to dwelling; and the present-at-hand object with properties emerges in modes of breakdown — unusability, absence, obstruction — which make the equipmental network conspicuous. **Analysis.** Because the chain terminates not in a further tool but in a possibility of Dasein's own being, worldhood is an existential structure rather than an inventory, and because the theoretical stance is reached by a modification of practical engagement, the modern order of explanation is reversed: the neutral object is the late arrival, not the datum. **Qualification.** ⚠️ Breakdown does not instantly abolish practical significance, and Heidegger's move is not a proof of the external world but a diagnosis of why the demand for one arises — a diagnosis whose force depends on accepting that the subject-object pair is derivative rather than basic.

**EXAM LINK.** This session is the first half of **2022 Q2(a)** ("a critical account of Heidegger's Being-in-the-world") and the final third of **2018 Q4(a)** ("being in the world as opposed to the concept of a transcendental ego"). It is also the necessary base for care (Session 14), since care is the unity of exactly this structure.

**UPSC TRAPS.**

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| Describing Dasein as "a human being in the world" | ⚠️ That decomposition is the error the compound exists to block; the structure is **unitary**. |
| Treating "world" as the sum of physical things | ✅ It is a **referential context of meaning** — the workshop, not the universe. |
| Presenting present-at-hand as the basic mode | ✅ **Ready-to-hand is primary**; the theoretical object is a **modification**. |
| Saying breakdown turns the world into mere objects | ⚠️ It makes the network **conspicuous** and can open thematisation; it does not erase significance. |
| Stopping the in-order-to chain at another tool | ✅ It terminates in **dwelling**, a possibility of Dasein's own being. |
| Expounding Descartes' dualism | ⚠️ Topic 02. Here he is the position dissolved, in a clause. |

**REVISION NOTES.**

- **Unitary** structure; the hyphens block decomposition; the bridge problem is dissolved, not solved.
- **Worldhood** = **referential context of meaning**, not the totality of objects.
- **Ready-to-hand** (*Zuhandenheit*) is **primary**; **present-at-hand** (*Vorhandenheit*) is **derivative**.
- **In-order-to** (*Um-zu*): hammer → nail → board → house → **dwelling as a way of being**.
- **Breakdown**: unusability, absence, obstruction → network becomes **conspicuous**; **does not erase significance**.
- "In" is **not spatial containment**; Descartes = Topic 02, one clause.

**CLOSING RECALL FLOW.**

```text
   SUBJECT + WORLD IS ALREADY THE MISTAKE -> BEING-IN-THE-WORLD IS ONE
   STRUCTURE -> WORLD = REFERENTIAL CONTEXT OF MEANING -> things are met
   FIRST as READY-TO-HAND EQUIPMENT -> IN-ORDER-TO chain: hammer, nail,
   board, house, DWELLING -> PRESENT-AT-HAND OBJECT arrives LATER, via
   BREAKDOWN (unusable / missing / obstructing) -> which makes the
   network CONSPICUOUS but does NOT erase significance -> so the
   theoretical object is DERIVATIVE, and the bridge problem never arises
```

### SESSION 14 — HEIDEGGER: THE EXISTENTIALIA, DISCLOSEDNESS, AND CARE AS THE BEING OF DASEIN

**DEFINITION.** *Plain language:* three things are always true of you at once — you always find yourself in some mood, you always understand your situation as offering things you could do, and that understanding is always articulable; put them together and you get "care", Heidegger's name for the single structure of a human life. *Technical:* the **existentialia** — **attunement** (*Befindlichkeit*), **understanding** (*Verstehen*) and **discourse** (*Rede*) — constitute the **disclosedness** (*Erschlossenheit*) of Dasein, the "there" (*Da*) of *Da-sein*; **care** (*Sorge*) is the unitary structural whole of Dasein's being, formally defined as **"ahead-of-itself — already-in (a world) — alongside (entities encountered within-the-world)"**.

**ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING.** ⚠️ *"Care is not an attitude Dasein sometimes takes but the formal name for the fact that Dasein is always simultaneously ahead of itself, already in a situation it did not choose, and absorbed in the things it deals with — which is why worry and indifference are equally expressions of care, not exceptions to it."*

**MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS.** existentiale against category · attunement or state-of-mind (*Befindlichkeit*) · mood (*Stimmung*) · understanding (*Verstehen*) · projection upon possibilities · discourse (*Rede*) · disclosedness (*Erschlossenheit*) · the "there" (*Da*) · care (*Sorge*) · ahead-of-itself / already-in / alongside · concern (*Besorgen*) · solicitude (*Fürsorge*) · being-with (*Mitsein*).

**Plain-language visual — the idea before the vocabulary.**

```text
   THREE THINGS THAT ARE ALWAYS TRUE OF YOU AT ONCE

   YOU ARE IN SOME MOOD              -> ATTUNEMENT (Befindlichkeit)
      There is no mood-free state.      The mood is how the situation
      Even 'neutral' is a mood.         MATTERS to you at all.

   YOU SEE THINGS AS DOABLE          -> UNDERSTANDING (Verstehen)
      Not 'I have a theory of the       Practical know-how; you PROJECT
      chair' but 'I can sit'.           yourself on possibilities.

   IT CAN BE ARTICULATED             -> DISCOURSE (Rede)
      The situation is already          Not 'you talk about it'; the
      structured for saying.            structure is sayable.

   PUT TOGETHER: the 'THERE' IS OPEN.  DISCLOSEDNESS.
   NAME OF THE WHOLE: CARE (Sorge).
```

*What this shows: the three existentialia are not three faculties. They are three inseparable aspects of one fact — that a world is open for Dasein at all.*

**🖼️ VISUAL — the three existentialia, disclosedness, and the three limbs of care.**

*Caption: the last panel is the reason care must be memorised in Heidegger's exact words. The three limbs are not a description of moods; they are the three places where temporality will be found in Division Two.*

**NUMBERED TEACHING.**

1. **Existentialia replace categories.** ✅ Heidegger replaces the traditional **categories** — which apply to things — with **existentialia**, the structures of Dasein's being. ⚠️ The substitution follows directly from §9 (Session 12): if Dasein's essence lies in its existence, then thing-concepts cannot describe it.
2. **Attunement (*Befindlichkeit*).** ✅ Dasein always **already** finds itself in a **mood** (*Stimmung*) — anxiety, boredom, joy. ⚠️ Mood is **not** a subjective colouring added to an objectively given world; it is a fundamental mode of **disclosing** the world and one's situation. "We are always already attuned": there is no mood-free access to anything, and even indifference is a mood.
3. **Understanding (*Verstehen*).** ✅ Dasein projects itself upon **possibilities** — it understands its world in terms of what it *can do*. ⚠️ Understanding is **not** theoretical cognition but practical **know-how** and projection upon **potentiality-for-being**.
4. **Discourse (*Rede*).** ✅ The **articulation** of understanding: Dasein's being-open to meaning is always linguistically structured, and discourse is the condition of possibility of both speech and listening. ⚠️ It is not the act of talking; idle talk (Session 15) is a **deficient mode** of it.
5. **Disclosedness.** ✅ The three together constitute the **disclosedness** (*Erschlossenheit*) of Dasein — the "**there**" (*Da*) of *Da-sein*: the fact that Being is disclosed or opened up for this entity.
6. **Care, in Heidegger's exact words.** ✅ **"Ahead-of-itself — already-in (a world) — alongside (entities encountered within-the-world)"** (*Sich-vorweg — schon-sein-in — Sein-bei*), *Being and Time* **§41**. ⚠️ Reproduce the formula; paraphrasing it loses the mapping onto temporality.
7. **The three limbs decoded.** ✅ **Ahead-of-itself** = projection toward future possibilities (understanding, freedom). **Already-in** = thrownness into a world and a situation not chosen (facticity, attunement). **Alongside** = absorption in practical dealings with entities (concern, falling).
8. **Care is ontological, not psychological.** ⚠️ It is **not** worry or anxiety. It is the **structure** that makes all particular attitudes and activities possible — including indifference, which is a *mode* of care rather than its absence. ✅ This correction is a standing examiner test.
9. **Concern and solicitude.** ✅ **Concern** (*Besorgen*) names Dasein's dealings with equipment and tasks; **solicitude** (*Fürsorge*) names its dealings with **other Dasein**, because **being-with** (*Mitsein*, §26) is constitutive rather than added on. ⚠️ Dasein is never first solitary and then social.
10. **Why the formula must be memorised.** ⚠️ The three limbs map exactly onto the three ecstases of temporality — future, having-been, present (Session 16). Learning the two lists together converts the hardest twenty-marker in the clause (2019 Q3(a)) into a single diagram.

**HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification.**

> **Claim.** Care is Heidegger's name for the unity of Dasein's being, and it is an ontological structure rather than an emotional attitude. **Named evidence.** *Being and Time* (1927) identifies three existentialia — attunement (*Befindlichkeit*), in which Dasein always already finds itself in a mood that discloses its situation; understanding (*Verstehen*), in which it projects itself upon what it can do; and discourse (*Rede*), the articulation of that understanding — which together constitute disclosedness, the "there" of Da-sein; §41 then formalises their unity as "ahead-of-itself — already-in (a world) — alongside (entities encountered within-the-world)". **Analysis.** Each limb collects one existentiale — projection for the first, thrownness and attunement for the second, absorbed concern for the third — so that care is not one more feature of Dasein alongside moods and skills but the single structure of which they are aspects; consequently even indifference expresses care, since it is a way of being ahead of oneself in a situation while dealing with things. **Qualification.** ⚠️ The formula is formal: it says how Dasein is structured, not what Dasein should do, and its ultimate purpose is not psychological description but the demonstration in Division Two that the meaning of this structure is temporality.

**EXAM LINK.** This session is the central section of **2022 Q2(a)** and the doctrinal hinge of **2019 Q3(a)**, where the question "how does he relate temporality with Dasein?" is answered by mapping the three limbs of care onto the three ecstases.

**UPSC TRAPS.**

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| Translating *Sorge* as worry or anxiety | ✅ **Ontological structure**, not a psychological attitude; indifference is a **mode** of care. |
| Treating mood as a subjective colouring | ✅ Mood **discloses**; there is no mood-free access. |
| Making *Verstehen* theoretical knowledge | ✅ Practical **know-how** and **projection upon possibilities**. |
| Reducing *Rede* to talking | ✅ It is the **articulability** of understanding; idle talk is a **deficient mode**. |
| Paraphrasing the care formula | ⚠️ Reproduce "**ahead-of-itself — already-in — alongside**"; the mapping onto temporality depends on it. |
| Treating being-with as an addition to a solitary Dasein | ✅ *Mitsein* (**§26**) is **constitutive**; solicitude is the correlative of concern. |

**REVISION NOTES.**

- **Existentialia** replace **categories** (things).
- **Befindlichkeit** — always already **attuned**; mood **discloses**, never colours.
- **Verstehen** — **projection upon possibilities**; practical know-how, not cognition.
- **Rede** — **articulation** of understanding; condition of speech and listening.
- Three together = **disclosedness** (*Erschlossenheit*) = the **"there"** (*Da*).
- **Care (§41)**: "**ahead-of-itself — already-in — alongside**" = projection · thrownness/attunement · concern/falling.
- Care is **ontological**, not psychological. **Concern** (equipment) vs **solicitude** (others); **being-with §26** constitutive.
- The three limbs map onto **future / having-been / present**.

**CLOSING RECALL FLOW.**

```text
   ALWAYS IN A MOOD (Befindlichkeit) + ALWAYS PROJECTING ON POSSIBIL-
   ITIES (Verstehen) + ALWAYS ARTICULABLE (Rede) -> together = DIS-
   CLOSEDNESS, the 'THERE' -> named as a whole: CARE (Sorge, Section 41)
   -> 'AHEAD-OF-ITSELF (projection) -- ALREADY-IN (thrownness) --
   ALONGSIDE (concern)' -> NOT worry: it is the STRUCTURE that makes
   worry AND indifference possible -> and its three limbs will become
   FUTURE / HAVING-BEEN / PRESENT in Division Two
```

### SESSION 15 — HEIDEGGER: THE "THEY", FALLING, THE CALL OF CONSCIENCE, BEING-TOWARD-DEATH, AND WHY AUTHENTICITY IS NOT A VIRTUE

**DEFINITION.** *Plain language:* most of the time you do what "one" does — what people say, what is normally thought — and Heidegger says this is not a moral failing but the ordinary condition of being among others; becoming authentic is not becoming a better person but taking over your own life as yours, and the thing that forces the question is your own death. *Technical:* **authenticity** (*Eigentlichkeit*) and **inauthenticity** (*Uneigentlichkeit*) are **existential modifications** — modes of Dasein's self-relation — not moral categories; Dasein is **proximally and for the most part** (*zunächst und zumeist*) inauthentic, dispersed in **the "they"** (*das Man*) and **falling** (*Verfallen*); **being-toward-death** (*Sein-zum-Tode*), taken over in **anticipatory resoluteness** (*vorlaufende Entschlossenheit*), individualises Dasein and lets it take over its existence as a **whole**.

**ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING.** ⚠️ *"Authenticity, for Heidegger, is not a moral achievement but a change in the ownership of one's existence: the call of conscience says nothing about what to do, and its whole content is that the life in question is mine to answer for."*

**MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS.** authenticity (*Eigentlichkeit*) · inauthenticity, not a moral failing · existential modification · proximally and for the most part (*zunächst und zumeist*) · the "they" (*das Man*) · idle talk (*Gerede*) · curiosity (*Neugier*) · ambiguity (*Zweideutigkeit*) · falling (*Verfallen*) · call of conscience (*Ruf des Gewissens*) · null and groundless · being-toward-death (*Sein-zum-Tode*) · ownmost, non-relational, certain, indefinite as to when · "one dies" (*man stirbt*) · anticipatory resoluteness.

**Plain-language visual — the idea before the vocabulary.**

```text
   WHO IS LIVING YOUR LIFE?

   THE 'THEY' (das Man)                  OWNING IT (Eigentlichkeit)
   One reads what one reads.             The same job, the same city,
   One takes the job one takes.          the same reading - but taken
   One retires when one retires.         over as MINE, chosen from the
   Nobody in particular decides.         possibilities I am thrown
   AND THAT IS COMFORTABLE:              among.
   nobody in particular is               NOTHING NEW IS PRESCRIBED.
   answerable either.                    THE OWNERSHIP CHANGES.
        |
        v                                WHAT FORCES THE QUESTION?
   THIS IS NOT A MORAL FAILING.          MY DEATH - which nobody can
   IT IS THE ORDINARY CONDITION          take over for me, and which
   OF BEING AMONG OTHERS.                'one dies' conceals.
```

*What this shows: the contrast is not between a bad life and a good life. It is between a life that is nobody's in particular and the same life owned.*

**🖼️ VISUAL — inauthenticity, the call, death's four characters, and what authenticity is not.**

   |     CERTAIN ............ it is inevitable                          |

   |       WHEN                                                         |

   |     INDIVIDUALISING FORCE by making it an event that happens to    |
   |     people in general                                              |
