*Caption: the four characters of death are the most reliably examinable list in the clause, and the bottom panel is the correction the paper has demanded twice — in 2019 and again in 2022.*

**NUMBERED TEACHING.**

1. **The preliminary that decides the answer.** ✅ **Authenticity is not a moral virtue and inauthenticity is not a moral failing.** They are **existential modifications** — modes of Dasein's self-relation. Dasein is **proximally and for the most part** (*zunächst und zumeist*) inauthentic, and this is not a deficiency but a **structural feature** of being-in-the-world. ⚠️ Both 2019 Q3(a) and 2022 Q2(a) directly demand this clarification.
2. **The "they" (*das Man*).** ✅ Dasein exists with others (*Mitsein*, §26) and tends to lose itself in what "**one**" (*man*) does, says and thinks — the anonymous public. ⚠️ *Das Man* is not "other people"; it is the impersonal norm-setting **anyone** in whose name things are done.
3. **The three marks.** ✅ **Idle talk** (*Gerede*) — passing along received opinions without genuine understanding; **curiosity** (*Neugier*) — flitting restlessly from novelty to novelty; **ambiguity** (*Zweideutigkeit*) — everything seems accessible and nothing is genuinely appropriated. ⚠️ Each is a **deficient mode** of an existentiale: idle talk of discourse, curiosity of sight, ambiguity of understanding.
4. **Falling (*Verfallen*).** ✅ Dasein's tendency to be absorbed in the world of its concern and in *das Man*, losing its "ownmost" self. ⚠️ **Not** a theological "fall from grace"; it is an **ontological structure**, which is why it carries no blame.
5. **The call of conscience.** ✅ Dasein is called **back** from dispersal in *das Man* to its **null**, groundless responsibility for taking over its thrown projection (§§54–60). ⚠️ The call is **silent** and gives **no moral rule and no substantive programme**: its whole content is that this existence is Dasein's own to answer for.
6. **Being-toward-death, with all four characters.** ✅ Death is Dasein's **ownmost** (no one can die for me), **non-relational** (it isolates me from others), **certain** (inevitable), and **indefinite as to when** possibility. ⚠️ It is a **possibility**, not a dated event — which is why the analysis is not morbid.
7. **The inauthentic relation to death.** ✅ We flee it: "**one dies**" (*man stirbt*), "everyone dies someday" — covering over death's individualising force by converting it into an event that happens to people in general.
8. **The authentic relation.** ✅ **Anticipatory resoluteness** (*vorlaufende Entschlossenheit*): I face death as *my own* possibility, which discloses the finitude and urgency of my existence, frees me from *das Man*, and lets me take over my existence **as a whole**. ⚠️ "As a whole" is doing technical work: only a being that can relate to its own end can be grasped as a totality, which is the problem Division Two is built to solve.
9. **Individualisation does not abolish being-with.** ✅ Authenticity does not prescribe social withdrawal; it **modifies how Dasein owns its already social possibilities**. ⚠️ This blocks the "rugged individualist" reading, which is the second commonest error after the moral reading.
10. **The four things authenticity is not.** ⚠️ Not moral goodness; not "being true to yourself" in the popular sense; not individualism or withdrawal; not contemplating suicide or fearing the empirical event of dying. ✅ It **is** a **formal** modification of everydayness — owning one's thrownness and projecting upon one's ownmost possibilities.
11. **The strongest objection: formal emptiness.** ❓ Authenticity prescribes no content — so, critics say, a Nazi could be "authentic", a charge sharpened by Heidegger's own **1933** complicity. **Heidegger's reply:** authenticity is deliberately non-prescriptive; it tells you *how* to hold your existence, not *what* to choose. ⚠️ Many find this ethically irresponsible, and **Adorno's** charge of a "jargon of authenticity" presses the same point. ✅ The honest verdict: the formality is deliberate **and** it is the doctrine's deepest problem — a criterion that tells you to own your choices without telling you which to make can dignify any resolute commitment; the defence that fundamental ontology is not ethics is textually correct and philosophically unsatisfying.
12. **The second objection: morbidity and the Other.** ❓ **Levinas** argues that the ethical relation to the Other, not death, is primary; **Arendt** sets **natality** against mortality. **Heidegger's reply:** death is not morbidity but the disclosure of finitude that makes *any* authentic engagement possible, including care for others. ⚠️ Levinas's alternative remains a powerful counter and should be named rather than dismissed.

**HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification.**

> **Claim.** Heidegger's authenticity is a change in the ownership of an existence rather than an improvement in its moral quality, and this is why the analysis passes through the "they" and through death rather than through virtue. **Named evidence.** *Being and Time* (1927) describes Dasein as proximally and for the most part absorbed in the "they" (*das Man*), whose three marks are idle talk, curiosity and ambiguity (§§27, 35–38), and calls this falling an ontological structure rather than a fall from grace; the call of conscience (§§54–60) is silent and issues no rule, summoning Dasein back to its null responsibility for its thrown projection; and death (§§46–53) is characterised as ownmost, non-relational, certain and indefinite as to when, so that the inauthentic "one dies" conceals precisely its individualising force. **Analysis.** Because the call has no content and death is a possibility rather than an event, what anticipatory resoluteness changes is not the range of options but their ownership: the same thrown possibilities are taken over as mine and thereby gathered into a whole, which is why individualisation modifies rather than abolishes being-with. **Qualification.** ❓ The formality that makes the doctrine ontologically clean is also its deepest liability — a criterion that requires resolute ownership without specifying content can dignify any commitment, a charge sharpened by Heidegger's own 1933 complicity and by Adorno's "jargon of authenticity", and the reply that fundamental ontology is not ethics is textually correct without being reassuring.

**EXAM LINK.** This session is the second half of **2022 Q2(a)** and the first half of **2019 Q3(a)** ("Is Dasein authentic existence for Heidegger?" — the answer is *no*, and this session says why). It also supplies the criticism paragraph any Heidegger answer needs to reach a verdict.

**UPSC TRAPS.**

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| "Authenticity = being morally good / true to yourself" | ✅ A **formal existential modification**; the call gives **no** content. |
| Treating inauthenticity as a failure to be corrected | ✅ Dasein is inauthentic **proximally and for the most part**; this is **structural**. |
| Identifying *das Man* with "other people" | ⚠️ It is the impersonal **anyone** that sets norms; being-with is constitutive and not itself inauthentic. |
| Reading being-toward-death as fear of dying or suicide | ✅ It is a **possibility** disclosed as ownmost, non-relational, certain and indefinite — not the empirical event. |
| Giving fewer than four characters of death | ✅ **Ownmost · non-relational · certain · indefinite as to when.** |
| Reading authenticity as withdrawal from society | ✅ It **modifies how** Dasein owns already social possibilities. |
| Treating falling as sin | ✅ **Ontological structure**, not theology. |

**REVISION NOTES.**

- Authenticity/inauthenticity = **existential modifications**, not moral categories; inauthenticity is the **default** and is structural.
- ***Das Man***: **idle talk** · **curiosity** · **ambiguity**; **falling** (*Verfallen*) is ontological, not theological.
- **Call of conscience** (§§54–60): **silent**, gives **no rule**, calls Dasein back to its **null** responsibility.
- **Death** (§§46–53): **ownmost · non-relational · certain · indefinite as to when**; a **possibility**.
- Inauthentic: "**one dies**" (*man stirbt*). Authentic: **anticipatory resoluteness** → existence **as a whole**.
- Individualisation **modifies**, does not abolish, **being-with**.
- Objections: **formal emptiness** (Nazi charge, 1933, Adorno's "jargon") · **Levinas** (the Other) and **Arendt** (natality).

**CLOSING RECALL FLOW.**

```text
   DASEIN IS INAUTHENTIC PROXIMALLY AND FOR THE MOST PART - STRUCTURAL,
   NOT A FAILING -> DAS MAN: IDLE TALK / CURIOSITY / AMBIGUITY ->
   FALLING (ontological, not sin) -> THE SILENT CALL OF CONSCIENCE
   summons Dasein back to its NULL responsibility -> DEATH: OWNMOST,
   NON-RELATIONAL, CERTAIN, INDEFINITE - a POSSIBILITY, not an event ->
   'ONE DIES' conceals it -> ANTICIPATORY RESOLUTENESS owns it and gives
   existence AS A WHOLE -> AUTHENTICITY IS FORMAL: no content ->
   WHICH IS THE DOCTRINE'S POWER AND ITS DEEPEST PROBLEM
```

---

### SESSION 16 — HEIDEGGER: TEMPORALITY AS THE MEANING OF CARE, THE THREE ECSTASES, AND WHY CLOCK-TIME IS DERIVED

**DEFINITION.** *Plain language:* Heidegger's claim is that a human life is not *in* time the way a stone is in a river; a life *is* time — a stretching-out toward what is coming, out of what has been, into what is here — and the clock's row of nows is an abstraction from that. *Technical:* **temporality** (*Zeitlichkeit*) is the **meaning of care**: the three limbs of care are grounded in three **ecstases** — **future** (*Zukunft*), **having-been** (*Gewesenheit*) and **present** (*Gegenwart*) — which are not segments of a line but ways in which Dasein **stands out** (*ek-stasis*) into its own being; **ordinary or world-time** is a **derived** interpretation, not a falsehood.

**ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING.** ⚠️ *"The central thesis of Division Two is that care has a meaning and that its meaning is temporality: to be ahead of oneself is to be futural, to be already-in is to have-been, and to be alongside is to make present — so Dasein does not occupy time but temporalises it."*

**MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS.** temporality (*Zeitlichkeit*) · the meaning of care · ecstasis, standing-out · future (*Zukunft*), coming-toward · having-been (*Gewesenheit*), the past I still am · present (*Gegenwart*), making-present · priority of the future · finite and unified · ordinary or world-time (*innerzeitige Zeit*) · the now-series · levelled interpretation · historicality (*Geschichtlichkeit*).

**Plain-language visual — the idea before the vocabulary.**

```text
   TWO PICTURES OF TIME

   THE CLOCK'S TIME                      DASEIN'S TIME
   ...  now  now  now  now  ...          I am ALREADY in a situation I
   A row of nows.  The past ones         did not choose (HAVING-BEEN)
   are gone, one is here, the            while COMING TOWARD what I am
   future ones are not yet.              becoming (FUTURE) and DEALING
   INFINITE in both directions.          with what is here (PRESENT).
        |                                          |
        v                                          v
   Time is a CONTAINER I am              THE THREE ARE SIMULTANEOUS.
   inside.                               Time is not a container; it is
                                         how I am STRETCHED OUT.
                                         FINITE - bounded by death.
```

*What this shows: the clock's picture is not wrong about clocks. It is wrong as a description of what it is to live a life — and Heidegger's claim is that it is derived from the second picture, not the other way round.*

*Caption: the two-column table at the bottom is the answer to any "primordial versus ordinary time" demand, and the qualification panel beneath it is what keeps the answer from sliding into the false claim that Heidegger condemns clocks.*

**NUMBERED TEACHING.**

1. **The thesis.** ✅ The central claim of **Division Two** of *Being and Time* is that the **meaning of care** — and therefore of Dasein's being — is **temporality** (*Zeitlichkeit*). ⚠️ "Meaning" here is technical: it names that in terms of which the structure becomes intelligible as a unity.
2. **The three ecstases, mapped.** ✅ **Future** (*Zukunft*), "coming toward" oneself → projection, anticipation, being-toward-death → the limb **ahead-of-itself**. **Having-been** (*Gewesenheit*), the past that I *still am* → thrownness, facticity, guilt → **already-in**. **Present** (*Gegenwart*), "making present" → absorption in entities, practical engagement → **alongside**.
3. **Why *Gewesenheit* is not "the past".** ⚠️ Heidegger's word means **having-been**, the past that Dasein *still is* and carries forward as possibility — not a stretch of elapsed time left behind. ✅ Using the English "having-been" once shows the point without a translation lecture.
4. **The priority of the future.** ✅ The future — Dasein projecting upon its ownmost possibility, **death** — is the **primary** ecstasis; past and present are **temporalised out of** the future. **Authenticity is futural**: in anticipatory resoluteness Dasein runs ahead to its death and thereby gathers its existence into a **whole**.
5. **What "ecstatic" is doing.** ✅ The three dimensions are **not** containers or segments of a line; they are **ecstases** — Dasein *stands out* (*ek-stasis*) into each **simultaneously**. Temporality is a **unity** of future-past-present in which Dasein holds itself open. ⚠️ This is why a diagram of three boxes in a row misrepresents the doctrine.
6. **Primordial against ordinary time.** ✅ Primordial temporality is **ecstatic, finite, unified and futural**; ordinary time (*innerzeitige Zeit*) is a **sequence of nows**, the time *in which* events occur, **infinite** in both directions and measured by clocks.
7. **The derivation, and the qualification that must accompany it.** ✅ Heidegger derives ordinary now-time **through world-time and public dating** from more primordial temporality. ⚠️ A **levelled** interpretation *can* accompany falling, **but clock-time is not thereby morally defective or practically dispensable**: "vulgar" names a levelled conceptual interpretation, not a sin. ⚠️ Scripts routinely overclaim here, and the qualification is the difference between a careful answer and a caricature.
8. **Historicality (*Geschichtlichkeit*).** ✅ Dasein **inherits** possibilities and **projects** them; history is an existential structure rather than merely an external sequence. ⚠️ This is how the finite, futural self is also a **handed-down** self, and it prevents the resoluteness doctrine from reading as pure self-creation.
9. **The incompleteness.** ⚠️ **Division III ("Time and Being")** — where the move from Dasein's temporality to the meaning of Being as such was to be made — **was never published**. ❓ Whether the subsequent **turn** (*Kehre*) toward the history of Being is a failure or a deepening is debated, and an answer on temporality should register that the argument stops short of its announced destination.
10. **Two comparative counters.** ❓ **Sartre**: temporality grounds **freedom** and the project, not merely a formal ontology — the for-itself is ecstatic because it nihilates its past, not because Being is disclosed. ❓ **Levinas**: death individualises excessively; what of the **temporality of the Other**, and of a future that comes from the Other rather than from my own end? ⚠️ One clause each is enough to convert an exposition into an assessment.

**HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification.**

> **Claim.** Heidegger relates temporality to Dasein by showing that temporality is the meaning of care, so that Dasein does not occupy time but temporalises it. **Named evidence.** *Being and Time* (1927), Division Two, grounds the three limbs of care in three ecstases — the future as coming-toward oneself, which corresponds to being ahead-of-itself and is exhibited in being-toward-death; having-been (*Gewesenheit*), the past Dasein still is, which corresponds to being already-in and is exhibited in thrownness; and the present as making-present, which corresponds to being alongside entities — and it assigns primacy to the future, since in anticipatory resoluteness Dasein runs ahead to its death and thereby gathers its existence into a whole. **Analysis.** Calling these ecstases rather than parts is decisive: Dasein stands out into all three simultaneously, so temporality is a unity rather than a sequence, and the now-series of clock measurement is reached only by way of world-time and public dating, which is why the ordinary conception is derivative rather than basic. **Qualification.** ⚠️ "Vulgar" names a levelled conceptual interpretation and not a moral defect — clock-time is neither false nor dispensable — and the argument itself stops short of its destination, since Division III, in which temporality was to yield the meaning of Being as such, was never published.

**EXAM LINK.** This session is the second half of **2019 Q3(a)** ("How does he relate temporality with Dasein?") and the closing movement of **2022 Q2(a)**. Combined with Session 14, it converts the temporality demand into a single mapping: three limbs of care, three ecstases, one priority.

**UPSC TRAPS.**

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| Drawing the ecstases as three segments of a line | ✅ They are **ecstases** — Dasein stands out into all three **simultaneously**; temporality is a **unity**. |
| Translating *Gewesenheit* as "the past" | ✅ **Having-been** — the past Dasein **still is** and carries as possibility. |
| Saying clock-time is false or inauthentic | ⚠️ It is **derived** and **levelled**, not defective; it is practically indispensable. |
| Omitting the priority of the future | ✅ Past and present are **temporalised out of** the future; authenticity is **futural**. |
| Forgetting the mapping onto care | ✅ **Ahead-of-itself → future; already-in → having-been; alongside → present.** |
| Presenting the temporality analysis as complete | ⚠️ **Division III was never published**; the turn followed. |

**REVISION NOTES.**

- **Temporality is the meaning of care** — Division Two's central thesis.
- **Future** (projection, being-toward-death) ↔ **ahead-of-itself**; **having-been** (thrownness, guilt) ↔ **already-in**; **present** (making-present) ↔ **alongside**.
- **Priority of the future**; authenticity is **futural**; anticipatory resoluteness yields existence **as a whole**.
- **Ecstasis** = standing out; the three are **simultaneous**, a **unity**, and **finite** (bounded by death).
- **Ordinary time** = infinite **now-series**, clock-measured, **derived** through **world-time and public dating**.
- Qualification: **"vulgar" = levelled interpretation, not a sin**; clock-time is indispensable.
- **Historicality**: Dasein **inherits** and **projects** possibilities.
- **Division III never published**; the **turn** (*Kehre*) followed; Sartre and Levinas supply comparative counters.

**CLOSING RECALL FLOW.**

```text
   CARE HAS A MEANING, AND ITS MEANING IS TEMPORALITY -> AHEAD-OF-
   ITSELF = FUTURE | ALREADY-IN = HAVING-BEEN | ALONGSIDE = PRESENT ->
   THE FUTURE HAS PRIORITY: past and present are temporalised OUT OF it
   -> ANTICIPATORY RESOLUTENESS runs ahead to death and gathers
   existence AS A WHOLE -> ECSTASES, not segments: SIMULTANEOUS, UNIFIED,
   FINITE -> CLOCK-TIME is the NOW-SERIES, DERIVED via world-time and
   public dating - LEVELLED, NOT FALSE -> HISTORICALITY: possibilities
   are INHERITED and PROJECTED -> and DIVISION III WAS NEVER PUBLISHED
```

### SESSION 17 — FEAR AGAINST ANXIETY, DREAD AND ANGUISH: THE DISTINCTION THE WHOLE CLAUSE TURNS ON

**DEFINITION.** *Plain language:* fear is always fear *of* something in the world; anxiety is not — and because it has nothing to be about, what it reveals cannot be a thing but must be the shape of existence itself. *Technical:* **fear** (*Frygt* / *Furcht* / *peur*) is directed at a **determinate entity within the world**; **anxiety, dread or anguish** (*Angest* / *Angst* / *angoisse*) has **no determinate object** and therefore discloses a **structure** — possibility and freedom (Kierkegaard), the collapse of world-significance and being-in-the-world as such (Heidegger), or one's own freedom (Sartre).

**ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING.** ⚠️ *"Fear and anxiety are not two intensities of one feeling but two structures: fear has an object and drives us back into the world, while anxiety has none and therefore cannot be disclosing an entity at all — which is precisely why all three thinkers make it philosophically privileged."*

**MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS.** determinate object against no object · "the object of anxiety is a nothing" · the dizziness of freedom · complete insignificance · *Grundbefindlichkeit* (fundamental attunement) · individualisation · *solus ipse* · the cliff path · the gambler · nausea (*la nausée*) · the Nothing (*das Nichts*) · disclosive mood, not infallible cognition.

**Plain-language visual — the idea before the vocabulary.**

```text
   FEAR                                   ANXIETY / DREAD / ANGUISH

   Something threatens me.                Nothing in particular is
   I can name it: the dog, the            wrong.  And yet the whole
   exam, the fall.                        thing - being here at all -
        |                                 has become a question.
        v                                          |
   I FLEE TOWARD the world:                        v
   safety, the familiar, other            IT INDIVIDUALISES: the world's
   people, routine.                       significance drains away, 'what
                                          one does' stops helping, and
   DISCLOSES: a THREAT.                   I am left with my own
                                          existence on my hands.

                                          DISCLOSES: FREEDOM, FINITUDE,
                                          GROUNDLESSNESS.
```

*What this shows: the two moods move in opposite directions. Fear sends me into the world; anxiety strips the world of the significance I was hiding in.*

**🖼️ VISUAL — the comparison table, the three canonical examples, and the argument they share.**

```text
   |                  |                      | FREEDOM' (The Concept   |

                                   |
                                   v
   THE THREE CANONICAL EXAMPLES - LEARN ONE FROM EACH THINKER
   ------------------------------------------------------------------
   1 KIERKEGAARD (INNOCENCE).  The man at the edge of an abyss looks
     down and grows dizzy - but THE DIZZINESS IS AS MUCH IN THE EYE AS
     IN THE ABYSS.  In innocence there is no knowledge of good and
     evil, yet there IS anxiety, because freedom's possibility is
     already sensed.  Anxiety is the PRESUPPOSITION of the LEAP by
     which sin - and selfhood - enters.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------
   2 HEIDEGGER (BEING AND TIME, SECTION 40).  In anxiety 'THE WORLD
     HAS THE CHARACTER OF COMPLETE INSIGNIFICANCE': the totality of
     involvements - the workshop of ready-to-hand things - COLLAPSES,
     and Dasein is thrown back on its bare THAT-IT-IS.
     THE COMPRESSED FORMULA: THAT IN THE FACE OF WHICH ONE IS ANXIOUS
     AND THAT FOR WHICH ONE IS ANXIOUS ARE THE SAME - BEING-IN-THE-
     WORLD.  Anxiety therefore discloses Dasein as SOLUS IPSE and
     FREES IT FOR AUTHENTIC CHOOSING.
     In 'WHAT IS METAPHYSICS?' (1929) Heidegger adds that anxiety
     reveals DAS NICHTS - the Nothing.  'Nothing noths' / 'the nothing
     nothings'; NEVER 'nothing noughts'.  This is the source of the
     sentence CARNAP attacks - see Topic 07.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------
   3 SARTRE (BEING AND NOTHINGNESS, PART I).  TWO examples, and BOTH
     should be named.
     THE CLIFF PATH.  Walking a narrow path above a precipice I feel
       FEAR of the drop - the loose stone, the gust of wind, the
       world's threat.  Then I realise that NOTHING PREVENTS ME FROM
       THROWING MYSELF OVER: no barrier within me, no fixed nature.
       That vertigo is ANGUISH - not fear of the abyss but anguish
       before MY OWN FREEDOM.
     THE GAMBLER.  A man resolved firmly, YESTERDAY, never to gamble
       again.  Standing at the table today he discovers that
       yesterday's resolution is THERE but POWERLESS - it must be
       REMADE NOW, and nothing guarantees he will remake it.  Anguish
       is this discovery that MY PAST SELF CANNOT BIND MY PRESENT
       SELF.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------
   THE ARGUMENT IMPLICIT IN ALL THREE
   1 Moods (Stimmungen, Befindlichkeit) are not private colourings
     added to a neutral world; they are ways in which one's situation
     is DISCLOSED.
   2 FEAR discloses an entity as threatening, and so KEEPS US WITHIN
     the world of concern.
   3 ANXIETY has NO entity as its object; therefore it CANNOT disclose
     an entity.
   4 YET it plainly discloses SOMETHING, since it has definite content
     and is not mere confusion.
   5 THEREFORE what it discloses is not an ENTITY but a STRUCTURE - my
     FREEDOM (Kierkegaard, Sartre) or BEING-IN-THE-WORLD AS SUCH
     (Heidegger).
   6 THEREFORE anxiety is philosophically PRIVILEGED: it is the one
     mood in which existence AS A WHOLE becomes an issue for the
     existing individual.
```

*Caption: the six-step argument at the bottom is what makes this a philosophical claim rather than a report about feelings. Steps 3 and 4 together are the pressure point: an objectless state that nevertheless has content must be disclosing something other than an object.*

**NUMBERED TEACHING.**

1. **This is the most frequently botched distinction in the clause.** ⚠️ Fear and anxiety are **not degrees of the same thing**; they differ in *what they are about*, and it is that difference which makes anxiety disclosive.
2. **Fear.** ✅ Directed at a **determinate entity within the world**; it discloses a threat, and it moves me **toward** the world, seeking shelter in what is familiar.
3. **Anxiety, in three voices.** ✅ **Kierkegaard:** the object of anxiety "is a **nothing**"; what it is about is **possibility**, "the possibility of being able". **Heidegger:** that in the face of which one is anxious "is completely indefinite"; what it is about is **being-in-the-world as such**. **Sartre:** it is **my own freedom**. ⚠️ Three related but **non-identical** analyses; presenting them as one mood theory is a trap.
4. **The Kierkegaardian formula and example.** ✅ "**Anxiety is the dizziness of freedom**" (*The Concept of Anxiety*, **1844**, Vigilius Haufniensis), and "a **sympathetic antipathy and an antipathetic sympathy**" — we are drawn to what we shrink from. The man at the abyss grows dizzy, and **the dizziness is as much in the eye as in the abyss**; anxiety is already present **in innocence** and is the **presupposition** of the leap by which sin and selfhood enter.
5. **The Heideggerian analysis.** ✅ In *Being and Time* **§40**, in anxiety "**the world has the character of complete insignificance**": the totality of involvements — the workshop of ready-to-hand things — collapses, and Dasein is thrown back on its bare **that-it-is**. The compressed formula: **that in the face of which one is anxious and that for which one is anxious are the same — being-in-the-world**. Anxiety discloses Dasein as *solus ipse* and **frees it for authentic choosing**.
6. **The 1929 addition, and its cross-link.** ⚠️ In "**What is Metaphysics?**" (**1929**) Heidegger adds that anxiety reveals **the Nothing** (*das Nichts*). ✅ Correct renderings: "**Nothing noths**" / "the nothing nothings". ❌ "**Nothing noughts**" is wrong and must not be used. This is the source of the sentence **Carnap** attacks in 1932 — one clause of cross-link to **Topic 07**, and no more.
7. **The two Sartrean examples, both named.** ✅ **The cliff path**: walking a narrow path above a precipice I feel *fear* of the drop, and then realise that **nothing prevents me from throwing myself over** — no barrier within me, no fixed nature — and that vertigo is **anguish** before my own freedom. **The gambler**: a man who resolved firmly yesterday never to gamble again discovers at the table that yesterday's resolution is *there* but **powerless**; it must be **remade now**, and nothing guarantees that he will remake it. Anguish is the discovery that **my past self cannot bind my present self**. ⚠️ The gambler is the example most scripts omit, and it is the one that connects anguish directly to the non-self-identity of the for-itself (Session 7).
8. **Nausea, briefly.** ⚠️ Sartre's *la nausée* is the affective disclosure of the **contingency** of the in-itself — brute, groundless, *de trop* — and is distinct from anguish, which discloses freedom. Naming both, and distinguishing them in a clause, is a precision mark.
9. **The presuppositions of the whole analysis.** ⚠️ (P1) moods are **cognitively disclosive**, not merely affective — deny this and anxiety is only a symptom; (P2) there is a difference **in kind**, not degree, between an indefinite and a definite intentional object; (P3) what is disclosed in a rare mood is not thereby less real than what is disclosed in ordinary comportment.
10. **The strongest objection and its reply.** ❓ The **naturalist** reply: anxiety is a pathological or neurochemical state with no cognitive credentials, and treating it as a source of ontological insight is a category mistake. **Reply:** the existentialist claim is not that anxious people know more, but that the **structure** of the mood — having no object — is itself the datum requiring explanation, and no account of it in terms of an unnoticed object has succeeded. ⚠️ The honest verdict: a mood can disclose a structure without becoming infallible cognition, and naturalistic and clinical explanations remain compatible competitors rather than refuted rivals.

**HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification.**

> **Claim.** Fear and anxiety differ in kind rather than in degree, and it is the objectlessness of anxiety that gives it its philosophical role in all three thinkers. **Named evidence.** Vigilius Haufniensis writes in *The Concept of Anxiety* (1844) that the object of anxiety is a nothing and calls anxiety the dizziness of freedom, illustrated by the man at the abyss in whom the dizziness lies as much in the eye as in the depth; Heidegger states at *Being and Time* §40 that in anxiety the world has the character of complete insignificance and that that in the face of which and that for which one is anxious are the same, namely being-in-the-world; and Sartre, in Part I of *Being and Nothingness* (1943), gives the cliff path, where the vertigo is not fear of the drop but anguish before the absence of any barrier within me, and the gambler, whose yesterday's resolution stands powerless until it is remade. **Analysis.** Since anxiety has no entity as its object it cannot be disclosing an entity, yet it plainly has content and is not confusion, so what it discloses must be a structure — possibility for Kierkegaard, being-in-the-world for Heidegger, freedom for Sartre — which is why each thinker treats it as the privileged mood in which existence as a whole becomes an issue. **Qualification.** ❓ The three analyses are related but not identical and must not be merged into one mood theory; and the naturalist objection that anxiety is a clinical state with no cognitive credentials is met, not defeated, by the reply that its objectlessness is itself the datum requiring explanation.

**EXAM LINK.** This session answers any "difference between fear and dread/anxiety" demand directly, and it supplies indispensable paragraphs to **2020 Q3(a)** and **2024 Q1(e)** (Kierkegaard), **2021 Q4(b)** and **2019 Q4(c)** (Sartrean anguish), and **2019 Q3(a)** and **2022 Q2(a)** (Heideggerian anxiety as the mood that individualises and frees Dasein for authentic choosing).

**UPSC TRAPS.**

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| Defining anxiety as intense fear | ✅ The difference is **in kind**: fear has a **determinate object**, anxiety has **none**. |
| Merging the three thinkers' analyses | ⚠️ Possibility (K) · being-in-the-world (H) · freedom (S) — three **related but non-identical** accounts. |
| Writing "nothing noughts" | ❌ Wrong. "**Nothing noths**" / "the nothing nothings", *What is Metaphysics?*, **1929**. |
| Giving only the cliff path for Sartre | ✅ **Both** examples; the **gambler** shows that the past self cannot bind the present self. |
| Confusing anguish with nausea | ⚠️ Anguish discloses **freedom**; nausea discloses the **contingency** of the in-itself. |
| Treating the disclosure as infallible | ❓ A mood can disclose a structure without being infallible cognition; naturalistic explanations remain competitors. |

**REVISION NOTES.**

- **Fear**: determinate object; flees **toward** the world; discloses a **threat**.
- **Anxiety**: **no object**; **individualises**; discloses **freedom, finitude, groundlessness**; a **privileged mood**.
- **K**: "object is a **nothing**"; "**dizziness of freedom**"; abyss — dizziness in the **eye**; present in **innocence**; presupposition of the **leap**.
- **H (§40)**: "**complete insignificance**"; in-the-face-of-which = for-which = **being-in-the-world**; *solus ipse*; frees for authentic choosing; **1929** the **Nothing**.
- **S**: **cliff path** (nothing prevents me) and **gambler** (yesterday's resolution is powerless); anguish ≠ **nausea**.
- Six-step argument: moods disclose → fear discloses an entity → anxiety has none → yet has content → so it discloses a **structure** → hence privileged.
- Presuppositions P1–P3; **naturalist objection** met but not defeated.

**CLOSING RECALL FLOW.**

```text
   FEAR HAS AN OBJECT -> ANXIETY HAS NONE -> so it cannot disclose an
   ENTITY -> yet it has CONTENT -> therefore it discloses a STRUCTURE ->
   K: POSSIBILITY, 'the dizziness of freedom', already in INNOCENCE ->
   H: BEING-IN-THE-WORLD, 'complete insignificance' (Section 40),
   solus ipse, and the NOTHING (1929) -> S: MY FREEDOM - CLIFF PATH and
   GAMBLER -> anxiety is the PRIVILEGED mood -> but disclosure is not
   INFALLIBLE, and the naturalist competitor is not refuted
```

---

### SESSION 18 — CRITICISM, COMPARISON AND ANSWER CRAFT: THE DEBATES, THE DIRECTIVE DECODER AND THE VERDICT BANK

**DEFINITION.** *Plain language:* this session is where the three projects are set against each other and against their critics, and where the machinery for actually writing the answer — what each command word obliges you to do, and what a defensible closing line looks like — is assembled. *Technical:* the clause requires **thinker-specific** criticism and reply, because movement-level objections (subjectivism, pessimism, social abstraction) have **three different answers** depending on whose project is being attacked; and it requires **directive fidelity**, because the paper's command words — comment, discuss, critically examine, explain the difference — each impose a distinct structural obligation.

**ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING.** ⚠️ *"Objections to 'existentialism' as a movement rarely survive contact with its members: the charge of subjectivism means something different when levelled at Kierkegaard's appropriation, at Sartre's choice and at Heidegger's ontology — and an answer that replies once has replied to nobody."*

**MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS.** thinker-specific reply · subjectivism · pessimism · social abstraction · decisionism · formal emptiness · fideism · elitism · situated freedom · practico-inert · natality against mortality · directive fidelity · graded verdict.

**Plain-language visual — the idea before the vocabulary.**

```text
   ONE OBJECTION, THREE DIFFERENT ANSWERS

   'EXISTENTIALISM IS SUBJECTIVIST'

   -> to KIERKEGAARD:  no - subjectivity names the MODE OF
                       APPROPRIATION, and he also writes
                       'subjectivity is untruth'
   -> to SARTRE:       no - choice is tied to RESPONSIBILITY and to
                       'in choosing myself I choose man'
   -> to HEIDEGGER:    no - he is doing ONTOLOGY; das Man is criticised
                       precisely for validating received opinion

   REPLY ONCE AND YOU HAVE REPLIED TO NOBODY.
```

*What this shows: the movement-level objection is a shape, not an argument. Filling in the shape requires knowing which project is being attacked.*

**🖼️ VISUAL — the criticism ledger, the debate grid, and the directive decoder.**

```text
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   |   CRITICISMS OF SARTRE                                             |
   |   1 RADICAL FREEDOM IS PSYCHOLOGICALLY IMPLAUSIBLE - people under  |
   |     slavery, torture or mental illness are not 'absolutely free'.  |
   |     SOURCE: common sense; Marxist tradition (Merleau-Ponty,        |
   |     Adventures of the Dialectic).                                  |
   |     REPLY: freedom-of-consciousness (I can always interpret) vs    |
   |     freedom-of-action (limited by situation) - BUT THIS WEAKENS    |
   |     THE RADICAL THESIS.  In the Critique of Dialectical Reason     |
   |     (1960) Sartre HIMSELF integrates scarcity, seriality and the   |
   |     practico-inert.                                                |
   |   2 BAD FAITH PRESUPPOSES WHAT IT DENIES - to deceive myself I     |
   |     must both know and not know; but Sartre rejects the Freudian   |
   |     unconscious.  SOURCE: Fingarette, Self-Deception.              |
   |     REPLY: pre-reflective, NON-THETIC awareness in a non-self-     |
   |     identical consciousness.  COHERENCE REMAINS DEBATED.           |
   |   3 CHOICE WITHOUT CRITERIA IS BLIND - 'existentialist             |
   |     DECISIONISM'.  SOURCE: Iris Murdoch, The Sovereignty of Good;  |
   |     Marxist critics.  REPLY: engagement generates its own          |
   |     criteria - critics find this CIRCULAR.                         |
   |   4 UNIVERSALISABILITY sits uneasily with the denial of objective  |
   |     values.  SOURCE: Kantians, communitarians.  REPLY: Sartre      |
   |     concedes tension; Notebooks for an Ethics never completed.     |
   |   5 UNDER-DESCRIBES GENDERED, RACIALISED AND MATERIAL CONSTRAINT.  |
   |     SOURCE: de Beauvoir, Fanon, feminist and postcolonial          |
   |     criticism.  REPLY: situated freedom appropriates facticity     |
   |     without being reducible to it; later Sartre broadens social    |
   |     mediation - but the early vocabulary remains abstract.         |
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   |   CRITICISMS OF HEIDEGGER                                          |
   |   1 OBSCURITY - neologisms obstruct rather than clarify.  SOURCE:  |
   |     Carnap (1932), Ayer, Russell, the analytic tradition.          |
   |     REPLY: ordinary language is saturated with the metaphysical    |
   |     tradition, so new terms are needed to break through to the     |
   |     phenomena.  WHETHER THIS JUSTIFIES THE OPACITY IS CONTESTED.   |
   |   2 THE PROJECT WAS NEVER COMPLETED - Division III (Time and       |
   |     Being) was never published.  SOURCE: internal.  REPLY:         |
   |     Heidegger acknowledged this and underwent the TURN (Kehre).    |
   |     Failure or deepening is debated.                               |
   |   3 AUTHENTICITY IS FORMALLY EMPTY - it prescribes no content; a   |
   |     Nazi could be 'authentic'.  SOURCE: political critics, given   |
   |     Heidegger's own 1933 complicity; Adorno's 'jargon of           |
   |     authenticity'.  REPLY: authenticity is deliberately non-       |
   |     prescriptive.  MANY FIND THIS ETHICALLY IRRESPONSIBLE.         |
   |   4 BEING-TOWARD-DEATH IS MORBID / EXCLUDES POSITIVE SOCIAL        |
   |     BONDS.  SOURCE: Levinas (ethics of the Other); Arendt          |
   |     (NATALITY over mortality).  REPLY: death discloses the         |
   |     finitude that makes ANY authentic engagement possible.  But    |
   |     Levinas's alternative remains a powerful counter.              |
   |   5 ONTOLOGICAL FORMALITY PERMITS POLITICAL AMBIGUITY.  REPLY:     |
   |     fundamental ontology is not a political programme - textually  |
   |     relevant, but it does not settle whether formal authenticity   |
   |     LACKS CRITICAL NORMS.                                          |
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   |   CRITICISMS OF KIERKEGAARD                                        |
   |   1 FIDEISM / IRRATIONALISM - the leap abandons rationality.       |
   |     SOURCE: Hegel implicitly; analytic philosophy of religion.     |
   |     REPLY: reason is not WRONG but LIMITED - it cannot deliver     |
   |     what the religious individual needs.  Whether this is a        |
   |     reductio or a valid critique of rationalism DEPENDS ON ONE'S   |
   |     EPISTEMOLOGY.                                                  |
   |   2 ELITISM OF THE INDIVIDUAL - elevating the single individual    |
   |     over the community undermines ethics and social               |
   |     responsibility.  SOURCE: communitarians, Hegelians.  REPLY:    |
   |     'the crowd is untruth' because it enables ethical evasion;     |
   |     genuine responsibility requires standing alone.                |
   |   3 'TRUTH IS SUBJECTIVITY' COLLAPSES INTO RELATIVISM.  SOURCE:    |
   |     common misreading.  REPLY: WHAT is believed vs HOW it is       |
   |     believed; objective truth is retained for logic and science,   |
   |     only its SUFFICIENCY for existential truth is denied.          |
   |   4 A PESSIMISTIC OR ELITIST IDEAL OF SOLITARY EXISTENCE.          |
   |     SOURCE: secular and social critics.  REPLY: despair is a       |
   |     UNIVERSAL STRUCTURAL POSSIBILITY and responsibility is         |
   |     individual - but THE LIMITED ACCOUNT OF INSTITUTIONS AND       |
   |     COMMUNITY REMAINS A GENUINE WEAKNESS.                          |
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   |   MOVEMENT-LEVEL OBJECTIONS NEED THINKER-SPECIFIC REPLIES          |
   |   SUBJECTIVISM / IRRATIONALISM  K: appropriation, not opinion.     |
   |     S: choice is tied to RESPONSIBILITY.  H: ontology, not the     |
   |     validation of opinion.                                        |
   |   PESSIMISM  anxiety, despair, death and nothingness are           |
   |     DIAGNOSTIC / DISCLOSIVE structures - not the claim that every  |
   |     life is emotionally miserable.                                 |
   |   SOCIAL ABSTRACTION  feminist, Marxist and postcolonial critics   |
   |     RIGHTLY pressure the early focus on solitary or formally free  |
   |     agents; facticity, being-with and later situated ethics give   |
   |     PARTIAL rather than complete replies.                          |
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
```

*Caption: the ledger is arranged so that each row is usable as a single exam sentence — objection, named source, reply, residue. The last panel is the one to reach for whenever a question attacks "existentialism" rather than a named thinker.*

**🖼️ VISUAL — the directive decoder and the graded verdict bank.**

```text
   DIRECTIVE DECODER - WHAT EACH COMMAND WORD OBLIGES YOU TO DO HERE
   ------------------------------------------------------------------
   'COMMENT / ELUCIDATE THE STATEMENT'  (2021 'if I do not choose...';
     2023 'consciousness is what it is not...'; 2020 'you can always
     make something...')
     DEMANDS: locate the sentence IN ITS OWN THINKER'S SYSTEM, then
       unpack the technical terms.
     DO: identify the work, the PAIR OF TERMS it turns on (pour-soi/
       en-soi; facticity/transcendence), then two consequences, then a
       judgment.
     FATAL: treating the sentence as a general maxim about life.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------
   'DISCUSS EXISTENCE PRECEDES ESSENCE'  (2024)
     DEMANDS: reconstruct Sartre's atheistic no-blueprint argument and
       the project/facticity consequence.
     DO: no divine artisan -> no predetermined blueprint -> persons
       take up projects in situations.  THEN note Heidegger's
       rejection.
     FATAL: attributing the slogan to every existentialist.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------
   'CRITICALLY EXAMINE AUTHENTICITY'  (2019, 2022)
     DEMANDS: ASSESSMENT DOMINATES, and the NON-MORAL reading is the
       marked point.
     DO: state that Eigentlichkeit is an existential modification, not
       a virtue; then das Man, the call of conscience, being-toward-
       death; then the charge of FORMAL EMPTINESS.
     FATAL: treating authenticity as 'being true to yourself'.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------
   'DISCUSS BEING-IN-THE-WORLD'  (2022, 20m)
     DEMANDS: THE HYPHENS ARE THE DOCTRINE - show that the compound
       resists decomposition.
     DO: refuse the subject/object split FIRST, then Zuhandenheit/
       Vorhandenheit, the broken hammer, care (Sorge), and ONLY THEN
       temporality.
     FATAL: describing Dasein as 'a human being in the world'.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------
   'HOW DOES KIERKEGAARD OPPOSE HEGEL?'  (2023, 2020)
     DEMANDS: a STRUCTURAL opposition, not a list of complaints.
     DO: the existing individual vs the system; either/or vs both/and;
       the LEAP vs MEDIATION; indirect communication vs the lecture.
       Add 'a logical system is possible; an existential system is
       impossible.'
     FATAL: 'Kierkegaard disliked abstraction.'
   ------------------------------------------------------------------
   'EXPLAIN FREEDOM AND DETERMINISM IN SARTRE'  (2019)
     DEMANDS: explain MEANING-CONFERRAL together with FACTICITY.
     DO: motives do not operate as mechanical causes of a thing-like
       self; analyse situation, project and later social constraint.
     FATAL: pretending coercion, trauma or material limits are unreal.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------
   'BRING OUT THE DIFFERENCE BETWEEN FEAR AND DREAD/ANXIETY'
     DEMANDS: compare object-directed fear with existential anxiety
       WHILE PRESERVING THINKER DIFFERENCES.
     DO: Kierkegaard = possibility; Heidegger = collapse of world-
       significance; Sartre = freedom.
     FATAL: treating three vocabularies as one identical mood theory.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------
   'ASSESS / DO YOU AGREE?'
     DEMANDS: A DEFENDED RULING IN THE FIRST LINE.
     DO: rule on radical freedom, on whether authenticity has content,
       or on whether conflict is really the basic relation with others.
     FATAL: a neutral survey.
   ==================================================================
   GRADED VERDICT BANK - EXECUTABLE CLOSING POSITIONS
   ------------------------------------------------------------------
   EXISTENCE PRECEDES ESSENCE
     10m  Sartre's atheistic argument denies a predetermined human
          blueprint; it does not erase biological or social facticity.
     15m  ...It is SARTRE'S slogan, not existentialism's common creed;
          Heidegger repudiates the humanist inversion and Kierkegaard
          is theistic.
     20m  The doctrine foregrounds project and responsibility but
          leaves value-criteria and situated constraint underdeveloped.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------
   RADICAL FREEDOM
     10m  Freedom is the for-itself's non-coincidence with facticity,
          exercised only in situation.
     15m  ...Motives acquire action-guiding significance WITHIN a
          project; they are not irrelevant causes or instantly
          dismissible feelings.
     20m  Coercion, trauma and material structures pressure early
          radicalism; later scarcity and seriality broaden Sartre
          without simply retracting freedom.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------
   BAD FAITH
     10m  Bad faith evades the TENSION of facticity and transcendence
          rather than merely lying.
     15m  ...Pre-reflective, non-thetic awareness is Sartre's proposed
          answer to the self-deception paradox.
     20m  The account is influential but normatively incomplete,
          because positive authenticity remains thin and its examples
          require contextual criticism.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------
   THE LOOK / BEING-FOR-OTHERS
     10m  Shame discloses myself as object for another subject; it is
          not an empirical proof of all other minds.
     15m  ...Sartre improves on inferential models but may generalise
          conflict from selective encounters.
     20m  Love, solidarity, de Beauvoir, Fanon and later Sartre
          pressure subject/object exclusivity; these are BOUNDED
          criticisms, not replacements.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------
   ANGUISH / FEAR
     10m  Fear has a determinate threat; existential anxiety discloses
          possibility, freedom or world-collapse depending on the
          thinker.
     15m  ...Kierkegaard, Heidegger and Sartre use related but NON-
          IDENTICAL moods.
     20m  A mood can disclose a structure without becoming infallible
          cognition; naturalistic and clinical explanations remain
          compatible competitors.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------
   AUTHENTICITY (HEIDEGGER)
     10m  Eigentlichkeit is an existential modification, not a moral
          achievement; Dasein is inauthentic proximally and for the
          most part, and this is structural.
     15m  ...Anticipatory resoluteness gives Dasein its ownmost, non-
          relational, certain and indefinite possibility, and thereby
          a whole - but it prescribes NO CONTENT WHATEVER.
     20m  The formality is deliberate and it is the doctrine's deepest
          problem.  Adorno's charge of a 'jargon of authenticity', and
          the political catastrophe of Heidegger's own 1933, press the
          same question: a criterion that tells you to own your
          choices without telling you which to make can dignify any
          resolute commitment.  The defence - that fundamental
          ontology is not ethics - is textually correct and
          philosophically unsatisfying.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------
   KIERKEGAARD VS HEGEL
     10m  No completed system substitutes for the temporal choice of
          the existing individual.
     15m  ...Leap, either/or and indirect communication resist
          mediated systematic closure, but Hegel need not simply deny
          individuality.
     20m  Kierkegaard exposes first-person appropriation that a
          system-view risks losing; he does not offer a rival
          comprehensive ontology.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------
   INDIRECT COMMUNICATION
     10m  Ethical-religious appropriation cannot be handed over as
          neutral information.
     15m  ...Pseudonyms stage existence-possibilities; attribution
          must follow their voices.
     20m  Revocation makes the method deliberately self-limiting,
          though whether it clarifies or evades doctrine remains
          disputed.
```

*Caption: the decoder tells you what to do; the verdict bank tells you how to stop. Both are graded, so the same sub-topic yields a different closing sentence at 10, 15 and 20 marks — which is what "answer to the marks" actually means.*

**NUMBERED TEACHING.**

1. **Criticise thinker by thinker.** ⚠️ The three criticism ledgers above are not interchangeable. An answer that attacks "existentialism" for subjectivism and then replies once has replied to nobody.
2. **The Sartre ledger, in order of force.** ❓ Psychological and social implausibility of radical freedom (strongest, and conceded in 1960) → the coherence of bad faith without an unconscious → decisionism → the universalisability tension → gendered, racialised and material constraint.
3. **The Heidegger ledger, in order of force.** ❓ Formal emptiness (strongest, sharpened by 1933 and by Adorno) → the unwritten Division III → Levinas and Arendt on death → obscurity → political ambiguity. ⚠️ Note that objection 2 is **internal**: the project's incompleteness is not a hostile claim but a fact about the book.
4. **The Kierkegaard ledger, in order of force.** ❓ Fideism → the thin account of institutions and community (a **genuine weakness**, and the honest concession) → elitism → the relativism misreading, which is a misreading rather than an objection.
5. **Movement-level objections, answered three ways.** ⚠️ **Subjectivism:** Kierkegaard's subjectivity concerns *appropriation*; Sartre links choice with *responsibility*; Heidegger is doing *ontology* rather than validating opinion. **Pessimism:** anxiety, despair, death and nothingness are **diagnostic and disclosive structures**, not the claim that every life is miserable. **Social abstraction:** feminist, Marxist and postcolonial critics **rightly** pressure the early focus on solitary or formally free agents; facticity, being-with and later situated ethics provide **partial rather than complete** replies.
6. **The debate grid is the comparison answer.** ⚠️ Eight axes — God, core term, path to authenticity, freedom, temporality, chief enemy, relation to Hegel, disclosive mood. ✅ Reproducing four or five of these rows, with one sentence each, is a complete comparative section.
7. **Directive fidelity.** ⚠️ Each command word imposes a structural obligation, listed in the decoder above. Three of the fourteen owned parts are **statement questions** (2020, 2021, 2023), and for those the first move is always to **locate the sentence in its own thinker's system**, not to comment on it as a maxim about life.
8. **The verdict bank is graded for a reason.** ⚠️ At 10 marks a verdict is a single defended clause; at 15 it adds the distinction that makes the clause non-obvious; at 20 it must name the residue and rule on it. ✅ Using the wrong grade — a 20-mark verdict on a 10-mark part — costs time that the earlier sections needed.
9. **Provenance discipline is part of answer craft, not pedantry.** ⚠️ Three languages, three translation traditions, one unfinished book, one lecture the author regretted, and one printed instruction about attribution. Naming a translation once, citing *Being and Time* by section, attributing the slogans to 1945/46 and quoting a pseudonym by name are four cheap marks available in every answer in this clause.
10. **What must never be written.** ❌ "The existentialists held that…" as though the three shared a doctrine; that authenticity means being true to oneself; that *Being and Time* is a completed system; that "leap of faith" is a phrase of Kierkegaard's; that "nothing noughts"; or that Sartre's slogan is Heidegger's.

**HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification.**

> **Claim.** Criticism of this clause must be thinker-specific, because the standard movement-level charges have three different answers depending on which project is in view. **Named evidence.** Against Sartre, Merleau-Ponty's *Adventures of the Dialectic* and the Marxist tradition press the implausibility of radical freedom, Fingarette's *Self-Deception* presses the coherence of bad faith without an unconscious, and Murdoch's *The Sovereignty of Good* presses decisionism; against Heidegger, Carnap's 1932 attack presses obscurity, Adorno's "jargon of authenticity" and Heidegger's 1933 complicity press formal emptiness, and Levinas and Arendt press the primacy of the Other and of natality over mortality; against Kierkegaard, the fideism charge is pressed by analytic philosophy of religion and the elitism charge by communitarians and Hegelians. **Analysis.** Each reply belongs to a different structure — Sartre answers by distinguishing freedom of consciousness from freedom of action and then, in the *Critique of Dialectical Reason* (1960), by conceding scarcity and seriality; Heidegger answers that fundamental ontology is not ethics; Kierkegaard answers that reason is limited rather than wrong and that the crowd enables ethical evasion — so a single reply to "existentialism" would have to be true of all three at once, which none is. **Qualification.** ❓ Two residues should be conceded rather than argued away: Heidegger's formality genuinely lacks critical norms, and Kierkegaard's account of institutions and community is thin; in both cases the defence is textually correct and philosophically unsatisfying.

**EXAM LINK.** This session supplies the assessment third of every twenty-mark part in the clause — **2018 Q4(a)**, **2019 Q3(a)**, **2020 Q3(a)**, **2020 Q4(a)**, **2022 Q2(a)**, **2022 Q4(a)** and **2024 Q4(a)** — and the closing verdict for every ten- and fifteen-mark part.

**UPSC TRAPS.**

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| One reply to a movement-level objection | ⚠️ Three thinkers, three replies. Subjectivism means something different in each. |
| Treating pessimism as a fair description | ✅ Anxiety, despair and death are **disclosive structures**, not emotional verdicts on life. |
| Dismissing the social-abstraction objection | ❓ It is **rightly** pressed; the replies are **partial**. Concede the residue. |
| Neutral survey when the directive says "critically" | ⚠️ Assessment must **dominate**, and the ruling belongs in the first line. |
| Using a 20-mark verdict on a 10-mark part | ⚠️ Verdicts are **graded**; the wrong grade costs the marks the earlier sections needed. |
| Skipping provenance | ⚠️ Section citation, translation naming, 1945/46 attribution and pseudonym citation are four cheap marks. |

**REVISION NOTES.**

- **Sartre ledger**: implausibility (conceded 1960) · bad-faith coherence (Fingarette) · **decisionism** (Murdoch) · universalisability tension · constraint (de Beauvoir, Fanon).
- **Heidegger ledger**: **formal emptiness** (1933, Adorno) · unfinished Division III · Levinas/Arendt · obscurity (Carnap) · political ambiguity.
- **Kierkegaard ledger**: **fideism** · thin institutions/community (**genuine weakness**) · elitism · relativism (a misreading).
- Movement-level: **subjectivism** (three replies) · **pessimism** (disclosive structures) · **social abstraction** (partial replies).
- Debate grid: **God · core term · path · freedom · temporality · enemy · Hegel · mood**.
- Directive decoder: statement questions → **locate in the thinker's system first**; "critically" → assessment dominates.
- Verdicts are **graded 10/15/20**; provenance discipline is **four cheap marks**.

**CLOSING RECALL FLOW.**

```text
   NAME THE THINKER BEFORE THE OBJECTION -> SARTRE: implausibility ->
   bad-faith coherence -> decisionism -> universalisability -> constraint
   | HEIDEGGER: formal emptiness -> unfinished -> Levinas/Arendt ->
   obscurity | KIERKEGAARD: fideism -> thin community -> elitism ->
   relativism misread -> MOVEMENT-LEVEL CHARGES GET THREE REPLIES ->
   COMPARE ON EIGHT AXES -> OBEY THE DIRECTIVE -> CLOSE WITH THE VERDICT
   AT THE RIGHT GRADE -> AND SPEND FOUR CHEAP MARKS ON PROVENANCE
```

## BASIC MCQS / REMEDIATION

### 32 original MCQs — 24 core diagnostics and 8 remedial error-targeting drills

**Answer placement:** each correct option was fixed **independently**, so the key sequence is unpatterned rather than rotated, and no cycle such as `ABCD` can be used to guess. Options are matched for length, specificity, grammar and visible formatting so that the answer cannot be spotted by shape alone. Every question carries four question-specific option explanations and one unique examiner trap.

**Coverage:** the twenty-four core questions run across the whole printed clause — *Existence and Essence; Choice, Responsibility and Authentic Existence; Being-in-the-world and Temporality* — together with the family-label warning and the ownership of the slogan, "truth is subjectivity" and its dialectical complement, the structural form of the anti-Hegelian argument, the three spheres with their characteristic collapses, the leap against mediation, the teleological suspension staged by Johannes de Silentio, anxiety as objectless and despair as a misrelation, indirect communication and the printed attribution rule, the paper-knife argument and the exact place of atheism in it, Heidegger's repudiation and *Being and Time* §9, the in-itself/for-itself distinction and both halves of the non-self-identity formula, nihilation and the impossible synthesis, non-choice as choice, the three moods of the 1945 lecture, the facticity/transcendence structure of bad faith, the self-deception paradox and its pre-reflective solution, motive-constitution against determinism, the Look and shame as recognition, and the two failing strategies that yield conflict. The eight remedial drills attack the confusions that cost marks every year: the provenance of the slogans, the unfinished state of *Being and Time*, the correct rendering of *das Nichts nichtet*, the Lowrie/Hong split over *Angest*, the non-moral status of authenticity, the corrected reading of "hell is other people", the derived rather than defective status of clock-time, and the ownership fence around Topics 02, 05, 07 and 09, Nietzsche and Paper II.

#### MCQ 1. What kind of thing "existentialism" is

Which statement most accurately describes the standing of the term "existentialism" in relation to the three thinkers printed in the syllabus?

A. It is a retrospective family label that only Sartre accepted and defined for himself.

B. It is a school founded by Kierkegaard, systematised by Heidegger and popularised in France by Sartre after the Second World War.

C. It is a method of philosophical analysis that all three thinkers applied to different subject-matters while sharing its procedural rules.

D. It is a doctrine whose founding thesis is that human beings have no nature, a thesis each of the three defended in his own vocabulary.

**Answer: A.**

**Option explanations:**
- **A:** Correct: Kierkegaard died in 1855 and never met the word; Heidegger explicitly denied the label in the "Letter on Humanism"; only Sartre accepted it and, in the 1945 lecture published in 1946, defined it.
- **B:** Incorrect: it invents a lineage. Kierkegaard founded nothing called existentialism, and Heidegger's fundamental ontology is not a systematisation of Kierkegaard's religious writings.
- **C:** Incorrect: there is no shared procedure — Kierkegaard writes indirectly through pseudonyms, Heidegger conducts an existential analytic, Sartre a phenomenological ontology.
- **D:** Incorrect: the "no nature" thesis in that form is Sartre's, and Heidegger repudiated precisely the humanistic reading it presupposes.

**Examiner trap 1:** Writing "the existentialists held that…" assumes a shared doctrine, and the assumption is refuted by two of the three printed thinkers, one of whom denied the label in print and one of whom died before it existed.

#### MCQ 2. Which printed limb belongs almost entirely to one thinker

The syllabus prints three limbs: *Existence and Essence*; *Choice, Responsibility and Authentic Existence*; *Being-in-the-world and Temporality*. Which claim about their distribution is correct?

A. The first limb is Heidegger's, since only he analyses the essence of Dasein as lying in its existence.

B. The second limb is Kierkegaard's, since only he treats choice as a qualitative leap rather than as deliberation.

C. The third limb is almost entirely Heidegger's, with Sartre entering only as a contrast.

D. All three limbs are shared equally, which is why the syllabus names three thinkers rather than one.

**Answer: C.**

**Option explanations:**
- **A:** Incorrect: the first limb turns on Sartre's slogan and on Heidegger's repudiation of it, so it cannot be assigned to Heidegger alone.
- **B:** Incorrect: choice, responsibility and authenticity are handled by all three in incompatible registers — faith, freedom and finitude.
- **C:** Correct: being-in-the-world and temporality are Heideggerian technical structures; Sartre's ecstatic temporality is read off freedom, not off Being, and Kierkegaard's "moment" is a different concept entirely.
- **D:** Incorrect: the asymmetry is exactly why 2019 Q3(a) and 2022 Q2(a) are Heidegger questions while 2024 Q4(a) is a comparison question.

**Examiner trap 2:** Distributing the three limbs evenly across the three thinkers produces an answer that spends a third of a Heidegger question on Kierkegaard, which is the commonest way of failing 2022 Q2(a).

#### MCQ 3. What "truth is subjectivity" asserts

The thesis that truth is subjectivity is argued in the *Concluding Unscientific Postscript* (1846). What does it assert?

A. That no proposition is true independently of someone's believing it, so that objective truth is an illusion produced by grammar.

B. That for ethical and religious truth the mode of appropriation is decisive, without denying objective truth in logic or history.

C. That the passion with which a belief is held is evidence for its truth, so that sincerity functions as a criterion of correctness.

D. That truth is whatever the existing individual finds it useful to affirm in the situation in which he is placed.

**Answer: B.**

**Option explanations:**
- **A:** Incorrect: Climacus denies the *sufficiency* of objective truth for existential matters, not its existence; logic and history retain their objective standing.
- **B:** Correct: the thesis concerns the *how* of appropriation — infinite passionate inwardness, commitment, risk — for truths that must be lived rather than merely known.
- **C:** Incorrect: passion is the mode of relation, not evidence; treating it as a criterion converts the thesis into the relativism it is designed to avoid.
- **D:** Incorrect: usefulness is a pragmatist criterion nowhere present in the *Postscript*, and it makes existential truth a matter of convenience.

**Examiner trap 3:** Reading the thesis as relativism makes its companion sentence "subjectivity is untruth" unintelligible, which is the internal evidence that the relativist reading cannot be right.

#### MCQ 4. The function of "subjectivity is untruth"

Why does Climacus also assert that subjectivity is untruth?

A. Because the existing subject stands in sin and finitude and therefore needs the leap.

B. Because subjective certainty is unreliable and must always be corrected by objective inquiry conducted in a community of enquirers.

C. Because the pseudonymous form requires each thesis to be balanced by its opposite in order to preserve the reader's freedom to choose.

D. Because passion distorts judgement, so that the more inwardly a belief is held the less likely it is to correspond to how things are.

**Answer: A.**

**Option explanations:**
- **A:** Correct: the existing subject is not identical with the eternal, and the recognition of that non-identity is what makes the leap necessary rather than optional.
- **B:** Incorrect: the sentence is not an epistemological caution about unreliable feelings; it is a claim about the subject's standing before God.
- **C:** Incorrect: this makes the sentence a formal device of the method rather than a substantive theological claim, which reverses the order of explanation.
- **D:** Incorrect: it converts a doctrine about sin and finitude into a psychological generalisation about bias.

**Examiner trap 4:** Quoting "truth is subjectivity" without its complement leaves the relativism charge unanswered; quoting both, and explaining why a relativist could not use the second, answers it in one sentence.

#### MCQ 5. The structure of Kierkegaard's objection to Hegel

Which formulation best captures the *structural* form of Kierkegaard's objection to Hegel's system?

A. The system is internally inconsistent, since its categories cannot be derived from one another without circularity at the decisive transitions.

B. The system is empirically false, since history does not in fact display the rational development the dialectic requires it to display.

C. No system can contain the one who builds it: a logical system is possible, an existential system is not.

D. The system is morally objectionable, since it subordinates the suffering of individuals to the achievements of the world-spirit.

**Answer: C.**

**Option explanations:**
- **A:** Incorrect: internal inconsistency is a Hegelian-scholarship dispute; Kierkegaard's point holds even if the derivations succeed.
- **B:** Incorrect: an empirical objection would be answerable by better history, whereas Kierkegaard's objection concerns what any completed system must omit.
- **C:** Correct: the systematiser must still exist, choose and die, so the existing individual is the one item the system cannot absorb without cancelling it.
- **D:** Incorrect: the moral complaint is a consequence some critics draw, not the argument Kierkegaard actually mounts.

**Examiner trap 5:** Reducing the critique to "Kierkegaard disliked abstraction" removes the argument and leaves a temperament, which is why the 2023 part asks specifically how he argues *in favour of the individual as the essence of spirit*.

#### MCQ 6. The three spheres and their limits

Which pairing of sphere and characteristic collapse is correct throughout?

A. Aesthetic — guilt; ethical — paradox; religious — boredom.

B. Aesthetic — paradox; ethical — boredom; religious — guilt.

C. Aesthetic — guilt; ethical — boredom; religious — paradox.

D. Aesthetic — boredom; ethical — guilt; religious — paradox.

**Answer: D.**

**Option explanations:**
- **A:** Incorrect: it inverts the sequence entirely; boredom belongs to a life organised around the interesting, not to faith.
- **B:** Incorrect: paradox is the structure of faith, which is the religious sphere, and it cannot characterise the aesthetic.
- **C:** Incorrect: guilt arises from the impossibility of perfectly fulfilling a universal ethical demand, so it belongs to the ethical sphere.
- **D:** Correct: nothing binds in the aesthetic, so the self fragments; the ethical demand cannot be perfectly met, so guilt arises; faith is not rational or universal, so it lives as paradox.

**Examiner trap 6:** Listing the spheres without their internal collapses makes them look like preferences; naming the collapse of each is what shows why a leap to the next becomes intelligible.

#### MCQ 7. The leap, and the phrase "leap of faith"

Which statement about the transition between spheres and the phrase "leap of faith" is correct?

A. The transition is a Hegelian mediation performed at a higher level, and "leap of faith" is Kierkegaard's own term for it in *Fear and Trembling*.

B. The transition is a gradual psychological maturation, and "leap of faith" translates the Danish *Troens Spring* exactly as Kierkegaard wrote it.

C. The transition is a qualitative leap, and "leap of faith" is the English tradition's fused phrase rather than a Kierkegaardian term.

D. The transition is a leap only from the ethical to the religious, and elsewhere the movement between spheres proceeds by ordinary deliberation.

**Answer: C.**

**Option explanations:**
- **A:** Incorrect: the leap is defined against mediation, and the fused English phrase is not Kierkegaard's.
- **B:** Incorrect: maturation would make the movement continuous, which is precisely what "qualitative" denies.
- **C:** Correct: Kierkegaard writes of *the leap* and of faith; the compound phrase belongs to the English reception, and saying so in one clause discharges the provenance caution.
- **D:** Incorrect: the spheres are qualitatively distinct throughout, and relapse from any of them is always possible.

**Examiner trap 7:** Presenting the spheres as a developmental ladder implies that one grows out of the aesthetic, whereas Kierkegaard's point is that one leaps and can always fall back.

#### MCQ 8. Who analyses Abraham, and what the analysis does

*Fear and Trembling* (1843) treats Abraham under the heading of the teleological suspension of the ethical. Which statement is correct?

A. It is written by Johannes de Silentio, who admires faith without possessing it, and it stages the paradox rather than licensing private revelation.

B. It is written by Kierkegaard under his own name, and it establishes that a direct divine command may override the moral law whenever the individual is sincere.

C. It is written by Johannes Climacus, whose humorist standpoint allows him to describe the knight of faith from within the religious sphere itself.

D. It is written by Anti-Climacus, and it shows that the ethical is permanently cancelled once the individual enters the religious sphere of existence.

**Answer: A.**

**Option explanations:**
- **A:** Correct: the pseudonym's name records his position — he can describe Abraham but not understand him — and the text presents the paradox of faith rather than a moral permission.
- **B:** Incorrect: the book is pseudonymous, and reading it as a licence for private revelation is the standard misuse Kierkegaard scholarship warns against.
- **C:** Incorrect: Climacus writes the *Fragments* and the *Postscript* and describes himself as not a Christian; he is not the author of *Fear and Trembling*.
- **D:** Incorrect: Anti-Climacus writes *The Sickness unto Death* (1849) and *Practice in Christianity* (1850), and the ethical is suspended in relation to a higher telos, not abolished.

**Examiner trap 8:** Attributing pseudonymous books to Kierkegaard ignores an instruction he printed himself in the "First and Last Declaration", where he asks that the pseudonym be quoted instead.

#### MCQ 9. What makes anxiety philosophically disclosive

On the analysis given in *The Concept of Anxiety* (1844), what distinguishes anxiety from fear?

A. Anxiety is more intense than fear, and its greater intensity overwhelms the capacity to identify what is threatening.

B. Anxiety concerns future harms while fear concerns present ones, so the two differ only in the temporal location of the object that each of them takes.

C. Anxiety has no determinate object, so what it discloses is not an entity but possibility — freedom's own "possibility of being able".

D. Anxiety is a mood while fear is an emotion, and moods differ from emotions in lacking any intentional structure whatever.

**Answer: C.**

**Option explanations:**
- **A:** Incorrect: intensity would make the two differ in degree, whereas the analysis requires a difference in kind.
- **B:** Incorrect: fear can be directed at future harms and remain fear; the distinguishing feature is objectlessness, not tense.
- **C:** Correct: because there is no entity for anxiety to be about, what it discloses must be a structure, and Vigilius Haufniensis names that structure the dizziness of freedom.
- **D:** Incorrect: it introduces a mood/emotion taxonomy the text does not use, and denying all intentional structure would make anxiety mere confusion.

**Examiner trap 9:** Describing anxiety as "fear without a clear cause" preserves the object and merely makes it obscure, which loses the argument that an objectless state must be disclosing something other than an object.

#### MCQ 10. Despair, as *The Sickness unto Death* defines it

What is despair, on the analysis given by Anti-Climacus in 1849?

A. A prolonged emotional condition of hopelessness that follows repeated failure to realise the projects one has chosen.

B. A misrelation in the relation the self is — the self failing to be itself before the power that established it.

C. The recognition that the ethical demand cannot be fulfilled, which drives the individual out of the ethical sphere.

D. The mood in which the possibility of one's own death first becomes an issue for the existing individual as such.

**Answer: B.**

**Option explanations:**
- **A:** Incorrect: despair on this account may be entirely unfelt, so it cannot be identified with an emotional condition.
- **B:** Correct: the self is defined as a relation that relates itself to itself and thereby to the power that established it, and despair is a fault within that relation.
- **C:** Incorrect: that describes the collapse of the ethical sphere into guilt, which is a different analysis in a different pseudonymous work.
- **D:** Incorrect: this conflates despair with anxiety, and imports a Heideggerian concern that Kierkegaard's text does not carry.

**Examiner trap 10:** Treating despair as sadness makes the claim that despair is universal look like an empirical exaggeration, when it is a structural claim about the self's relation to its ground.

#### MCQ 11. Why the doctrine forces the method

Consider the following claims about indirect communication.

1. It is required because ethical and religious truth is a mode of existing rather than a result.
2. It is required because Kierkegaard wished to protect himself from censorship and from the Danish church.
3. Direct communication of such truth would falsify it, since the recipient could then hold it without becoming it.
4. The model is Socratic midwifery, in which the author becomes a vanishing occasion rather than an authority.

Which of the above are correct?

A. 1 and 2 only.

B. 2 and 3 only.

C. 1, 2 and 4 only.

D. 1, 3 and 4 only.

**Answer: D.**

**Option explanations:**
- **A:** Incorrect: it includes the biographical motive, which is not the argument, and omits the two steps that make the argument work.
- **B:** Incorrect: it retains the biographical motive and drops the premise about what kind of truth is at stake.
- **C:** Incorrect: 4 is correct but 2 is not, and dropping 3 removes the step in which the falsification actually occurs.
- **D:** Correct: the three steps form the argument — the kind of truth, the falsification that direct transmission would cause, and the Socratic form that avoids it.

**Examiner trap 11:** Explaining pseudonymity by the author's temperament or circumstances converts an argument into a biography, and loses the point that the form is deduced from the doctrine.

#### MCQ 12. The "First and Last Declaration"

What does Kierkegaard do in the "First and Last Declaration" appended to the *Postscript* (1846)?

A. He renounces the pseudonymous authorship entirely and asks readers to disregard the books published under invented names.

B. He acknowledges the pseudonymous books while calling himself their "author's author" and asks that citations name the pseudonym.

C. He explains that the pseudonyms were adopted to avoid prosecution, and announces that he will henceforth publish only under his own name.

D. He identifies each pseudonym with a stage of his own biography, so that the works may be read as a spiritual autobiography.

**Answer: B.**

**Option explanations:**
- **A:** Incorrect: he does not renounce them; he acknowledges authorship while refusing to be quoted as their speaker.
- **B:** Correct: he describes himself as only the "author's author", a prompter (*souffleur*) who produced characters, and asks that the pseudonym's name be quoted instead of his own.
- **C:** Incorrect: no such legal motive is stated, and the pseudonymous works continue after 1846 with Anti-Climacus in 1849 and 1850.
